# Mehrgo Driver — Android → iOS Parity Implementation Plan

**Date:** 2026-07-20
**Android reference build:** `uz.teamwork.mehrgodriver` **2.1.0 (61)**, branch `mehrgo_driver_app`
**iOS target:** `mehrgo-driver-ios-main` — `project.yml` declares `MARKETING_VERSION 1.5.0` / `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION 30`, while `README.md` claims feature parity with Android **v1.9.1 (build 47)**.

> **Note on that discrepancy:** the two iOS version sources disagree with each other, and both trail the current Android build. Reconcile `project.yml` against the README before planning a release. This document assumes the *code* is the truth, not either version string — every claim below was checked against actual Swift source.

---

## 1. How to read this document

This is a **gap report**, not a rewrite order. The iOS app is well-built and in several places is *ahead* of Android. Only work that Android has and iOS does **not** (or does in a way that is demonstrably wrong) is listed.

Every gap has six fields:

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **What to build** | Concrete implementation instructions — field names, formulas, thresholds, state transitions |
| **Android reference** | `file:line` in the Android repo that defines the behavior — the source of truth |
| **iOS target** | The exact Swift file(s) to modify or create |
| **Gotchas** | Traps Android already paid for. Read these; they are the expensive part |
| **Acceptance** | How to verify on a device |
| **Priority** | `P0` money / crash / data-loss · `P1` core UX or protocol parity · `P2` polish |

**Priorities are about risk, not effort.** A `P2` may take a week; a `P0` may take an hour.

### Method (why you can trust these findings)

Thirteen feature areas were analysed by independent agents that read the Android source, the Android engineering postmortems, **and** the iOS source. Each area's findings then went through a second, **adversarial** pass whose only job was to *refute* each claimed gap by finding the iOS code that already implements it — searching the whole repo across Swift naming conventions, not just the originally named file. Refuted claims were deleted.

**What this means in practice:** a gap surviving here is one that a reviewer actively tried and failed to disprove. What it does **not** mean is that the plan is infallible — it is a static reading of both codebases. Anything marked *MUST-TEST* in a gotcha is an assumption that needs a device before you build on it.

---

## 2. Executive summary

**121 verified gaps** across **27 areas**: **26 P0**, **41 P1**, **54 P2**.

Delivered in three passes, each with a different question:

| Pass | Areas | Question it asked | Yield |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** (§8) | 1–13 | What deep systems is iOS missing? | 72 gaps, 18 P0 |
| **2** (§9) | 15–21 | What screen-level surfaces did pass 1 never look at? | 19 gaps, 3 P0 |
| **§10** | 22 | (coverage audit finding) | 2 gaps |
| **3** (§11) | 23–28 | Of the files that DO exist on iOS, which ones *diverge*? | 28 gaps, 5 P0 |

**Pass 3 was the highest-yield of the three** — read §11 first if you read nothing else. Every file it examined already existed on iOS, so nothing looked wrong from the outside; the divergences only appear when you put the two implementations side by side and do the arithmetic.

Six findings deserve to be read before any planning happens:

1. **The live-fare protocol (§7) is the single biggest hole, and it is a money hole.** iOS has an excellent GPS-batch pipeline — idempotent outbox, accuracy gating, gap-filling cadence — but only the *legacy* half of the fare contract. It never sends the cumulative wait timers, never parses the `data.live` block, and settles the trip client-side (`serverGross: nil`). On Android this exact combination produced wrong money on real trips. **Areas 1, 2 and 4 independently discovered the same missing batch-timer work** — three separate analyses converging is strong evidence it is real.

2. **Paylov (cards + withdrawal) does not exist on iOS at all.** Five P0s in area 6 are one absent feature, not five bugs. Scope it as a feature project, not a patch.

3. **Two decode gaps can hard-block the app.** A single unexpected type on `user/me` blocks the splash permanently (area 11), and numeric fields that arrive as strings silently change the computed fare. Android's equivalent bug killed the app with a `FATAL` when `waiting_cost` came back as `2613.33` into a `Long`. iOS is *partly* protected — `FareSummary` already routes through `FlexDecode` — but the protection is not applied uniformly.

4. **Two iOS-only defects surfaced that Android does not have.** Comparing codebases finds bugs in both directions: tapping a row in `MyOrdersView` spins up a **second `TripMVVM`** for an order the trip panel already owns (duplicate fare ticker, duplicate Live Activity, conflicting `LocationTracker` state), and there is **no permission-regression gate** — a driver who revokes location after first run stays "online" with no GPS. Neither is a port; both are fixes.

**Where iOS is ahead of Android** (do not "fix" these):
- A **Live Activity / `WaitWidget`** for the waiting timer — Android has no equivalent.
- **Lenient numeric decoding** (`FlexDecode`) in the fare models, which is exactly the defence Android had to add after a production crash.
- A **`FareCalculator` unit-test suite** (11 cases). Android has no meaningful tests at all. **Keep this and extend it** — every P0 in area 1 and 4 should land with a test.

---

## 3. Suggested sequencing

Ordered by risk retired per unit of work. **Milestones 1–3 are the release-blocking set.**

| # | Milestone | Areas | Why this order |
|---|---|---|---|
| **M1** | **Live fare protocol §7** — batch wait timers, timer-only batches, `data.live` parsing, server-first settlement, wait split at `order/complete` | 1, 2, 4 | Money correctness. Everything else is cosmetic next to billing the rider the wrong amount |
| **M2** | **Decode hardening** — `User.init(from:)`, uniform `FlexDecode`, element-wise array decoding | 11 | Cheap, and it protects M1: a fare field arriving as a string silently corrupts the money you just fixed |
| **M3** | **Paylov cards + withdrawal** | 6 | A whole absent feature. Independent of M1/M2, so it can run in parallel with a second engineer |
| **M4** | **Session + realtime integrity** — rotated `auth_key` on resend, active-order resync on reconnect, Telegram OTP channel | 3, 5 | The `auth_key` item is broken *today*, independent of Telegram |
| **M5** | **Verification gate, nav gating, video handoff** | 7, 8, 9 | Correctness-flavoured UX. Small, well-isolated |
| **M6** | **Push routing, theming, profile/history polish** | 10, 12, 13 | Polish. Safe to defer |

**Pass-2 work slots in as follows** (§9, areas 15–21):

| # | Milestone | Areas | Why this order |
|---|---|---|---|
| **M0** | **Ship-blockers found in pass 2** — real App Store URL (force-update currently has **no Update button**), Crashlytics dSYM upload phase, permission-regression gate, the duplicate-`TripMVVM` bug | 16, 19, 20, 21 | These are hours of work each, and two of them mean a shipped build cannot be updated or debugged. Do them **before** M1 |
| **M7** | **Order-pool + My-Orders completeness** — unreachable `MyOrdersView` entry point, socket invalidation, driver-created taximeter order | 15, 16 | Real feature holes, but no money or crash risk |
| **M8** | **Remaining pass-2 polish** | 15, 17, 18, 19, 20 | Defer freely |

**Pass-3 work (§11, areas 23–28) mostly folds INTO M1, not after it.** The services double-bill, the stale-order settlement and the wait-split at `order/complete` are the *same fare path* M1 rebuilds — doing them separately means touching `FareCalculator.swift` and `TripMVVM.finish()` twice:

| # | Milestone | Areas | Why this order |
|---|---|---|---|
| **M1+** | **Fold into M1** — quoted-arm services double-bill, stale-order refresh before settle, pickup/on-route wait split at `order/complete`, departure auto-start headless path | 23, 24 | Same files, same review, same device test. Splitting them wastes a regression cycle |
| **M9** | **Registration hardening** — photo downscaling (multipart currently risks a 60 s timeout), camera capture, stop submitting fabricated field defaults | 26 | Blocks real sign-ups on a fresh phone |
| **M10** | **Gate integrity** — splash sign-out routing, nil-user gate bypass, "Later"/"Continue" skipping downstream gates, `version-driver` key mismatch | 27 | Several of these silently disable the gates that already exist |
| **M11** | **Route-server 401 isolation, driver self-cancel call site** | 25, 28 | Small, independent |

### Sequencing hazard — `TripMVVM.swift`

Counted across all 121 gaps, the work concentrates hard into a handful of files:

| File | Gaps targeting it |
|---|---|
| `TripMVVM.swift` | **12** |
| `GpsBatch.swift` | 8 |
| `LocationTracker.swift` | 7 |
| `TripView.swift` | 7 |
| `DynamicClient.swift` | 5 |
| `GpsBatchUploader.swift` | 5 |
| `SharedModels.swift` | 5 |
| `PaylovModels.swift` | 4 |

The top four are all in M1's blast radius, and the pass-3 receipt/wait P0s (§23, §24) land in the same two files as M1.

> **Land M1 (plus the M1+ pass-3 items) first, as one coherent change.** If M1 and a later milestone touch `TripMVVM.swift` and `LocationTracker.swift` concurrently on separate branches, you will spend more time resolving conflicts than writing the features. Everything else in the plan is comparatively isolated and can be parallelised across engineers.

## 4. Cross-cutting notes — build these ONCE

Because each area was analysed independently, the same underlying work surfaces in several places. **These are not separate tasks.** Build one implementation and check it off everywhere.

| Work item | Reported in | Build it in |
|---|---|---|
| Cumulative `waiting_time` / `waiting_time_ontheway` in every batch | Area 1 (P0), Area 2 (P1), Area 4 (P0) | `GpsBatchUploader.swift` + `LocationTracker.swift` — **once** |
| Parse `data.live`; do not add services on top of `live.price` | Area 1 (P0), Area 4 (P0) | `GpsBatch.swift` model + `TripMVVM.swift` consumption |
| `events` array → billing sound cue | Area 1 (P1), Area 2 (P2) | Wherever the fare ack is handled — one handler |
| Adopt the server's wait totals (reconnect / second device) | Area 1 (P1), Area 2 (P1) | `LocationTracker.swift` |
| Refresh fare / resync order on socket reconnect | Area 1 (P1), Area 3 (P0) | `WebSocketManager` reconnect callback → `TripMVVM` |

**One architectural correction that applies across M1:** install the wait-timer provider on **`LocationTracker`** (the singleton that owns the counters and survives screen changes), **not** on `TripMVVM` (per-screen, can be deallocated mid-trip). Android's equivalent lives in the always-on `MyTrackingService` for exactly this reason. Getting this wrong means the timers stop when the driver navigates away.

## 5. Conventions this plan assumes

From the iOS repo's own `README.md` — follow these, do not import Android idioms:

- `@MainActor` ViewModels exposing `@Published var state: VMState`
- `Array<T>` syntax, **never** `[T]`
- `// MARK: -` sections in the documented order
- File header: `// FileName.swift / MehrgoDriver / Created by Umar on DD/MM/YY.`
- `os_log(.level, "APP-Class: message")` for telemetry — **auth tokens are never logged**
- Deployment target **iOS 17**, Swift 5.9, XcodeGen (`xcodegen generate`, open the `.xcodeproj`, not a workspace)
- Strings: `uz` is the development language; `kk`/`ky` are currently seeded from `uz` and still need a translation pass

**Any new user-facing string must be added to every `.lproj` bundle**, matching the Android rule of translating into all four locales. ⚠️ **Today iOS ships only two** (`uz`, `ru`) — Kazakh and Kyrgyz do not exist. See §22 before writing any new strings, so new work does not deepen the hole.

---

## 6. Explicitly NOT applicable

These are real Android work items from the same period that have **no iOS counterpart**. They are listed so nobody ports them by mistake:

| Android work | Why it does not apply |
|---|---|
| Edge-to-edge insets, Android 15 opt-out, dialog inset padding | SwiftUI safe-area handling is a different model entirely |
| `Activity.recreate()` crossfade for theme/locale switching | SwiftUI re-renders in place; no Activity to recreate |
| `SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW` auto-offer overlay | iOS has no system overlay. The correct iOS mechanisms are time-sensitive/critical notifications, notification actions, and Live Activities — see area 13 |
| Foreground-service notification + `START_STICKY` restart | iOS background execution is a different contract (background modes, `BGTaskScheduler`) |
| `LocalBroadcastManager` event routing | Combine subjects already fill this role on iOS |
| Back-stack `popUpTo` on logout | SwiftUI navigation state, not a fragment back stack |
| Native-lib `dlopen` / APK packaging fixes | Android packaging only |

## 7. Open questions for the team

1. **Backend `events` semantics.** Android observed the server re-sending `waiting_charge_started` in **every** response, contrary to the spec's one-shot contract. Android works around it with a latch. Confirm whether this is being fixed server-side before iOS copies the workaround — if it is fixed, iOS needs the simpler code.
2. **iOS version reconciliation** — `project.yml` (1.5.0/30) vs `README.md` (parity with Android 1.9.1/47). Which is authoritative?
3. **YouTube playback on iOS (area 9)** is flagged *MUST-TEST*. Android proved in-app playback is impossible there, but a `WKWebView` is a different client than an Android `WebView`. Test before adopting the poster-handoff design.
4. **Paylov endpoint contract** — the Android client reaches the Paylov base with a relative-path trick that iOS's `buildURL` cannot reproduce. iOS needs a proper second `ApiBase`; confirm the canonical base URL with the backend team.

---

5. **The same trip settles to a DIFFERENT amount on the two platforms today (§23, §24).** This is the sharpest finding in the document, and it is not a missing feature — it is arithmetic:
   - **Services are double-billed on quoted trips.** iOS adds `servicesFloor` on top of `order.price`; Android's `agreedTotal` does not (`MapFragment.kt:2706`). A 50 000 booked trip with a 5 000 add-on settles at **55 000 on iOS, 50 000 on Android**. Worse, iOS's own frozen trip hero shows 50 000 all ride — so the receipt exceeds the number the driver stared at.
   - **The on-route wait slice is billed at the pickup tariff.** iOS sends the *combined* wait as `waiting_time` at `order/complete`, so waiting that happened mid-route is priced with `priceOfWaiting` instead of `priceOfWaitingOnWay`. Android splits it (this was Android task 3).
   - **Settlement reads a stale cached `Order`** — iOS never re-fetches before finishing, so an add-on applied mid-trip can be counted twice or missed entirely.

   The **empirical question these two platforms disagreed about is now SETTLED** (prod capture, order 77041, 2026-07-20): `order.price` **DOES** include the add-ons that were applied at booking time. Android's assumption is correct; the iOS comment is wrong. Evidence — tariff `starting_price` 5 000, distance 0 km, one add-on ("Ayol haydovchi", 10 000), and `order.price` = **15 000** = 5 000 + 10 000, corroborated by `breakdown.estimate` = `{price: 15000, services_price: 10000, podacha: 0, extra_price: 0}`.

   The same capture surfaced a second fact that makes the fix cleaner than this plan originally assumed: the server keeps **booking-time services and current services as separate numbers**. A second add-on ("Konditsioner", 2 000) applied *after* booking left `estimate.services_price` frozen at 10 000 while `live.services_price` moved to 12 000. So there is no need to infer how much service money is already inside `order.price` — `breakdown.estimate.services_price` states it exactly, and only the delta (`live.services_price − estimate.services_price`) is money that is not yet in `order.price`. That is the principled answer to the mid-trip-add-on double-count called out in the §16 gotcha; use those two fields rather than a heuristic.

6. **Firebase on iOS is LIVE, not disabled** — the README says the calls are commented out pending `GoogleService-Info.plist`. That is stale; pass 2 verified the wiring is active. Fix the README before it sends someone hunting for lines that do not exist. (The real analytics problem is the missing dSYM upload — §21.)

---

## 8. Detailed gap reports — pass 1 (deep systems)

Areas 1–13: the fare protocol, socket, meter, auth, payments, verification, maps, video, profile, resilience, theming, push. Pass 2 (§9) covers the screen-level surfaces.

---

### 1. Live fare protocol (server-computed fare during a trip): GPS batch upload, cumulative wait timers, `data.live` block, billing `events` + sound, socket price push, server-wait adoption

**Gaps:** 7 total — P0: 3 · P1: 3 · P2: 1

#### iOS today

iOS has a genuinely well-built GPS-batch pipeline but only the LEGACY half of the fare protocol. `MehrgoDriver/Shared/GpsBatchUploader.swift` is a @MainActor singleton with a 50-point `batchLimit`, a 20-point burst trigger plus a 15 s staleness trigger both evaluated inside the location-callback Task (`onEnqueued`, :273-289), a drain loop in `flush()` (:292-367), a 5-consecutive-403/404 tolerance escalating to `stopRequestedSubject` (:339-347, consumed at TripMVVM.swift:1026-1044 which calls `stop()` + `LocationTracker.disableBatchUpload()`), and an idempotent-cpid outbox in `Persistence/GpsBatchStore.swift`. `sendREST` (:371-383) POSTs `order-gps/batch?order_id=` with a body of `["points": ...]` and NOTHING else — I confirmed there is no second key. The ack decodes to `FareSummary` (`Shared/Models/GpsBatch.swift:62-120`), which covers ONLY the legacy top-level fields (`order_id, distance_km, in_city_km, out_city_km, waiting_sec, traffic_sec, price, fare`); grepping the whole repo for `live`, `FareLive`, `events`, `waiting_billing`, `kept_projection`, `agreed_price`, `services_price`, `podacha`, `on_way_sec` returns only prose comments and one unrelated debug literal (HomeMVVM.swift:152), so the `data.live` block and `events` array are absent, not merely renamed. iOS DOES consume the socket push (`WebSocketManager.swift:178-192` decodes `Constants.socketOrderPriceUpdated` = `order_price_updated` into `FareSummary` → `fareUpdateSubject` → TripMVVM.swift:986-1005 → `@Published serverFare`) and DOES GET `order-gps/fare` in `refreshServerFare()` (TripMVVM.swift:900-914) — call sites are init/screen-open (:168), after `order/start` (:423), after "Kettik" (:535) and after `order/complete` (:758); the function's own doc comment claims "after a socket reconnect" but NO such call site exists, so the comment is stale. Money typing is already safe: every `FareSummary` field goes through `FlexDecode.double/.int` (GpsBatch.swift:89-99) and decode failures are caught, not fatal, so the fractional-`waiting_cost` value that FATAL'd Android cannot crash iOS. `flushNow()` already exists (:206) but is called from exactly one place, `finish()` (TripMVVM.swift:626). `GpsBatchSocketEnvelope`/`GpsBatchSocketData` (GpsBatch.swift:39-53) are declared with ZERO call sites — batches only ever go over REST. Missing is the entire §7 layer: no wait timers in any batch (the only `waiting_time` send in the app is `order/complete`, TripMVVM.swift:693), no points-less batch (`flush()` returns at :312 on `points.isEmpty`), no `live`, no `events`/sound, no server-wait adoption, and no reconnect refresh.

#### Android today

Android implements the full §7 protocol and every reference cited in this report was checked against the real source. `common/fare/GpsBatchUploader.kt` exposes `waitTimersProvider: (() -> Pair<Long, Long>)?` (:130), holds `lastSentWaitMs`/`lastSentOnWayMs` declared -1L (:134-137) but reset to 0L in start (:157-158), computes deltas in `pendingTimers()` (:392-396), attaches `waiting_time`/`waiting_time_ontheway` to every batch (:362-367), and has a dedicated points-less branch at :332-359 that POSTs `points = emptyList()` plus the timers when `batch.isEmpty()` and latches `isBatchUploadEnabled = false` on TERMINAL_FAIL. Timers are marked sent only per successful transport via `markTimersSent()` (:405-415), which also logs under the `FareLive` tag. `MyTrackingService.kt` installs the provider in `onCreate` (:350-361) with the Go split `if (rideHasGone) Pair(waitedTimeUntilGone, (total - pickup).coerceAtLeast(0)) else Pair(total, 0L)`; collects `gpsBatchUploader.fareUpdates` at service level (:363-391) to beep `R.raw.audio_notification` on `waiting_charge_started` (guarded by `chargeStartedBeeped`, :189/370) and on `waiting_charge_step` (guarded by a `surcharge ?: waitingCost / 5000` bucket, :190/375-380), with both latches reset at :760-761 and :825-826 — and its comment at :183-188 records that the backend re-sends `waiting_charge_started` in EVERY response, which is why the latch is load-bearing. `adoptServerWaits(fare)` (:398 call site, :942+) raises the local wait counters to the server's MAX, with an explicit carve-out (:945-951) refusing to adopt the `on_way` component before Go to avoid double-billed waiting. `domain/model/fare/FareLive.kt:18-37` defines all 14 `live` fields with every money value as `Double` and a header note that a `Long` field made Gson throw on a live `"waiting_cost": 2613.33` and killed the app. Consumption sets `serverPriceIncludesExtras = fare.live?.price != null` in MapFragment.kt (flag declared :236, set by `applyServerFare` :242) and rebuilds the receipt services row accordingly (MapFragment.kt:2679-2691, with the no-double-add taximeter arm at :2711-2717). `GpsBatchSocketChannel.kt` provides the socket-first transport with a FIFO deferred ack queue that `uploadBatch` prefers when connected (:416-421), REST being the fallback.

<a id="gap-1-1"></a>

#### 1.1 `[P0]` Send cumulative waiting_time / waiting_time_ontheway in every GPS batch

**What to build**

VERIFIED ABSENT: `sendREST` (GpsBatchUploader.swift:371-383) builds `let body: [String: Any] = ["points": points.map(Self.encode)]` and nothing else; the only `waiting_time` the app ever sends is at `order/complete` (TripMVVM.swift:693). Add both cumulative wait timers to the batch body, in MILLISECONDS, as Int64.

1. Add `var waitTimersProvider: (() -> (pickupMs: Int64, onWayMs: Int64))?` to `GpsBatchUploader`. Android's equivalent is `waitTimersProvider: (() -> Pair<Long, Long>)?` at GpsBatchUploader.kt:130, installed in `MyTrackingService.onCreate` (kt:350-361) as `if (rideHasGone) Pair(waitedTimeUntilGone, (total - pickup).coerceAtLeast(0)) else Pair(total, 0L)`.
2. CORRECTION to the original plan: install the provider from `LocationTracker`, NOT `TripMVVM.init`. `TripMVVM` is per-screen and can be deallocated mid-trip, whereas Android's provider lives in the always-on service. `LocationTracker` is the true analogue — it is the singleton that OWNS `waitedTimeMs` and `waitedTimeUntilGoneMs` (LocationTracker.swift:43/49) and already survives the screen. Install it in `startTripTracking(orderId:branchPolygon:)`.
3. Use `LocationTracker`'s own `wayStarted` flag (private, LocationTracker.swift:85, set true in `markPassengerOnboard()` at :336) as the aboard predicate — NOT `TripMVVM.currentState == .changedGone`. `wayStarted` is already preserved across screen re-entry (see the re-entry comment at :223) and is reset in the same place the counters are (:244). You will need to expose it (e.g. `private(set) var wayStarted`).
4. Add `private var lastSentWaitMs: Int64 = 0` and `lastSentOnWayMs: Int64 = 0`, and reset them to 0 (NOT -1) inside `start(orderId:)` alongside the existing `consecutiveServerRejects = 0` at :121. Android declares them -1L (kt:134/137) but explicitly resets to 0L in start (kt:157-158) so a fresh order does not fire a pointless {0,0} batch.
5. Add `private func pendingTimers() -> (Int64?, Int64?)` returning each value only when it differs from its lastSent counterpart, nil otherwise (Android kt:392-396).
6. In `sendREST`, add `waiting_time` / `waiting_time_ontheway` to the dict ONLY when non-nil. This is natural here because the body is already an untyped `[String: Any]` and `Self.encode` (:389-402) uses the same omit-when-nil idiom for point fields. If you keep the `GpsBatchRequest` Codable struct (GpsBatch.swift:33-35) in sync, its new fields must be `Int64?` with `encodeIfPresent`.
7. Mark `lastSentWaitMs`/`lastSentOnWayMs` ONLY after a 2xx — inside the `do` block at flush():314-326, next to `GpsBatchStore.shared.ack`. Never optimistically. Android learned this the hard way: its socket path silently marked timers sent while dropping them, hence `markTimersSent()` being gated per-transport at GpsBatchUploader.kt:405-415.

Without this the server never learns the wait during the trip, `waiting_sec` in the ack stays ~0, the server-side waiting bill never accrues, no billing event can fire, and the live price shown to the driver is systematically LOW.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/fare/GpsBatchUploader.kt:130,134-137,156-158,362-367,392-396,405-415; app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/services/MyTrackingService.kt:350-361

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/GpsBatchUploader.swift (provider property, lastSent state in start(), pendingTimers(), sendREST body, mark-after-ack in flush()); MehrgoDriver/Shared/LocationTracker.swift (install the provider in startTripTracking; expose wayStarted); MehrgoDriver/Shared/Models/GpsBatch.swift (GpsBatchRequest fields if the Codable struct is kept in sync)

> **Gotchas.** Send MILLISECONDS — the server field is ms even though the ack reports `waiting_sec` in seconds; mixing them silently under-bills by 1000x. `waitedTimeUntilGoneMs` is a SUBSET of `waitedTimeMs`, not a sibling (LocationTracker.swift:44-49) — the on-way value is the SUBTRACTION `waitedTimeMs - waitedTimeUntilGoneMs`, floored at 0; sending `waitedTimeMs` as the on-way figure double-bills the whole pickup wait. Omit the keys entirely when unchanged (Gson omits nulls on Android); sending explicit `null` is a different wire shape than what the backend validators saw in testing. Do NOT install the provider from a per-screen object — the uploader outlives TripMVVM and a nil provider silently degrades to today's behaviour with no error. LocationTracker's counters are only correct after `startTripTracking` has run; the provider must read them live (closure), never capture values.

**Acceptance** — Start a trip, arrive, sit still 2 min, board, drive. Proxy the `order-gps/batch` POSTs: the first batch after any wait accrual carries `waiting_time` in ms; batches where nothing changed carry NEITHER key; after Kettik the pickup value FREEZES and `waiting_time_ontheway` starts growing. The ack's `waiting_sec` should track ~ the ms sent / 1000 instead of staying 0. Kill the app mid-wait, relaunch, confirm no {0,0} regression batch is sent and the values resume from the persisted totals.

<a id="gap-1-2"></a>

#### 1.2 `[P0]` Send timer-only (points-less) batches while the driver is stationary

**What to build**

VERIFIED ABSENT: `flush()` returns unconditionally at GpsBatchUploader.swift:312 (`if points.isEmpty { return }`). A parked driver produces no surviving GPS fixes (the client filter in `decideFilter`/`enqueue` drops them and the outbox stays empty), so the timers from item 1 would never reach the server during the exact window that matters — the waiting bill.

Mirror Android's branch (GpsBatchUploader.kt:332-359): when the store returns an empty array, call `pendingTimers()`; if EITHER timer is non-nil, POST `order-gps/batch` with `points: []` plus the timers, publish the returned `FareSummary` through the existing `fareSubject.send(FareUpdateEvent(...))` (same shape as :323-325), then return. Android also resets its flush cadence in this branch (`pendingSinceFlush.set(0)`, `lastFlushAtMs = now`); the iOS equivalent already stamps `lastFlushAt = Date()` at :298, so only `pendingSinceFlush` needs care.

CORRECTION to the original plan's failure path: iOS's point path does NOT latch on 401 — the `catch let error as ClientError` block (:327-358) only counts 403/404 and escalates via `stopRequestedSubject.send(id)` after `maxServerRejects`; every other status just logs and returns. So do NOT invent a 401 latch for the timer-only path alone. Reuse the SAME `consecutiveServerRejects` counter and the same `stopRequestedSubject` escalation, which is already wired to kill the flush timer (TripMVVM.swift:1026-1044 → `GpsBatchUploader.stop()` invalidates `flushTimer` and nils `orderId`).

CORRECTION on the nudge: `flushNow()` ALREADY EXISTS (GpsBatchUploader.swift:206-208, `Task { await self.flush() }`) and is already called from `finish()` (TripMVVM.swift:626). Do not add a duplicate `nudge()` — just call the existing `flushNow()` (a) from the 1 s `fareTimer` tick (`startFareTimer`, TripMVVM.swift:1177-1193, alongside `recompute()`/`checkFinishPromptIfDue()`) on a 5 s boundary while `LocationTracker.shared.isWaiting`, and (b) once immediately after `LocationTracker.shared.markPassengerOnboard()` (TripMVVM.swift:534) so the first post-board server fare lands in ~1 s instead of up to 15 s. `flushNow()` is already a safe no-op when a flush is in flight (`guard !isFlushing` at :293).

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/fare/GpsBatchUploader.kt:332-359 (empty-batch branch, TERMINAL_FAIL latch, cadence reset)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/GpsBatchUploader.swift (empty-points branch inside flush(), reuse flushNow()); MehrgoDriver/MVVM/TripMVVM.swift (call flushNow() from the fareTimer tick on a 5 s boundary while waiting, and once after markPassengerOnboard at :534)

> **Gotchas.** Order of work: this item is what makes item 1 actually reach the server during a wait — a 5 s nudge is worthless while `flush()` still bails at :312, so land the empty-points branch first. Guard the timer-only POST behind `pendingTimers()` returning something, or a parked driver POSTs every 15 s forever with an unchanged body. The `stopRequestedSubject` latch has a per-screen subscriber (TripMVVM.swift:1026) — if the trip screen is deallocated there is nobody to stop the uploader, so a dead order could keep POSTing timer-only batches; consider also short-circuiting inside the uploader itself on the terminal streak rather than relying solely on the subject. `fareSubject` should only be sent when the summary is meaningful (the point path already gates on `summary.price != nil || summary.distanceKm != nil` at :323) — keep that gate so an empty ack does not blank the fare card.

Confirm an `order-gps/batch` POST fires ~every 15 s with `"points": []` and a growing `waiting_time`, and that the response's `waiting_sec` climbs. Confirm no POST at all once the timer stops changing. Tap Kettik and confirm a batch goes out within ~1 s (not 15 s). Force a persistent 403 and confirm the POSTs stop after the 5th rather than continuing every 15 s.

Android's comment at MyTrackingService.kt:183-188 records that the server re-sends `waiting_charge_started` in EVERY subsequent response, contradicting the spec — so the `chargeStartedBeeped` latch is load-bearing, not defensive: without it the driver gets a beep every 15 s for the rest of the wait. The 5000-bucket on `waiting_charge_step` serves the same de-dup purpose. Reset both latches on new-order start, not only on stop, or a second trip in the same process is born silent. #### 1.5 `[P1]` Adopt the server's wait totals on reconnect / second device, and refresh fare on socket reconnect

**What to build**

VERIFIED ABSENT: no `adoptServerWaits` or any equivalent in the iOS repo; `LocationTracker`'s counters are only ever written from local ticking (`waitAccumulatedMs` at LocationTracker.swift:536/544/571) or restored from `CalculationStore` (:265-270). Nothing ever raises them from a server value.

Product rule from the backend owner (Muhammad, 2026-07-20): the server keeps the MAX of every timer value ever sent for an order, so on a fresh install, a second device, or a long process death the app must take the waits from the server ONCE and CONTINUE from there rather than restarting at zero. Port `MyTrackingService.adoptServerWaits` (kt:942+): on every fare publish, read `live.waiting_sec * 1000` and — ONLY when the rider is aboard — `live.on_way_sec * 1000`; compare against the local `waitedTimeUntilGoneMs` / `(waitedTimeMs - waitedTimeUntilGoneMs)`; take the max of each; and if the new total exceeds the current total by MORE THAN 2000 ms, lift the local accumulator (`waitAccumulatedMs` + the published `waitedTimeMs`, and `waitedTimeUntilGoneMs` when aboard) and persist via `CalculationStore`. The existing `setWaitState(orderId:baseMs:segmentStartedAt:)` and `setWaitedTimeUntilGone(orderId:ms:)` calls at LocationTracker.swift:410-411 are the right persistence mutators. Expose this as an `adoptServerWaits(pickupMs:onWayMs:)` method ON `LocationTracker` — the counters are `private`/`private(set)` so it cannot be done from outside.

Second half: also call `refreshServerFare()` when the socket transitions back to `.online`. iOS currently refreshes only at screen open (TripMVVM.swift:168), after `order/start` (:423), after Kettik (:535) and after `order/complete` (:758) — I confirmed there is NO reconnect call site, even though `refreshServerFare`'s own doc comment at :897-899 claims one. Copy the existing transition-into-.online pattern already used at `RootMVVM.swift:78-80` and `MapMVVM.swift:326` (`guard status == .online else { return }`) rather than inventing a new one, and fix the stale comment.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/services/MyTrackingService.kt:398 (call site), :942-954+ (adoptServerWaits, incl. the pre-Go on_way carve-out at :945-951)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/LocationTracker.swift (new adoptServerWaits(pickupMs:onWayMs:) mutating the counters + CalculationStore persist); MehrgoDriver/MVVM/TripMVVM.swift (call it from BOTH fare sinks — subscribeSockets :986-1005 and subscribeServerFare :1009+; add the .online-transition refresh; fix the stale doc comment at :897-899)

> **Gotchas.** Android's carve-out at MyTrackingService.kt:945-951 is the trap and must be ported verbatim in spirit: pre-Go the server's `on_way` component is DELIBERATELY not adopted, because the local model is single-bucket before Go and `markPassengerOnboard()` freezes `waitedTimeUntilGoneMs` from the running total (LocationTracker.swift:329) — an adopted on-way slice would land inside that freeze and then be re-sent as PICKUP wait, which the server MAXes upward = double-billed waiting. The 2000 ms threshold exists to stop adoption from fighting the 1 s local tick; without it the counters jitter. Adoption must go through `waitAccumulatedMs`, not the derived `waitedTimeMs`, or the next tick (`waitedTimeMs = waitAccumulatedMs + openWaitSegmentMs`, :544) instantly overwrites the adopted value. This only becomes meaningful once items 1-3 land — until the app sends timers, the server has nothing larger to offer.

**Acceptance** — Start a trip, accrue 3 min of wait, force-quit and reinstall (or sign in on a second device) and reopen the active order: the wait counter resumes at ~3 min, not 0, and the receipt bills the full wait. Toggle airplane mode for 60 s mid-wait and confirm a fare refresh fires on reconnect and the counters do not double-count. Confirm that BEFORE Kettik the pickup counter never absorbs an on-way value from the server.

#### 1.7 `[P2]` Optional: send batches over the existing websocket with ack, REST as fallback

**What to build**

VERIFIED: `GpsBatchSocketEnvelope` / `GpsBatchSocketData` (GpsBatch.swift:39-53) are declared with ZERO call sites — I grepped both type names across the repo and they appear only in their own declaration. The transport was modelled and never wired; every batch goes over `sendREST`.

If you wire it: add the two timer fields to `GpsBatchSocketData`, add a send + await-ack path on `WebSocketManager` (it already has a keyed-dispatch switch at :178+ and a status machine to hang this off), and keep REST as the fallback on every failure. The ack must be matched to the specific batch, not just "the next frame"; without a correlation id a reordered ack marks the wrong cpids acknowledged. Any timeout must fall back to REST rather than dropping the batch, and the cpid idempotency (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`) is what makes a REST replay after a lost socket ack safe — do not weaken it. `WebSocketManager`'s status can be `.connecting` (WebSocketManager.swift:31, :282) which is NOT ok to send on; gate on `.online` only.

Delay the ack past 5 s and confirm the REST fallback fires and the same cpids are not double-counted.

### 2. Waiting timer, process-kill resume, and background survival (driver trip meter)

**Gaps:** 5 total — P0: 1 · P1: 2 · P2: 2

#### iOS today

VERIFIED: iOS models the wait as timestamp-anchored segments — `waitAccumulatedMs` (closed segments) + an open segment from `waitSegmentStartedAt` (LocationTracker.swift:71-79), ticked at :505-557 with helpers `openWaitSegmentMs`/`closeWaitSegment`/`persistWaitState` at :560-587. Both fields are written in ONE Core Data transaction by `CalculationStore.setWaitState(orderId:baseMs:segmentStartedAt:)` (CalculationStore.swift:138-143) every 5 s and on every arm/disarm, and `startTripTracking` recomputes `waitedTimeMs = base + (now − segmentStart)` on the `!isReentry` branch (LocationTracker.swift:264-271) — so the WAIT clock genuinely survives suspension and full termination at ms precision, better than Android's `was_waiting`/`wait_last_tick` dead-gap patch. The pickup/on-way split matches Android (`markPassengerOnboard` closes the segment, snapshots `waitedTimeUntilGoneMs`, persists, sets `wayStarted`, LocationTracker.swift:322-341), and TripView.swift:1380-1381 already computes the exact pickup/on-way split formula (`onWayMs = max(0, waitedTimeMs − waitedTimeUntilGoneMs)`) that any server-sync work should reuse. Display trip time has its own persisted wall-clock anchors `tripStartedAt`/`passengerOnboardAt` in UserDefaults per order (TripMVVM.swift:180-241), and the ActivityKit Live Activity (WaitActivityManager/WaitActivityAttributes/WaitWidget) is a genuine iOS-only strength, not a gap. Two corrections to the brief: `flushNow()` ALREADY exists on the uploader (GpsBatchUploader.swift:206) so no new `nudge()` is needed, and `trackedMs` is a DEAD parameter in `FareCalculator.compute` (declared FareCalculator.swift:105, never referenced in the body) — trip time is not priced client-side on iOS, which lowers the blast radius of the trip-clock gap. What remains genuinely missing: the trip clock loses the kill gap, the wait-ARMED flag is unpersisted (and is wrongly force-re-armed on ordinary re-entry), no timers ever reach the server (`GpsBatchRequest` is `{points}` only, GpsBatch.swift:33-35), FareSummary decodes no `events` and no `on_way_sec`, and nothing resumes tracking on a background relaunch.

#### Android today

Android accrues wait and trip time as tick counters inside `MyTrackingService`, patched for process-kill with a persisted `was_waiting` flag plus a `wait_last_tick` heartbeat; on restart the dead gap between `wait_last_tick` and now is credited to BOTH the wait and the tracked-time counters, so neither clock loses the kill window (see the `wait_timer_resume` work). The wait meter itself has no speed gate — `startWaitingTimer` accrues wall clock while armed (MyTrackingService.kt:812-836) and `startTrackingTimer` accrues `while (isTracking)` with no boarding gate (MyTrackingService.kt:788-808). `GpsBatchUploader`/`GpsBatchSocketChannel` send cumulative `waiting_time` and `waiting_time_ontheway` (milliseconds) inside the `order-gps/batch` body and adopt the server's larger value back via the `live` block on `FareResponse`, per the live-fare protocol v4 work, so a reinstall or second device does not restart the wait from zero. The service beeps at service level on the server's waiting-charge start/step billing events, audible with the screen off and while the app is backgrounded. `MyTrackingService` is START_STICKY: a null-intent restart re-reads the persisted active order and resumes GPS plus uploads with zero driver action.

<a id="gap-2-1"></a>

#### 2.1 `[P0]` Persist the wait-armed flag — the meter is force-re-armed on ordinary re-entry, over-billing the rider

**What to build**

CONFIRMED and UPGRADED P1→P0. The brief understated this: the over-billing half needs no process kill at all. TripView.swift:900-950 exposes a real manual Start/Stop wait button (`tracker.stopWaiting()` / `tracker.startWaiting()`), and `restoreLifecyclePhase` calls `LocationTracker.shared.startWaiting()` UNCONDITIONALLY at TripMVVM.swift:262 for `.changedArrived`. TripMVVM is rebuilt on every TripView re-entry, so: driver deliberately PAUSES the wait at pickup → minimises or navigates away → reopens the trip → the meter silently re-arms and bills the rider for a wait the driver stopped. The in-code comment at :252-253 asserting `startWaiting` 'is a no-op — flag stays true regardless' is only true if the driver never paused, and is therefore wrong. That is a rider-facing overcharge on a routine path, which is why this outranks the trip-clock item. The mirror defect is the self-documented KNOWN GAP at TripMVVM.swift:271-283: after a kill at `.changedGone`, `markPassengerOnboard(snapshotUntilGone: false)` closes the segment and disarms, so a manually-started on-route wait stops accruing (under-billing). Fix both with one persisted flag: add a `waitArmed` Bool (defaultValueString NO) to the `Calculation` entity; extend the atomic writer to `setWaitState(orderId:baseMs:segmentStartedAt:armed:)` so base/segmentStart/armed are always written in the same transaction; set it from `startWaiting()` and `stopWaiting()` (LocationTracker.swift:374-383 — note `startWaiting` is currently a one-line body with no persist at all, so it needs a `persistWaitState()` call added), from `markArrivedAtPickup()` (armed = true) and `markPassengerOnboard()` (armed = false). Restore `isWaiting = dto.waitArmed` in `startTripTracking`'s `!isReentry` branch alongside the wait counters, and expose it as `private(set) var restoredWaitArmed: Bool` so TripMVVM need not re-read Core Data. Then in `restoreLifecyclePhase`: `.changedArrived` → call `markArrivedAtPickup()` and call `startWaiting()` ONLY when the persisted flag was true OR no Calculation row existed for this order (preserving today's first-entry auto-arm, which is the behavior the driver expects right after tapping 'Yetib keldim'); `.changedGone` → after `markPassengerOnboard(snapshotUntilGone: false)`, re-arm via `startWaiting()` when the persisted flag was true.

**Android reference** — The persisted was_waiting flag in the wait-timer resume path — Android restores the armed state rather than inferring it from the lifecycle stage; MyTrackingService.kt:812-836 (startWaitingTimer)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/LocationTracker.swift (startWaiting/stopWaiting :374-383, markArrivedAtPickup :305-308, markPassengerOnboard :322-341, startTripTracking :232-288); MehrgoDriver/MVVM/TripMVVM.swift (restoreLifecyclePhase, switch at :242-284, unconditional startWaiting at :262); MehrgoDriver/Persistence/CalculationStore.swift (setWaitState :138-143); MehrgoDriver.xcdatamodeld

> **Gotchas.** The 'no Calculation row existed' carve-out is load-bearing — drop it and the driver who taps 'Yetib keldim' for the first time gets a meter that never starts. Distinguish it properly: `upsert(orderId:)` runs BEFORE the `get` in startTripTracking (:252-254), so by the time you read the DTO a row always exists; you must capture 'row was absent' from inside `upsert` (or check before upserting) rather than testing for nil afterwards. Keep `waitArmed` inside the same `setWaitState` transaction — an armed flag that lands without its matching segment timestamp reintroduces exactly the double-count the coupling comment at CalculationStore.swift:131-137 warns about. Also note HomeMVVM.swift:538-542 auto-disarms above 20 km/h via `stopWaiting()`; that path must persist armed = false too or a kill right after the auto-stop resurrects the meter.

(2) Under-billing: after boarding, manually start an on-route wait, force-quit, relaunch and reopen the trip — the wait counter must resume accruing without the driver tapping anything. (3) First arrival is unaffected: fresh order, tap 'Yetib keldim', wait counter starts automatically.

#### 2.2 `[P1]` Credit the process-kill dead gap to the trip clock, not just the wait clock

**What to build**

CONFIRMED, but DOWNGRADED P0→P1: the claimed money mechanism is unverified. `trackedMs` is a dead parameter in `FareCalculator.compute` (declared FareCalculator.swift:105, never read in the body), so trip time is NOT priced client-side on iOS; the assertion that the backend proportionally shrinks the wait charge when waiting_time > execution_time is an assumption about server behavior that nobody has checked. Confirm it with the backend before scheduling — if true, re-raise to P0. The defect itself is real: `trackedTimeMs` is dt-accumulated in tick() (LocationTracker.swift:541) and flushed to Core Data only every 10 s (:546-550), and `startTripTracking` restores the raw stored value with no gap credit (LocationTracker.swift:255), while the wait counter three lines below at :264-271 DOES recover its gap. So `order/complete` can report `execution_time` (TripMVVM.swift:694) smaller than `waiting_time` (:693) — a logically impossible state for the same trip. Fix: (a) add a `trackedLastTickAt` Double (timeIntervalSince1970, defaultValueString 0) attribute to the `Calculation` entity in MehrgoDriver.xcdatamodeld — the file is a single unversioned model with no .xccurrentversion siblings, so this is a plain lightweight migration; add the field to `CalculationDTO` (CalculationStore.swift:15-30), to the `upsert` seed block (:54-61), and to the `get` mapping (:88-95). (b) Replace `setTrackedTime(orderId:ms:)` (CalculationStore.swift:117-121) with an atomic `setTrackedState(orderId:ms:lastTickAt:)` writing both keys in ONE `update` block, exactly as `setWaitState` couples its two fields (:138-143). (c) In tick() write `lastTickAt = now` alongside `trackedTimeMs` on the existing 10 s cadence; in `stopTripTracking` (:409) write lastTickAt = 0. (d) In `startTripTracking`, inside the `if !isReentry` branch only (never on re-entry — the singleton is already authoritative there and a re-entry credit would double-count), after `trackedTimeMs = dto.trackedTimeMs` add `if dto.trackedLastTickAt > 0 { trackedTimeMs &+= Int64(max(0, Date().timeIntervalSince1970 - dto.trackedLastTickAt) * 1000) }`, and set `lastTickAt = Date()` before `startTicker()` so the first tick cannot re-count the same span. Reject the brief's 'simpler alternative' of deriving from `tripStartedAt`: that anchor is explicitly display-only and `restoreLifecyclePhase` BACKFILLS it to `now` whenever it is absent (TripMVVM.swift, the block above the switch), so on a reinstall or second-device resume it would silently reset the trip clock to zero.

**Android reference** — MyTrackingService.kt:788-808 (startTrackingTimer, no speed/boarding gate); the was_waiting + wait_last_tick heartbeat restart path that credits the dead gap to wait AND track

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/LocationTracker.swift (tick :505-557, startTripTracking :232-288, stopTripTracking :392-425); MehrgoDriver/Persistence/CalculationStore.swift (CalculationDTO :15-30, upsert :47-77, get :80-98, setTrackedTime :117-121); MehrgoDriver/Persistence/MehrgoDriver.xcdatamodeld/MehrgoDriver.xcdatamodel/contents (Calculation entity)

Do not forget `lastTickAt = Date()` before `startTicker()`: `tick()` computes dt from `lastTickAt`, which is initialised at property-declaration time (:59), so a restore without re-anchoring adds the gap twice. `GpsBatchStore.swift:185-186` also seeds `trackedTimeMs`/`waitedTimeMs` when it self-heals a missing Calculation row — seed the new attribute there too or the self-heal path writes a row the DTO cannot read consistently.

Then finish and inspect the `order/complete` body — `execution_time` must be >= `waiting_time` for a trip where the driver waited the whole time.

#### 2.3 `[P1]` Send cumulative wait timers in order-gps/batch and adopt the server's MAX

**What to build**

CONFIRMED. `GpsBatchRequest` is `{points}` only (GpsBatch.swift:33-35) and `sendREST` posts `["points": points.map(Self.encode)]` with nothing else (GpsBatchUploader.swift, sendREST ~:369-383). `flush()` bails at `if points.isEmpty { return }` inside its drain loop, so a parked driver with a running wait meter sends the server literally nothing, and a reinstall or second device restarts the wait from zero. `FareSummary` already decodes top-level `waiting_sec` (GpsBatch.swift:67, CodingKeys :84) but has NO `on_way_sec` and NO `live` block. Build three things. (1) Outbound: add optional `waiting_time` and `waiting_time_ontheway` (Int64, MILLISECONDS, snake_case) to `GpsBatchRequest` and the `sendREST` body dict, attached ONLY when changed since the last SUCCESSFUL send — keep `lastSentWaitMs`/`lastSentOnWayMs` (init -1, reset to 0 in `start(orderId:)`, updated only after the request returns 2xx, i.e. after the `ack` call, never on the 403/404 retry path). Timer provider reads from LocationTracker on each flush and should reuse the split already written in TripView.swift:1380-1381: total = `waitedTimeMs`; if `wayStarted` → (pickup: `waitedTimeUntilGoneMs`, onWay: `max(0, total − waitedTimeUntilGoneMs)`), else → (pickup: total, onWay: 0). Expose `wayStarted` — it is currently `private` (LocationTracker.swift:85). (2) Empty-batch path: when `points.isEmpty` and either timer changed, POST a points-less batch carrying just the timers instead of returning. Do NOT add a `nudge()` as the brief proposes — `flushNow()` already exists (GpsBatchUploader.swift:206) and does exactly this; call it from `tick()` on the existing 5 s wait cadence (LocationTracker.swift:551-555). (3) Inbound: add a `live` block to FareSummary decoding (`waiting_sec`, `on_way_sec`) alongside the top-level fields, and implement `adoptServerWaits`: compute curPickup/curOnWay as above, newPickup = max(curPickup, waiting_sec*1000), newOnWay = max(curOnWay, on_way_sec*1000) but only when `wayStarted`, and apply the delta to `waitAccumulatedMs`/`waitedTimeUntilGoneMs` only when `newTotal > curTotal + 2000` ms. Entry point is the existing `fareSubject` sink (TripMVVM.subscribeServerFare) or directly in `flush()` after the ack.

**Android reference** — GpsBatchUploader.kt / GpsBatchSocketChannel.kt (waiting_time + waiting_time_ontheway in the batch body); FareResponse.kt live block; the live-fare protocol v4 §7 timers-in-batch work

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/Models/GpsBatch.swift (GpsBatchRequest :33-35, FareSummary :62-120 — extend BOTH the `init(from decoder:)` and the memberwise `init`, the struct has two); MehrgoDriver/Shared/GpsBatchUploader.swift (flush ~:292-365, sendREST ~:369-383, flushNow :206, lastSent* state); MehrgoDriver/Shared/LocationTracker.swift (expose wayStarted, timer provider, flushNow call in tick :551-555, adopt entry point)

> **Gotchas.** Milliseconds, not seconds, on the way OUT (matching `order/complete`'s `waiting_time`) while the server's `waiting_sec`/`on_way_sec` come back in SECONDS — the unit flip is the easy bug here. Update `lastSentWaitMs` only after a genuine 2xx: `flush()` has three distinct failure exits (403/404 streak, ClientError, generic catch) and marking sent on any of them permanently strands the timer. Adopting server values must go through `waitAccumulatedMs`, never by assigning `waitedTimeMs` directly — `waitedTimeMs` is recomputed as base + open segment on the very next tick (:544) and a direct write is erased within 1 s. The 2 s threshold exists to stop adopt/report feedback oscillation; keep it. Calling `flushNow()` every 5 s from tick() means a points-less POST every 5 s for a long wait — confirm the backend tolerates that rate before shipping.

### 3. WebSocket connection, heartbeat, reconnect, event routing (driver app)

**Gaps:** 6 total — P0: 1 · P1: 0 · P2: 5

#### iOS today

VERIFIED: `MehrgoDriver/Shared/WebSocketManager.swift` is a `@MainActor` singleton over `URLSessionWebSocketTask` and is at or ahead of Android on core mechanics. It never calls `sendPing(pongReceiveHandler:)` (repo-wide grep: zero hits) and instead sends the app-level text frame `task.send(.string("ping"))` (`sendPing()`, :263-273) on a `Constants.minimalTimeConnectSocket` cadence, waiting for the `pong` key (:160-166) — the RFC6455-control-frame trap that made Android flap is genuinely avoided. `maxMissedPongs = 3` with a re-ping on each tolerated miss (`healthTick()`, :241-260), a single de-duped failure funnel (`handleDrop()` :279-284 + `reconnectScheduled` :289), and task-identity guards in `receive()` (:131/135), `sendPing()` (:264/268) and both delegate callbacks (:324, :344) are all present and correct. It is BETTER than Android in two confirmed ways: `SocketStatus` (:36) distinguishes `.connecting` from `.notOnLine` (`scheduleReconnect()` :293-300, sticky across retries per the guard at :94, rendered by `MapHomeView.statusBanner` :522 and gated at :128), and `didOpenWithProtocol` (:318-334) deliberately stays `.connecting` until a real pong lands. Order-cancel id-guarding is confirmed everywhere: `TripMVVM.subscribeSockets()` hard-guards `event.order.id == self.order.id` (TripMVVM.swift:931) and additionally guards stale `.accepted` echoes on `currentState`; `MapMVVM.subscribeSockets()` removes by id only (MapMVVM.swift:298-300); `HomeMVVM.handle()` compares `pendingOffer?.id` / `queuedOrder?.id` / `activeOrder?.id` (HomeMVVM.swift:699-724). Socket keys match Android 1:1. GPS batches go over REST `order-gps/batch` only (GpsBatchUploader.swift:376), so ignoring the `order_gps_batch` ack key is correct. Two partial mitigations exist that the report understated: `MapMVVM.subscribeSocketStatus()` (:322-330) already resyncs the pool on every `.online` edge, and `RootMVVM.subscribeSocketReconnectValidation()` (:73-84) already probes `user/me` on the same edge AND on foreground via `RootView.swift:62-64`. Correction to the report: `HomeMVVM.loadActive()` hits `order/my-orders` (HomeMVVM.swift:629), NOT `order/active` — no per-id order GET exists anywhere in the iOS client. Also `TripPanel` is now a plain always-mounted view (MainView.swift:59-63), so TripMVVM is alive even at the peek detent — HomeMVVM's `isTripMinimized`-gated cancel teardown comment (:712-724) is stale relative to that.

#### Android today

Android's socket lives in `MyTrackingService` with `MySocketListener`. VERIFIED: `checkingConnectToSocket()` (MyTrackingService.kt:873-900) is a `Handler.postDelayed` loop on the service Looper at `MINIMAL_TIME_CONNECT_SOCKET`, resetting `missedPongs` on a pong and calling `reconnectSocketNow()` (:693-698) only after `MAX_MISSED_SOCKET_PONGS = 3` consecutive silences — i.e. reconnects are paced at ~30 s and there is no per-failure immediate retry, confirming the churn comparison in claim 4. `MySocketListener.onMessage` (:55-100) gates BOTH new-order keys on `UserManager.getStatusValue() == DRIVER_ACTIVE` (:70-80) before broadcasting, while `ORDER_ACCEPTED` / `ORDER_CANCELLED` / `ORDER_CANCELLED_PRIVATE` pass ungated — exactly the listener-level placement claim 5 describes. `MapFragment.socketListenerReceiver` (maps/yandex_map/MapFragment.kt:286-305) fires on socket-open and re-fetches BOTH lists — `getOrders()` + `getActiveMyOrders()` — after wiping route/marker state; the `orderDataReceiver` below it (:307-338) also re-fetches the active order on an id-matched cancel. `MyTrackingService` turns every `NOTIFICATION_NEW` frame into a system notification via `createNotificationForNotification(data.message, data.text)` (:465-476 → :1215), unconditionally, not gated on backgrounded. Note `MyFirebaseMessagingService` also posts a system notification for any FCM alert payload, so Android may itself double-notify on the same event.

#### 3.1 `[P0]` Resync the ACTIVE order on every socket reconnect, not just the pool

**What to build**

CONFIRMED, with one correction: the endpoint is `order/my-orders`, not `order/active` — there is no per-id order GET anywhere in the iOS client (repo-wide grep of `path: "order`), so every resync must GET `order/my-orders` and match by id. Today `HomeMVVM.loadActive()` (HomeMVVM.swift:625-645) has exactly ONE caller — `MainView.swift:67 .task`, which runs once on appear and never re-fires (the view is not re-identified) — and its `if activeOrder == nil` guard at :638 means it can never clear or correct an already-seeded order. `MapMVVM.subscribeSocketStatus()` (:322-330) resyncs only `recommendedOrders`; `RootMVVM.subscribeSocketReconnectValidation()` (:73-84) only probes `user/me` and deliberately discards the result. So an `order_cancelled` that lands while the socket is down is stuck forever for the session: the home map keeps a dead trip, `TripPanel` stays mounted, and HomeMVVM's own comment at :718-720 documents that the departure-confirm watcher will then loop a failing `startTrip` against it. Build: (1) add `subscribeSocketStatus()` to HomeMVVM sinking `WebSocketManager.shared.$status.removeDuplicates()` and, on a transition INTO `.online`, call a new `resyncActive()`. (2) `resyncActive()` GETs `order/my-orders`, sets `activeOrdersCount = items.count`, and — bypassing the :638 seed guard — reconciles: no item with `activeOrder!.id` ⇒ the order died while we were down, run the same teardown the socket cancel branch at :729-736 runs (`stopTripTracking` + `GpsBatchStore.purge` + `CalculationStore.delete` + `WaitActivityManager.shared.end()` + `clearActiveOrder()`), and `clearQueuedOrder()` if `queuedOrder?.id` also vanished; same id present ⇒ route through the EXISTING `syncActiveOrder(_:)` (HomeMVVM.swift:412-419), which already enforces the `incoming.state >= local.state` monotonicity guard — do not write `activeOrder` directly. (3) In `TripMVVM.subscribeSockets()` (TripMVVM.swift:926) also sink `$status` and on `.online` re-fetch `order/my-orders`, pick the entry matching `self.order.id`, and merge with the same `>=` rule; if it is absent, run TripMVVM's existing `.cancelled` teardown path so an open trip self-heals.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/maps/yandex_map/MapFragment.kt:286-305 (socketListenerReceiver → getOrders() + getActiveMyOrders() on every socket-open broadcast); :307-338 (orderDataReceiver re-fetches the active order on an id-matched cancel)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MVVM/HomeMVVM.swift (add subscribeSocketStatus + resyncActive; loadActive at :625, seed guard at :638, syncActiveOrder at :412, cancel-teardown template at :723-737) and MehrgoDriver/MVVM/TripMVVM.swift (extend subscribeSockets at :926)

> **Gotchas.** TripPanel is NO LONGER a sheet — MainView.swift:59-63 mounts it as a plain view whenever `activeOrder != nil`, so TripMVVM is alive even at the peek detent. HomeMVVM's `isTripMinimized`-gated teardown at :723-724 (and its comment claiming TripMVVM is unmounted while minimised) is stale against that. If you add the resync to BOTH HomeMVVM and TripMVVM without a gate, a missing order will run two teardowns concurrently — purging GPS batches and CoreData rows twice and calling clearActiveOrder while TripMVVM is mid-teardown. Pick one owner (HomeMVVM, since it survives the panel) or gate TripMVVM's copy the same way. Second trap: `$status` also emits `.online` on the very first connect, which races MainView's one-shot `.task` loadActive — make resyncActive idempotent and a no-op when `activeOrder == nil` and the fetched list is empty. Third: never let resync write `activeOrder` directly; the monotonicity guard in syncActiveOrder exists specifically because the server replays a cached `order_accepted` with the pre-advance `state = 2` on reconnect (documented at TripMVVM.swift:952-968) — a raw server snapshot would snap a mid-trip driver back to 'Boshlash'.

**Acceptance** — With an active accepted order, put the device in airplane mode; from the dispatcher cancel that order; restore network. Within one reconnect the trip panel must tear down, the home map must clear the route/markers, and the wait Live Activity must end — no app relaunch. Inverse check: slide Boshlash → Yetib keldim, then force a reconnect (toggle airplane mode for ~5 s) and confirm the CTA stays at the advanced stage and does not revert to Boshlash.

<a id="gap-3-2"></a>

#### 3.2 `[P2]` Revalidate the socket on foreground instead of trusting a possibly-stale .online

**What to build**

CONFIRMED absent, but DOWNGRADED from P1 — the premise is narrower than claimed. `WebSocketManager` has no `lastPongAt`, no `revalidate()`, and no lifecycle observer; the only foreground hooks in the whole repo are `MehrgoDriverApp.swift:111-127` (`refreshBalance()`), `RootView.swift:62-64` (`validateSessionIfDue()`) and `MapHomeView.swift:250-252` (`loadVerification()`) — none touches the socket. `connect()` has only two callers, both MapMVVM (:228 goOnline, :255 goOnlineForActiveOrder). HOWEVER: the socket is only ever connected while online, and going online also calls `LocationTracker.startWhileOnline()` (MapMVVM.swift:229/256), which runs continuous updates with `allowsBackgroundLocationUpdates = true` and `pausesLocationUpdatesAutomatically = false` (LocationTracker.swift:112-113) under the `location` background mode (Info.plist:49-56). For an Always-authorized driver the process is therefore NOT suspended in background and both socket and health timer keep running — the claimed 30 s stale-.online window does not occur. It DOES occur for the When-In-Use cohort (LocationTracker.swift:123-125 requests Always but drivers can decline, and AccessPermissionsView.swift:65 treats WhenInUse as `.partial`), after an OS background termination, and after a Wi-Fi↔cellular handover that leaves a half-open socket with no error callback. In those cases `status` still reads `.online`, MapHomeView.swift:128 hides the banner, and the driver is told they are taking orders for up to 3 health ticks. Build: record `lastPongAt: Date` in the pong branch (WebSocketManager.swift:160-166); add `func revalidate()` that returns unless `wantsConnection`, calls `handleDrop()` when `Date().timeIntervalSince(lastPongAt) > 2 * Constants.minimalTimeConnectSocket`, and otherwise resets `pongReceived = false; missedPongs = 0` then `sendPing()` + `startHealthTimer()`. Install a `UIApplication.willEnterForegroundNotification` observer inside the manager so it fires regardless of which screen is up.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/services/MyTrackingService.kt:873-900 — the heartbeat runs on the service Looper inside a foreground service, so Android has no suspend/resume staleness window at all

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/WebSocketManager.swift (add lastPongAt beside the pong branch at :160-166, a revalidate() method, and a willEnterForeground observer registered in init at :76)

> **Gotchas.** `handleDrop()` → `scheduleReconnect()` increments `failedAttempts`, and at 3 it flips the banner to the sticky `.notOnLine` 'account live on another device' state (WebSocketManager.swift:292-300). A foreground revalidate that force-drops must not be allowed to walk the driver into that message on three ordinary app switches — either reset `failedAttempts` before the forced drop or add a separate revalidate path that reconnects without scoring an attempt. Also do not double-fire: pair this with claim 1's `$status` sink or a foreground-triggered `.connecting → .online` edge will re-run resyncActive on every app switch. `init()` is `private override init()` and the class is `@MainActor`; add the observer with a block-based `NotificationCenter.addObserver(forName:object:queue:.main)` hopping through `Task { @MainActor in }` rather than a selector.

The status banner must show 'connecting' and clear within one heartbeat, and a test order dispatched during that window must arrive after the reconnect. On an Always-authorized device, background for the same period and confirm the socket was never dropped (no banner flash) — revalidate should take the cheap ping path, not handleDrop.

#### 3.3 `[P2]` Schedule the heartbeat timer in .common run-loop mode

**What to build**

CONFIRMED. `startHealthTimer()` (WebSocketManager.swift:230-238) uses `Timer.scheduledTimer(withTimeInterval:repeats:)`, which registers on the main run loop in `.default` mode only; repo-wide grep finds zero uses of `RunLoop.main.add` or `forMode:` anywhere. While a UIScrollView-backed view is being dragged the main run loop is in `UITrackingRunLoopMode` and the timer does not fire — we neither send `"ping"` (the server may decide we are dead) nor count missed pongs (a socket that died mid-scroll goes undetected). DOWNGRADED from P1: the stall lasts only as long as the drag itself (seconds on the orders sheet / notifications / history lists), and the `maxMissedPongs = 3` tolerance absorbs a skipped tick. The Yandex map's own pan gestures do NOT switch run-loop modes (it is not a UIScrollView), so the driver's most common interaction is unaffected. Fix: `let t = Timer(timeInterval: Constants.minimalTimeConnectSocket, repeats: true) { [weak self] _ in Task { @MainActor in self?.healthTick() } }; RunLoop.main.add(t, forMode: .common); healthTimer = t`. Keep the existing `healthTimer?.invalidate()` calls in startHealthTimer (:231), disconnect() (:109) and scheduleReconnect() (:302) — `.common` registration does not change invalidation.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/services/MyTrackingService.kt:873-900 — Handler.postDelayed on the service Looper, never suspended by UI input

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/WebSocketManager.swift:230-238 (startHealthTimer)

> **Gotchas.** Timers created with `Timer(timeInterval:...)` are NOT auto-scheduled — forgetting the `RunLoop.main.add` line silently kills the heartbeat entirely, which is far worse than the bug being fixed. The same `.default`-mode hazard exists in GpsBatchUploader.swift:408, LocationTracker.swift:499 and :624, and TripMVVM.swift:1187 (the live-fare ticker); those are outside this area — do not silently change them here, raise them separately.

**Acceptance** — Instrument `healthTick()` with an os_log, open the notifications or history list, and drag-scroll continuously for ~30 s. Before the fix the log goes silent for the whole drag; after it, ticks continue at the 10 s cadence throughout.

#### 3.4 `[P2]` Back off the reconnect interval instead of retrying every 2 s forever

**What to build**

CONFIRMED. `scheduleReconnect()` (WebSocketManager.swift:287-313) always uses `DispatchQueue.main.asyncAfter(deadline: .now() + 2)` with no escalation and no cap, and `connect()` failing feeds `receive()`'s error branch straight back into `handleDrop()` → `scheduleReconnect()`, so a backend outage or a long tunnel is ~30 TCP+TLS handshakes per minute for the whole shift. The Android comparison holds and was verified: `reconnectSocketNow()` (MyTrackingService.kt:693-698) is called ONLY from the paced heartbeat (:892), i.e. roughly once per 30 s, with an explicit comment that this is what stops it tight-looping. Build: derive the delay from the already-tracked `failedAttempts` — `let delay = min(2 * pow(2.0, Double(min(failedAttempts - 1, 4))), 30)` → 2, 4, 8, 16, 30, 30… — and let it reset where `failedAttempts = 0` already happens (the pong branch, :163, and disconnect(), :108). Keep the `.notOnLine` takeover threshold at 3 attempts so banner semantics are unchanged.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/services/MyTrackingService.kt:693-698 and :873-900 — reconnect is driven exclusively by the ~30 s heartbeat, never per-failure

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/WebSocketManager.swift:287-313 (scheduleReconnect)

> **Gotchas.** `failedAttempts` is incremented at :292 BEFORE the delay is computed, so the `min(failedAttempts - 1, 4)` offset is required or the first retry waits 4 s instead of 2. This must ship together with the foreground `revalidate()` from the other claim — otherwise a driver reopening the app after a long outage sits out a 30 s backed-off timer with a stale banner, which is a worse experience than the churn being fixed. Do not gate the backoff on `status == .notOnLine`: the takeover case is exactly the long-lived one that needs it most.

**Acceptance** — Put the device in airplane mode while online and watch the os_log 'connecting' lines: intervals must widen 2/4/8/16/30/30 s instead of a flat 2 s. Restore network and confirm the very next attempt succeeds and that a subsequent unrelated drop restarts at 2 s (proving failedAttempts reset on the pong).

Promote it to `private(set) var wantsConnection` (or expose `var driverIsOnline: Bool { wantsConnection }`) and add an early return in `handle()` (:193-218) for `Constants.socketOrderNew` / `socketOrderNewPrivate` when it is false — one listener-level gate covering both HomeMVVM and MapMVVM, matching Android's placement.

### 4. Trip finish receipt, fare display rules, and slide-to-confirm CTAs

**Gaps:** 10 total — P0: 5 · P1: 3 · P2: 2

#### iOS today

Verified: `TripMVVM.finish()` (MVVM/TripMVVM.swift:609-796) fires `GpsBatchUploader.shared.flushNow()` without awaiting, computes a `FareBreakdown` via `FareCalculator.compute(...)` with `serverGross: nil` (TripMVVM.swift:659, commented "boss spec 2026-06-24 … DISPLAY-ONLY"), POSTs `order/complete`, and only then calls `refreshServerFare()` (`GET order-gps/fare`, TripMVVM.swift:900-914) to fill `finalServerFare`. So the billed gross comes 100% from the local taximeter / 300 m-radius / 1000 m-tolerance ladder (Shared/FareCalculator.swift:169-245); the `.server` short-circuit at FareCalculator.swift:188-218 is genuinely dead in production. `GpsBatchUploader.sendREST` (Shared/GpsBatchUploader.swift:371-383) posts `{"points":[...]}` only, and `flush()` returns early on `points.isEmpty` (line 312), so a stationary driver ships nothing at all. `FareSummary` (Shared/Models/GpsBatch.swift:62-119) decodes exactly eight legacy fields — no `live`, no `breakdown`, no `events` — and both the socket path (`WebSocketManager.swift:188`) and the batch ack (`GpsBatchUploader.swift:324`) reuse it, so nothing on iOS can see `live.price`/`surcharge`/`waiting_billing`. `submit()` sends the whole wait as a single `"waiting_time"` (TripMVVM.swift:693). Partial credit where due, and it narrows two claims: `TripFinishView` ALREADY prefers the server values for the two info rows — `displayedDistanceMeters` uses `serverFare?.distanceKm` and `displayedWaitedMs` uses `serverFare?.waitingSec` (TripFinishView.swift:80-93) — so only the wait COST line is still local. Double-fire protection is genuinely solid (`guard !isTransitioning, !didFinish`, `defer`, `processingPill` replacing the control, `didFinish` swapping the CTA to a "done" exit at TripView.swift:979-984). CTAs: `.accepted`/`.started`/`.changedArrived` are `SlideToActButton`, `.changedGone` is a plain `PrimaryButton` (TripView.swift:988-1000). One thing the report missed: `performFinish()` has TWO call sites — the Yakunlash CTA and the near-destination `departureConfirm` card (TripView.swift:149-158) — and that existing card is a bare Ha/Yo'q prompt carrying no fare numbers, so it is not a receipt.

#### Android today

Android is the newer contract on every point checked. `FareGpsBatchRequest` (domain/model/fare/FareGpsBatchRequest.kt:5-11) carries optional `waiting_time` / `waiting_time_ontheway` Longs alongside `points`; `GpsBatchUploader.pendingTimers()` (common/fare/GpsBatchUploader.kt:391-397) attaches each only when it differs from `lastSentWaitMs`/`lastSentOnWayMs`, and `uploadBatch.markTimersSent()` (kt:406-415) advances that cache ONLY on a successful send. Critically, `drainOnce()` (kt:331-359) sends a POINTS-LESS batch when the buffer is empty but a timer changed, because a parked driver emits no GPS. `RequestOrderFinish` (domain/model/requests/RequestOrderFinish.kt:10-19) has both wait fields, and the finish path computes the split at `MapFragment.kt:2849-2858` (pickup = `waitedTimeUntilGone.coerceAtMost(currentWaitTimeInMillis)` when `state >= ORDER_STATE_CHANGED_GONE`, else the whole wait; on-way = the remainder floored at 0), sending both in `RequestOrderFinish` at :2859-2871. `FareResponse.kt:16-31` + `FareLive.kt:18-37` parse `live`, `breakdown`, `price_breakdown` and `events`, with every money field a Double because a Long threw `NumberFormatException` on a live `"waiting_cost": 2613.33`. `MapFragment.applyServerFare` (MapFragment.kt:240-253) prefers `live` over the legacy fields, sets `serverPriceIncludesExtras = fare.live?.price != null`, and derives `serverWaitCost` from `live.waitingCost` or `breakdown` `wait_cost + on_way_cost + traffic_cost`. Settlement is server-first as of 2026-07-20 (`MapFragment.kt:2711-2724`): taximeter arm = `serverPriceIncludesExtras ? roundPrice(sp) : roundPrice(sp + additionalPrice + totalServicePrice)`, local meter only as the offline fallback, while `agreedTotal = roundPrice(order.price + waitLine)` adds NO services. The judge itself is unchanged (final point only, 300 m, 1000 m). Finish is gated by a pre-settlement dialog with `mcvCancel`/`mcvSubmit` (MapFragment.kt:2842-2846), preceded by a `/user/me` re-pull that replaces `order` and re-sums `totalServicePrice` (MapFragment.kt:2529-2572) while the fare GET runs in parallel and never blocks. `updateAgreedSurchargeHint()` (MapFragment.kt:3375-3389) flips the footnote to the surcharge + kept-projection once waiting bills.

#### 4.1 `[P0]` Send cumulative waiting timers in every order-gps/batch body

**What to build**

Add two optional top-level Int64 fields (milliseconds) to the batch body in `sendREST` (Shared/GpsBatchUploader.swift:371-383), matching Android `FareGpsBatchRequest`: `waiting_time` = cumulative PICKUP wait and `waiting_time_ontheway` = cumulative ON-ROUTE wait. Read them straight off `LocationTracker.shared` — it owns both as `@Published private(set) var waitedTimeMs` / `waitedTimeUntilGoneMs` (LocationTracker.swift:43-49), so the uploader needs no injection from TripMVVM: pickup = `waitedTimeUntilGoneMs`, on-route = `max(0, waitedTimeMs - waitedTimeUntilGoneMs)`. Both are CUMULATIVE totals, not deltas. Keep a `lastSentWaitMs` / `lastSentOnWayMs` cache, omit a key entirely when unchanged (never send `null`), and advance the cache ONLY after a 2xx — Android does exactly this in `pendingTimers()` + `markTimersSent()` or the server permanently misses an increment. SECOND HALF, which the original claim omitted and which makes the feature actually work: `flush()` currently bails at `if points.isEmpty { return }` (GpsBatchUploader.swift:312). Android's `drainOnce()` (kt:331-359) instead POSTs a POINTS-LESS batch carrying just the timers when the buffer is empty but a timer changed — a stationary driver produces no GPS fixes, which is precisely when the waiting bill runs. Without that branch the timers would only ride along with movement and a parked wait would still never reach the server. Latch the uploader off on a terminal failure of the timers-only send too, so a ticking timer can't hammer every 15 s.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/domain/model/fare/FareGpsBatchRequest.kt:5-11; app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/fare/GpsBatchUploader.kt:134-137, 331-359, 391-397, 399-415

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/GpsBatchUploader.swift (sendREST, flush, plus a lastSentWaiting cache), MehrgoDriver/Shared/Models/GpsBatch.swift (GpsBatchRequest)

> **Gotchas.** iOS models a socket batch envelope (`GpsBatchSocketEnvelope`, GpsBatch.swift:39-53) but the uploader never uses it — only the REST path needs changing; if the socket channel is ever enabled it needs the same fields or the cache will mark timers sent that never left. Do not roll the last-sent cache forward inside the 403/404 retry branch (GpsBatchUploader.swift:327-353) — those points are replayed with the same cpid and must replay their timers too. Android learned that money fields come back fractional; keep the outbound values integral ms but never assume the ack echoes integers.

**Acceptance** — Park the car with the wait meter running and no GPS movement: Charles/proxy shows a `order-gps/batch` POST with `points: []` and a rising `waiting_time`. Move again and confirm the key disappears from bodies where the value did not change. Force a 500 on one send and confirm the next send re-attaches the same (not a later) cumulative value.

#### 4.4 `[P0]` Send the pickup/on-route wait split to order/complete

**What to build**

`submit()` (TripMVVM.swift:689-697) sends `"waiting_time": totalWaitingMs` — the WHOLE wait — and no `waiting_time_ontheway`. Android splits it identically at both finish sites: `waiting_time` = pickup wait only = `min(waitedTimeUntilGoneMs, waitedTimeMs)` when the order has reached state 9 (changedGone), otherwise the whole wait (pre-Go the entire wait IS pickup wait); `waiting_time_ontheway` = `max(0, waitedTimeMs - pickupWaitMs)`. Both are millisecond STRINGS in the body. Send both keys. Note iOS already has the right raw inputs on `ticker` (`waitedTimeMs`, `waitedTimeUntilGoneMs`) and the comment at TripMVVM.swift:668-673 already explains the subset relationship — only the body construction changes.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/domain/model/requests/RequestOrderFinish.kt:8-19; app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/maps/yandex_map/MapFragment.kt:2846-2871

Android's `RequestOrderFinish` defaults `waiting_time_ontheway = "0"` and the backend treats a missing value as 0, so a partial port that sends only the shrunken pickup value SILENTLY DROPS the on-route wait entirely — ship both keys in the same commit.
