### 5. AUTH — SMS/Telegram OTP channel choice across all auth screens

**Gaps:** 5 total — P0: 1 · P1: 4 · P2: 0

#### iOS today

VERIFIED ABSENT: a repo-wide case-insensitive grep for `telegram|t\.me|channel` across the whole iOS repo returns exactly 4 hits, all false positives (`toast.message` in View+Modifiers.swift:314, `event.message` in NotificationsMVVM.swift:62-63, `NSMergeByPropertyObjectTrumpMergePolicy` in CoreDataStack.swift:37). There is no `TelegramAuthStatus` type, no `telegram-auth/status` call, no channel constant, no bot URL anywhere. The five auth surfaces exist and are SMS-only exactly as claimed: `LoginView`/`LoginMVVM` (password `user/login` at LoginMVVM.swift:65, no OTP — correctly out of scope), `SignUpMVVM.submit()` at :70-102 (`guard canSubmit else { return }` at :71, POSTs `user/register` at :86 with only phone/first_name/father_name/last_name/password/password_repeat), `PasswordRecoveryMVVM.submit()` at :51-72 (`user/recover` at :59, body is literally `["phone": phone.asServerPhoneUZ]` at :54), `SignUpVerifyMVVM` and `PasswordVerifyMVVM` (both `remaining = Constants.waitTimeVerifyCode` = 60, both `let authKey: String` at line :24, both discard the resend response). `Constants.swift` has only `phoneNumberSize = 13` (:46), `verificationCodeSize = 4` (:47), `waitTimeVerifyCode = 60` (:51) — no channel or bot keys. Both verify views hardcode SMS wording via the `verify_code_body` key (uz.lproj/Localizable.strings:39). THREE THINGS THE REPORT MISSED, all of which add real work: (a) `SecondaryButton` (Utilities/SecondaryButton.swift) accepts only `title`/`systemImage`/`isEnabled`/`action` — it has NO `isLoading` and NO asset-image support, so it must be extended or a bespoke button written; (b) `Assets.xcassets/Images` contains only `logo`, `logoIcon`, `logoSplash` — there is NO Telegram glyph asset, and no SF Symbol exists for Telegram, so a vector asset must be added; (c) iOS ships only three locales (en/ru/uz) against Android's four (uz/kk/ky/ru), and iOS's body key is `verify_code_body` where Android's is `verification_code_subtitle` — key names must not be blind-copied. Nothing in this area is done better on iOS than Android.

#### Android today

Android has the full feature, verified in source. `Constants.kt` defines `CHANNEL_SMS = "sms"` (:328), `CHANNEL_TELEGRAM = "telegram"` (:329), `TELEGRAM_RESEND_WAITING_TIME = 20` (:336) alongside `WAIT_TIME_VERIFY_CODE = 60` (:294), and a per-brand `TELEGRAM_BOT_USERNAME = "mehrgo_bot"` (:45) with all 14 other brand blocks commented out as `""` (:70-278) — empty means the brand has no bot and every bot-open call no-ops. `domain/model/TelegramAuthStatus.kt` maps `enabled`/`linked`/`bot_url` (all with defaults) and its KDoc explicitly warns the endpoint is "not deployed on every backend yet — callers must tolerate a 404/failure". `ApiService.kt:90` declares `@GET("telegram-auth/status")`, plumbed through `TelegramAuthStatusUC`/`AuthRepository`/`AuthRepositoryImpl`. `SignUpFragment` and `PasswordRecoveryFragment` each run two CTAs into one `submit(channel)` (SignUpFragment.kt:151/154, PasswordRecoveryFragment.kt:133/136), gate Telegram on `requestTelegramCode(phone, allowBotOpen)` (SignUpFragment.kt:247-283) with the linked/not-linked/threw branches, fail open in `applyTelegramAvailability()` via `telegramStatus?.enabled != false` (:349-354), probe with an empty phone in `onViewCreated` (:78) and refetch at `PHONE_NUMBER_SIZE - 1` digits (:105-107) deduped by `telegramStatusPhone` (:336), and resume-handle `pendingTelegramLink` in `onResume` gated on the form still being valid (:135-146). Both verify fragments take `channel` as a nav arg, set `waitTime = TELEGRAM_RESEND_WAITING_TIME` when telegram (SignUpVerifyFragment.kt:64-66), re-theme bidirectionally in `applyChannelUi()` (:202-237), show `tvOpenBot` only for telegram (:221), and route both resend and switch through `resendVia(targetChannel)` (:251-307) which disables both buttons while in flight, hides the switch chip for the new cooldown, and — critically — reassigns the rotated key at `authKey = it.data?.data?.authKey!!` (SignUpVerifyFragment.kt:266, PasswordRecoveryVerifyFragment.kt:271). `SignUpResendCode.kt` and `PasswordRecoveryResendCode.kt` both carry `@SerializedName("auth_key")`. All nine user-visible strings exist at res/values/strings.xml:493-500.

#### 5.1 `[P0]` Adopt the rotated auth_key returned by resend (already broken today; channel switching makes it unavoidable)

**What to build**

CONFIRMED — and the report understated it: this is a live lockout TODAY, not only once channel switching lands. `SignUpVerifyMVVM.swift:24` and `PasswordVerifyMVVM.swift:24` both declare `let authKey: String`, and both resend paths throw the response away with `let _: BaseResponse<SignUp> = try await …` (SignUpVerifyMVVM.swift:150, PasswordVerifyMVVM.swift:134). The backend rotates `auth_key` on `user/refresh` and `user/refresh?recover=1` — Android models both responses with `@SerializedName("auth_key")` (`SignUpResendCode.kt:6`, `PasswordRecoveryResendCode.kt:6`) and force-unwraps the reassignment on every resend (`authKey = it.data?.data?.authKey!!`), so the field is always present in practice. On iOS, any driver who taps 'resend' is left holding a stale key and the subsequent `user/confirm` / `user/change-password` fails — stranding them in sign-up or password recovery with no path forward but killing the app and restarting the flow. Fix: change to `private(set) var authKey: String` in both MVVMs, decode the resend response instead of discarding it, and assign before restarting the countdown. Type mapping is asymmetric and easy to get wrong: the sign-up case decodes `BaseResponse<SignUp>` and reads `res.data?.authKey`; the recovery case decodes `BaseResponse<PasswordRecovery>` whose CodingKeys map `authKeyVerify = "auth_key"` (SharedModels.swift:991-1003, where an in-file comment already documents a prior bug from getting this exact mapping wrong), so read `res.data?.authKeyVerify` there. Assign defensively — `if let k = res.data?.authKey { authKey = k }` — rather than mirroring Android's `!!`.

**Android reference** — SignUpVerifyFragment.kt:266 (`authKey = it.data?.data?.authKey!!` inside resendVia); PasswordRecoveryVerifyFragment.kt:271 (same); domain/model/SignUpResendCode.kt:6 and domain/model/PasswordRecoveryResendCode.kt:6 (both `@SerializedName("auth_key")`)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MVVM/SignUpVerifyMVVM.swift:24 (`let authKey`) and :144-165 (resend, discard at :150); MehrgoDriver/MVVM/PasswordVerifyMVVM.swift:24 and :129-150 (discard at :134)

> **Gotchas.** The two flows use DIFFERENT property names for the same wire field: `SignUp.authKey` vs `PasswordRecovery.authKeyVerify` (both from JSON `auth_key`). SharedModels.swift:994-999 carries a comment about a previous bug where this mapping was wrong and the recovery flow silently stalled after a 200 — read that before touching it. Do not force-unwrap; a nil should leave the old key in place rather than crash. Assign the new key BEFORE restarting the countdown so a fast second resend uses the fresh key. Fix this before the channel-switch work lands: switching channels goes through the same resend endpoint, so a stale key there turns every switch into a guaranteed dead end.

**Acceptance** — On sign-up, wait out the countdown, tap resend, then enter the code from the SECOND message — `user/confirm` succeeds. Repeat on password recovery with `user/change-password`. Instrument or proxy the resend response and confirm the returned `auth_key` differs from the original and that the next confirm request carries the new one.

#### 5.2 `[P1]` Add the Telegram-OTP data layer: channel constants, TelegramAuthStatus model, status probe

**What to build**

CONFIRMED ABSENT — no channel/telegram symbol exists anywhere in the iOS repo. 1) In `Shared/Constants.swift`, alongside the existing `phoneNumberSize` (:46), `verificationCodeSize` (:47) and `waitTimeVerifyCode = 60` (:51), add `static let channelSms: String = "sms"`, `static let channelTelegram: String = "telegram"`, `static let telegramBotUsername: String = "mehrgo_bot"` (per-brand; empty string means the brand has no bot and every bot-open call must no-op), `static let telegramResendWaitingTime: Int = 20`. Keep `waitTimeVerifyCode = 60` for SMS. 2) In `Shared/Models/SharedModels.swift` add `struct TelegramAuthStatus: Codable { let enabled: Bool?; let linked: Bool?; let botUrl: String?; enum CodingKeys: String, CodingKey { case enabled, linked, botUrl = "bot_url" } }`. Use OPTIONALS deliberately — Android's model uses non-optional `Boolean = false` defaults, which is strictly worse: a payload missing `enabled` decodes to `false` there. Swift optionals let `enabled == nil` mean 'unknown' and stay visible. 3) Add one shared fetch helper (`TelegramAuthService.status(phone:)`) calling `DynamicClient.shared.request(path: "telegram-auth/status", method: .GET, query: [URLQueryItem(name: "phone", value: phone.asServerPhoneUZ)], type: BaseResponse<TelegramAuthStatus>.self)` — that signature is confirmed against the existing call sites (LoginMVVM.swift:64-70, PasswordVerifyMVVM.swift:134-142 which already uses the `query:` parameter). No auth needed; a stray Bearer header is harmless. 4) Every caller must FAIL OPEN: any thrown error (404, timeout, decode) leaves the last known status untouched and keeps the Telegram UI visible, because `channel=telegram` is SMS-safe server-side. NOTE: `RootMVVM.authFlow401Paths` (:119-122) whitelists auth endpoints from the global 401-revocation handler — `telegram-auth/status` is a GET that should never 401, but if it can, add it to that set or a probe failure will sign the driver out.

> **Gotchas.** The status endpoint is NOT deployed on every backend — Android's own model KDoc says so. Treat 404 as normal, never as an error banner. Do not copy Android's non-optional `Boolean = false` model shape: it conflates 'absent' with 'disabled' and would hide the button on a partial payload. Do not gate the Telegram button on a successful probe — the probe is an optimization, not a precondition.

**Acceptance** — With the device pointed at a backend where `telegram-auth/status` 404s, the sign-up screen still renders the Telegram CTA and tapping it still issues a code (server SMS-falls-back). With a backend returning `{"enabled":false}`, the CTA and its divider are hidden. No sign-out occurs on a probe failure.

Branch (b) must NOT fire the register call before the number is linked or the backend SMS-falls-back and the driver gets a surprise SMS — the whole point of the linked-first gate. The scenePhase hook fires on every foreground including permission dialogs and control-centre pulls, so the `pendingTelegramLink` flag must be cleared BEFORE the async call or a double-resume double-fires the register. Guard on `canSubmit` — the user may have edited the form while away.

**Acceptance** — On a phone not yet linked to the bot: tapping Telegram opens the bot and sends NO SMS; returning to the app after pressing the bot's Share-contact auto-issues the code via Telegram with no second tap and no bot re-open. On an already-linked phone: one tap goes straight to the verify screen. With the status endpoint 404ing: one tap still reaches the verify screen (server falls back to SMS) and the bot never opens. The SMS CTA produces a `user/register` body identical to today plus `channel=sms`.

Route both buttons through one `resendVia(_ target: String)`: on success set `channel = target`, set `remaining` to that channel's cooldown, and RESTART the countdown; on failure re-enable both. While a resend is in flight disable BOTH buttons. Localization: add the nine new keys to all THREE lproj files (en/ru/uz) — iOS has no kk/ky, and note iOS's body key is `verify_code_body` where Android's is `verification_code_subtitle`, so do not blind-copy key names.

Render the switch button only inside the existing `remaining == 0` else-branch — a visible switch chip during cooldown lets the driver burn the server-side rate limit. Disable BOTH resend and switch while either is in flight or a double tap races two codes through two channels. The chip is not present or tappable while the countdown runs, and a double tap on resend issues exactly one request.

### 6. Balance / Paylov cards / withdrawal / top-up (driver "Hisob")

**Gaps:** 7 total — P0: 5 · P1: 1 · P2: 1

#### iOS today

iOS has a working balance + top-up screen only. `MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Settings/BalanceView.swift` renders a gradient hero card off `UserManager.shared.user?.balance` with a `.refreshable` + toolbar refresh button, both calling `BalanceMVVM.refreshBalance()` (GET `user/me` → `UserManager.save`, BalanceMVVM.swift:56-70), and a top-up `.sheet` with a space-grouped amount field (`amountBinding`/`grouped`, BalanceView.swift:22-42), 50k/100k/200k/500k quick capsules, and Click/PayMe tiles opening `SFSafariViewController` via `IdentifiableURL`. Top-up URL construction is confirmed byte-identical to Android (BalanceMVVM.swift:32-53; PayMe correctly ×100 for tiyin against `payme.uz/fallback/merchant/`), so top-up is at parity and needs no work. `SettingsView.walletCard` (SettingsView.swift:145) links Balance / Earnings / Subscriptions. I attempted to refute every gap: a repo-wide case-insensitive grep for `paylov|withdraw|user-card|expireDate|cardId|card_id` across all 128 Swift files returns ZERO hits, and dumping every `path:` literal in the project yields 42 endpoints — none is a Paylov route, `user-card/*`, `withdrawal/*`, or `balance/index`. The only near-miss is `"payment_card" = "Karta"` in uz.lproj, which is the order payment-method label, not a saved card. `EarningsMVVM` calls only `driver-earnings/summary`; `HistoryView` is order history. All 7 gaps therefore survive verification. Two infrastructure facts sharpen the work: `JSONDecoder` is used with NO `keyDecodingStrategy` anywhere in the repo, so every snake_case DTO needs explicit `CodingKeys` (there is no global conversion to "opt out of"); and `DynamicClient.request` already supports `query:`, `formUrlEncoded:` and `.DELETE`, so no client plumbing beyond a new base case is required.

#### Android today

Android has the complete feature, verified end to end. `data/remote/ApiService.kt:409-496` declares 12 Paylov endpoints via the documented `../paylov/` RFC 3986 dot-segment trick against the `/api/v1/` base, with `balance/index` deliberately left on v1. DTOs live in `domain/model/paylov/` (9 files: PaylovCard, CardsResponse, CardCreateResult, WithdrawalInfo, WithdrawalRequest, WithdrawalList, BalanceHistory, BalanceHistoryItem, PaymentCreateResult) and use `@SerializedName` for snake_case. Use cases are in `domain/use_case/paylov/` (8 `*UC` classes). UI is `presentation/main/ui/my_payment/MyPaymentFragment.kt` (hero, cards section, withdraw sheet, pending card), plus separate navigated screens `add_card/AddCardFragment.kt`, `add_card_confirm/AddCardConfirmFragment.kt`, and `paylov_history/PaylovHistoryFragment.kt`. The withdraw limit formula is at MyPaymentFragment.kt:475-510 and the "0 = no limit" semantics are explicit in-code. `Constants.kt:491-492` defines `FCM_TYPE_PAYLOV_WITHDRAWAL = "paylov_withdrawal"` with a comment recording the payload shape `{ type, request_id, status }` — no `key` field.

#### 6.1 `[P0]` Add a `paylov` ApiBase — iOS buildURL cannot use Android's `../paylov/` trick

**What to build**

VERIFIED: `enum ApiBase` at DynamicClient.swift:21 is exactly `case main, route`, and `buildURL` at DynamicClient.swift:301 is plain concatenation — `URLComponents(string: base.url + path)` — which does not resolve dot-segments. Copying Android's `"../paylov/user-card/create"` would ship a literal `/api/v1/../paylov/...` path (and even where a Foundation version might normalize it, that behavior is unspecified across OS versions — do not depend on it). Add `case paylov` to ApiBase returning a new `Constants.paylovBaseURL: String = "https://\(BASE_URL)/api/paylov/"`, placed next to `baseURL` at Constants.swift:24 (which is `"https://\(BASE_URL)/api/v1/"`). Every Paylov call then passes `base: .paylov` with a clean path (`user-card/create`, `withdrawal/info`, …). `balance/index` is the exception and stays on the default `.main` base — Android documents this at ApiService.kt:487. No other client plumbing is needed: `DynamicClient.request` (DynamicClient.swift:135-145) already exposes `query:`, `formUrlEncoded:` and supports `.DELETE`, and `formEncode` (DynamicClient.swift:328-334) is already documented as matching OkHttp's FormBody output, so `formUrlEncoded: true` on the @FormUrlEncoded POSTs is correct.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/data/remote/ApiService.kt:409-414 (dot-segment comment), :487-496 (balance/index stays on v1)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/DynamicClient.swift:21 (enum ApiBase), MehrgoDriver/Shared/Constants.swift:24 (add paylovBaseURL next to baseURL)

> **Gotchas.** Do not add a trailing-slash mismatch: `baseURL` ends with `/` and paths are passed without a leading `/`, so `paylovBaseURL` MUST also end with `/`. Constants also has a DEV/PROD switch (`BASE_URL = BASE_PROD`, isUsingDevURL at :17-21) — derive paylovBaseURL from the same `BASE_URL` interpolation so it follows the env switch instead of hardcoding a host.

**Acceptance** — Log the resolved URL for `user-card/cards` on device and confirm it is exactly `https://<host>/api/paylov/user-card/cards` with no `..` and no `/v1/`, and that `balance/index` still resolves under `/api/v1/`.

#### 6.2 `[P0]` Saved Paylov cards: list, empty state, delete

**What to build**

Add a `Mening kartalarim` section under the balance hero in BalanceView. GET `user-card/cards` (base `.paylov`) → `BaseResponse<CardsResponse>`. Model `PaylovCard { cardId: String?, number: String?, balance: Int64?, bankName: String?, vendor: String? }` — these keys are genuinely camelCase on the wire, and since the repo sets NO `keyDecodingStrategy` anywhere, they decode with no CodingKeys at all (note: this also means every snake_case DTO in gaps 4-6 needs EXPLICIT CodingKeys — there is no global conversion). Every field must be Optional. Row shows `number` as title, `bankName ?? vendor` as subtitle (hide when both nil), trailing delete button. Delete = DELETE `user-card/delete` with cardId as a QUERY item (`query: [URLQueryItem(name: "cardId", value: id)]`), NOT a body, behind a confirmation alert titled `delete_card_q` with the card number as message, then reload. Empty state = `no_cards_yet` ("Hali karta qo'shilmagan") plus the Add-card row. Card loading must be error-tolerant: on failure render the empty state, never a blocking error — Android deliberately swallows this at MyPaymentFragment.kt:283 (`is Resource.Error -> renderCards()`), so an older backend returning 404 leaves the section quiet.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/data/remote/ApiService.kt:435-441 (cards + delete-by-query); domain/model/paylov/PaylovCard.kt, CardsResponse.kt; presentation/main/ui/my_payment/MyPaymentFragment.kt:283 (error → renderCards)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/Models/PaylovModels.swift (new), MehrgoDriver/MVVM/BalanceMVVM.swift, MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Settings/BalanceView.swift

> **Gotchas.** CRITICAL, worse on iOS than Android: `CardsResponse.kt` carries an in-code comment that the endpoint returns EITHER `data.cards[]` OR a bare `data[]` array depending on backend build. Gson tolerates the mismatch by yielding null; Swift's Decodable will THROW and the whole response fails to decode. Decode `CardsResponse` with a custom `init(from:)` that tries the keyed `cards` container first and falls back to an unkeyed array of PaylovCard, or the card list will be permanently empty against one of the two backend builds. Also: `PaylovCard.balance` is in TIYIN (Android annotates it and never displays it) — do not render it as so'm.

**Acceptance** — With at least one saved card the list renders number + bank; deleting prompts, removes the row after reload, and the DELETE goes out with `?cardId=` in the query string and an empty body. Point the app at a backend without the route and confirm the section shows the empty state with no error dialog.

#### 6.3 `[P0]` Add-card: 2-step flow (PAN + expiry → SMS OTP confirm) as two navigated screens

**What to build**

Two pushed screens, not a sheet — Android moved off the sheet deliberately and device-verified it (separate `add_card/` and `add_card_confirm/` packages). Step 1 (AddCardView): masked PAN field `____ ____ ____ ____` and expiry `__/__` typed as MM/YY; CTA enabled only when PAN digits == 16 AND expiry digits == 4; validate month is 1...12 (Android parses `expiry.substring(0,2)` and range-checks it, AddCardFragment.kt:62). POST `user-card/create` form-encoded with `cardNumber` = 16 raw digits and `expireDate` = YYMM — Android builds it as `expiry.substring(2) + expiry.substring(0,2)` (AddCardFragment.kt:68), i.e. MM/YY 12/27 → "2712". Swift equivalent: `expiry.suffix(2) + expiry.prefix(2)` on the 4 raw digits. `phoneNumber` is optional and Android omits it. Response `CardCreateResult { cid: String?, otpSentPhone: String? }` (both camelCase, no CodingKeys needed) → push step 2 with cid, the raw cardNumber and the converted expireDate, plus otpSentPhone. Step 2 (AddCardConfirmView): card-icon chip, `card_otp_sent` copy ("Karta egasining raqamiga tasdiqlash kodi yuborildi"), `otpSentPhone` when present, a centered numeric code field with submit enabled at >= 4 characters (AddCardConfirmFragment.kt:72), and a 60s resend countdown. Confirm = POST `user-card/confirm` form-encoded with `cardId` (= the cid) and `otp`; `cardName`/`pinfl` exist in the API but Android sends neither. Success → toast `card_added`, pop straight back to the balance screen, which reloads cards.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/data/remote/ApiService.kt:416-433; presentation/main/ui/add_card/AddCardFragment.kt:62-85 (month check + YYMM); presentation/main/ui/add_card_confirm/AddCardConfirmFragment.kt:72 (>=4 gate), :103-115 (resend overwrites cardId)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Settings/AddCardView.swift (new), MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Settings/AddCardConfirmView.swift (new), MehrgoDriver/MVVM/AddCardMVVM.swift (new), MehrgoDriver/Shared/Models/PaylovModels.swift

> **Gotchas.** (1) CORRECTION to the original plan: do NOT reuse `Utilities/OTPCodeField.swift` here. It takes a FIXED `length: Int` and fires `onComplete` at that exact length (OTPCodeField.swift:22-27), whereas Android uses a free-length EditText gated at `>= 4` because the Paylov OTP length is not pinned by the backend. Hardcoding 4 boxes would silently truncate a 5- or 6-digit Paylov code. Use a plain centered numeric TextField with a `>= 4` enable gate. (2) Resend re-calls `user-card/create` with the SAME cardNumber/expireDate and the server returns a BRAND NEW cid — you must overwrite the stored cardId and clear the code field (Android does exactly this at AddCardConfirmFragment.kt:111-115, and bails if cid comes back null), or confirm will submit against a dead cid and fail.

**Acceptance** — On device, add a real card: confirm the outgoing form body has `expireDate=2712` for an entered 12/27 (not 1227), that a 6-digit OTP is accepted, that tapping resend changes the cardId used by the subsequent confirm, and that success pops directly to Balance with the new card listed.

**What to build**

GET `withdrawal/info` (base `.paylov`) → `WithdrawalInfo`. All keys are snake_case and REQUIRE explicit CodingKeys in Swift (no global strategy is set): `balance`, `withdrawable_balance`, `bonus_left`, `min_balance_left`, `min_amount`, `max_amount`, `daily_limit`, `today_withdrawn`, `pending_request` (nested `WithdrawalRequest`, itself snake_case). All Int64? / Optional. Under the hero show `withdrawable_amount` ("Chiqarish mumkin: %@ so'm", from `withdrawable_balance`) — hidden until the call answers — plus a note block joining with newlines, when > 0: `bonus_not_withdrawable` ("Shundan %@ so'm — bonus, kartaga chiqarilmaydi") and `min_balance_note` ("Balansda kamida %@ so'm qolishi kerak"). The server's formula is withdrawable = balance − bonus_left − min_balance_left (documented in WithdrawalInfo.kt); DISPLAY the server value, never recompute it. Add a `Pul chiqarish` secondary CTA next to the existing top-up PrimaryButton in BalanceView. When `pending_request != nil`: render a pending card showing "<amount> so'm · <card_number>" with a red-soft Cancel pill (no confirm dialog — cancelling is reversible), dim the withdraw CTA to 0.6 opacity, and make tapping it show an info alert with the pending details instead of opening the form — the backend allows ONE active request per driver. Cancel = POST `withdrawal/cancel` form-encoded with `id` (Int, not String) → toast `withdrawal_cancelled` → reload info.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/data/remote/ApiService.kt:443-444 (info), :461-464 (cancel takes @Field id: Int); domain/model/paylov/WithdrawalInfo.kt (incl. the withdrawable formula comment), WithdrawalRequest.kt

> **Gotchas.** Every numeric field must be Optional with a `?? 0` at the use site — Swift Decodable throws on a missing non-optional, and older backends omit several of these. Do not client-side compute withdrawable from balance − bonus − minLeft as a 'sanity check' and show the larger of the two; if the server and client disagree the server rejects the request and the driver sees an unexplained failure. `withdrawal/cancel` takes `id` as Int while `withdrawal/create` takes `card_id` as String — do not unify the types.

**Acceptance** — With a pending request on the account, the pending card renders with amount + masked card number, the withdraw CTA is dimmed and opens an info alert rather than the form, and Cancel removes it and re-enables the CTA after reload. With no pending request the withdrawable line and both notes match the values returned by `withdrawal/info`.

#### 6.5 `[P0]` Withdraw request sheet: card picker + amount with the exact limits formula

**What to build**

A sheet opened by the withdraw CTA. Guards first: if `pending_request != nil` show the pending info alert and return; if the card list is empty show the `no_cards_yet` toast and return. Contents: the `withdrawable_amount` line; a card picker reusing the card rows with a checkmark and the FIRST CARD PRESELECTED (Android: `var selectedCardId = cards.firstOrNull()?.cardId`); an amount field with live space-grouping (reuse the existing `BalanceView.amountBinding` / `Self.grouped` at BalanceView.swift:22-42, parsing from `digitsOnly`); and a limits line joining with newlines, when > 0: `min_withdraw_amount` ("Eng kam summa: %@ so'm"), `daily_limit_today` ("Kunlik limit: %@ so'm (bugun: %@)") and `min_balance_note`. The submit gate must be the exact Android formula, verified verbatim at MyPaymentFragment.kt:475-510: dailyRemainder = daily_limit > 0 ? max(0, daily_limit − today_withdrawn) : Int64.max; effectiveMax = min(withdrawable_balance, max_amount > 0 ? max_amount : Int64.max, dailyRemainder); enabled = amount > 0 && amount <= effectiveMax && (min_amount <= 0 || amount >= min_amount) && selectedCardId != nil. A limit value of 0 means NO LIMIT, not zero allowed. Submit = POST `withdrawal/create` form-encoded with `card_id` (String, snake_case), `amount` (Int64, so'm not tiyin), optional `note`; in-button spinner while in flight; on success dismiss, toast `withdraw_request_sent`, reload cards + info.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/main/ui/my_payment/MyPaymentFragment.kt:465-510 (effectiveMax + updateSubmit + first-card preselect); data/remote/ApiService.kt:446-452 (create: card_id String, amount Long, note optional)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Settings/BalanceView.swift (withdraw sheet), MehrgoDriver/MVVM/BalanceMVVM.swift

> **Gotchas.** The `0 = no limit` sentinel is the single highest-risk detail here: treating `max_amount == 0` or `daily_limit == 0` as a real ceiling makes the submit button permanently disabled for every driver on a backend that does not set limits. Use Int64.max as the neutral element exactly as Android does, and note `daily_limit − today_withdrawn` is clamped at 0 (coerceAtLeast) so an over-withdrawn day yields 0, not a negative max. `amount` is in SO'M here, unlike `PaylovCard.balance` which is tiyin — do not multiply by 100. Presenting this sheet from BalanceView while the top-up sheet exists risks the sibling-`.sheet` conflict already documented at BalanceView.swift:189-194; attach it to a distinct presenter or use `.sheet(item:)`.

**Acceptance** — On an account with daily_limit=0 and max_amount=0 the button enables for any amount within withdrawable; with daily_limit set and a prior same-day withdrawal, entering more than the remainder keeps it disabled; submitting sends `card_id`/`amount`/`note` form-encoded and the new request then appears as the pending card after reload.

#### 6.6 `[P1]` Paylov history screen: two tabs (withdrawal requests / balance ledger), paged 20

**What to build**

One pushed screen with a segmented control, reached from two rows on the balance screen (`withdrawal_history` "So'rovlar tarixi" and `balance_history` "Balans tarixi") and opened on the matching tab. Requests tab: GET `withdrawal/index` (base `.paylov`) with `page` + `per-page` (note the HYPHEN — it is not a Swift-legal label, so build it as a URLQueryItem name) = 20 → `WithdrawalList { items, total, page, per_page }` of `WithdrawalRequest { id, card_id, card_number, amount, approved_amount, status, status_name, note, admin_note, paylov_transaction_id, created_at, processed_at }` — snake_case, needs CodingKeys. Row = colored status pill using the server's `status_name` string with status 0 pending amber+clock / 1 approved green+check / 2 rejected red+x / 3 driver-cancelled gray+minus (the mapping is documented in WithdrawalRequest.kt); when status == 1 and approved_amount differs from amount render "50 000" struck-through gray → "40 000 so'm" green; amount grays out for status 3; card_number chunked in 4s ("860013******4751" → "8600 13** **** 4751"); `admin_note` beats `note`, shown in a red-soft box with a warning glyph when rejected, otherwise a gray box with a chat glyph. Balance tab: GET `balance/index` on the `.main` (v1) base with `page`, `per-page`, optional `type` → `BalanceHistory { items, total, total_income, total_expense, page, per_page }` of `BalanceHistoryItem { id, type, type_name, value, total_after, reason, reason_name, info, created_at, datetime }`. Row = `reason_name` + HH:mm on line one, `remaining_after` ("Qoldiq: %@ so'm", from `total_after`) + a ±value (green when type == 1, red otherwise) on line two. Above the list, two equal-width total pills (income green-soft, expense red-soft); tapping one applies the server `type` filter (1 income / 2 expense), tapping again clears it. Both tabs: infinite scroll (load next page when within 4 rows of the end), stop when accumulated count >= total or a page returns empty, and day-section headers using the existing orders-history rule — "Bugun" for today, otherwise a localized "d MMMM" from `LanguageManager.shared.current`.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/data/remote/ApiService.kt:454-459 (withdrawal/index, per-page), :487-496 (balance/index on v1); domain/model/paylov/WithdrawalList.kt, WithdrawalRequest.kt, BalanceHistory.kt, BalanceHistoryItem.kt; presentation/main/ui/paylov_history/PaylovHistoryFragment.kt

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Settings/PaylovHistoryView.swift (new), MehrgoDriver/MVVM/PaylovHistoryMVVM.swift (new), MehrgoDriver/Shared/Models/PaylovModels.swift

> **Gotchas.** The two tabs sit on DIFFERENT bases — `withdrawal/index` is `.paylov`, `balance/index` is `.main`. Putting balance/index on the paylov base yields a 404 and an empty ledger that looks like 'the driver has no history'. `created_at` is an epoch Long on both DTOs while `BalanceHistoryItem` ALSO carries a preformatted `datetime` String — pick one and be consistent, and decode `created_at` as Int64 not Date (there is no dateDecodingStrategy set). `balance/index` additionally accepts `reason`, `from`, `to` params that Android does not send; ignore them for parity. `total` drives the stop condition — if the server omits it, fall back to stopping on an empty page or you will loop forever.

**Acceptance** — Both tabs load 20 rows, scroll to page 2/3 and stop cleanly at the end without duplicate rows; a rejected request shows the admin note in the red box; a partially-approved request shows the struck-through original next to the approved amount; tapping the income pill filters to type=1 and tapping again restores the full list.

**What to build**

VERIFIED: `PushNotificationCenter.routeTap` reads the discriminator as `let key: String = (userInfo["key"] as? String) ?? ""` and its `PushRoute` enum has exactly four cases (`home`, `earnings(balanceId:)`, `notification(id:)`, `verification`) — there is no paylov handling. The backend fires a push when an admin approves or rejects a withdrawal, and its payload has NO `key` field: Android records the shape as `{ type: "paylov_withdrawal", request_id, status "1"/"2" }` at Constants.kt:491. Change the key read to fall back to `type`: `(userInfo["key"] as? String) ?? (userInfo["type"] as? String) ?? ""`. The `?? type` fallback is order-sensitive: keep `key` first so no existing push (order offers, document decisions, bonus) changes behavior, since several of those payloads may also carry an unrelated `type` field. `PushRoute` is `Equatable`; adding an associated-value case is source-compatible but every exhaustive `switch` over PushRoute must gain the new arm or the build breaks.

### 7. Driver pre-shift verification (document checks + go-online gate)

**Gaps:** 5 total — P0: 0 · P1: 1 · P2: 4

#### iOS today

iOS has a genuinely faithful port, and I confirmed the claimed parity line-by-line rather than taking it on trust. `MehrgoDriver/Shared/Models/VerificationStatus.swift` mirrors Android's `domain/model/VerificationStatus.kt` exactly: same CodingKeys (`can_go_online`/`all_passed`/`checks`; per-check `key,title,message,applicable,ok,required,can_skip,video_url,action`), same formulas (`isBlocking = applicable && !ok && (required || !canSkip)` :76, `isActionableBlocking = isBlocking && !isPending` :78, `isSuggestion` :79, `hasPending` :44), and the same client-authoritative `canDriverGoOnline = !hasBlocking` (:48) that deliberately ignores the advisory server `can_go_online` — matching Kotlin :50-51. The pending carve-out is correctly ABSENT on both sides: a required doc with `action == "pending"` still blocks and renders only as an amber "under review" row in the Done section (VerificationView.swift:267, :283-301). Gate coverage really is complete: `grep "OnlineToggle("` returns nothing (`View/Main/Common/OnlineToggle.swift` is compiled but never instantiated — only pbxproj build refs and its own `struct OnlineToggle: View` declaration appear), so iOS has exactly one go-online path (MapHomeView slide → `MapMVVM.toggleOnline()` → `goOnline()`, MapMVVM.swift:154-190), whereas Android needs four copies of `checkAndStartWork()`. Ancillary plumbing is present and in places ahead of Android: single-flight `verifyLoadTask?.cancel()` (MapMVVM.swift:135) plus a second cancel inside the gate (:172) so a stale chip fetch can't overwrite the gate's fresh result; `freshConnection: true` fail-fast (:178); sheet-dismiss re-fetch (MapHomeView.swift:244); `verificationChangedSubject` wired into BOTH MapMVVM (:337) and VerificationMVVM's init (:41) so an already-open screen refreshes on a `DOCUMENT_*` push (PushNotificationCenter.swift:136, :165); explicit 409 soft-handling (`catch let ClientError.server(status, _, _) where status == 409`, VerificationMVVM.swift:99); and a progress title splitting actionable-vs-pending (VerificationView.swift:131-145) that Android's `updateProgress` still lacks. The remaining gaps are defensive/UX polish inside iOS's own single path, not missing features. Two mitigations the original report understated: `VerificationView` already has `.refreshable { await vm.load() }` (:68), so a retry path exists even in the bad empty state, and the screen is presented as a `.sheet`, so it is swipe-down dismissible.

#### Android today

Android is the source of truth for this feature. `app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/domain/model/VerificationStatus.kt` defines the model plus five computed properties, each defensively written as `checks.orEmpty().filterNotNull()...` (:27, :31, :35, :39, :44) with an explicit comment that Gson-via-Unsafe can leave both a null list AND a null ELEMENT inside it — that null-element guard is the exact hardening iOS lacks. `canDriverGoOnline = !hasBlocking` (:50-51) with a comment stating `can_go_online` is advisory because server enforcement is OFF. The gate itself is duplicated across four screens — `checkAndStartWork()` in `MapFragment.kt:1551` (called at :1075), `NotificationsFragment.kt:219` (:82), `MyOrdersFragment.kt:195` (:106), plus Settings — and every copy fails CLOSED on a nil payload: `if (status != null && status.canDriverGoOnline) startWork() else { resetStartJobSlider(); findNavController().navigate(R.id.action_global_to_verificationFragment) }` (MapFragment.kt:1569-1573; identically NotificationsFragment.kt:231-235), while genuine network errors fail open. `VerificationFragment.kt` handles the screen: single end-of-screen skip is a pure dismissal (`binding.tvSkipAll.setDebouncedClickListener { findNavController().navigateUp() }`, :91) shown only when `optional.isNotEmpty() && !hasBlocking` (:151); the empty view is bound to `visible.isEmpty()` (:153) and reachable only from `render()` on `Resource.Success` (:120-122), since `Resource.Error` toasts and never calls render (:125-128); uploads compress through `id.zelory.compressor` (`Compressor.compress(requireContext(), original)`, :361) and show a full-screen `showLoading(true)` (:280-283) across the entire compress+upload+reload cycle (:367, :369, :376, :392), with a catch-all that surfaces an error for corrupt/0-byte/HEIC inputs (:389-395).

<a id="gap-7-1"></a>

#### 7.1 `[P1]` Close the two fail-open decode holes in the iOS go-online gate

**What to build**

CONFIRMED — both holes exist and both let a blocked driver online where Android fails closed.

(1) `VerificationStatus.init(from:)` line 37 does `checks = (try? c.decode(Array<VerificationCheck>.self, forKey: .checks)) ?? []`. `VerificationCheck.init(from:)` line 64 starts with `try d.container(keyedBy: CodingKeys.self)`, which THROWS `DecodingError.valueNotFound` on a JSON `null` element — every other field in that initializer is `try?`-defaulted, so the element decode is otherwise total, but the container acquisition is not. One `null` inside `"checks": [null, {...}]` therefore fails the WHOLE array decode, the outer `try?` swallows it, `checks` becomes `[]`, `hasBlocking == false`, `canDriverGoOnline == true` — every blocking check silently vanishes. Android hit exactly this and fixed it by dropping only the bad element: `checks.orEmpty().filterNotNull()` in all five computed properties (VerificationStatus.kt:27,31,35,39,44) with the comment "Gson (via Unsafe) can also leave a null ELEMENT inside the list". Fix: decode per-element leniently — add `private struct Failable<T: Decodable>: Decodable { let value: T?; init(from d: Decoder) throws { value = try? T(from: d) } }` and use `checks = ((try? c.decode([Failable<VerificationCheck>].self, forKey: .checks)) ?? []).compactMap { $0.value }`. Keep the outer `try?`/`?? []` — a plain `[Failable<...>]` decode still throws when `checks` itself is not an array.

(2) `MapMVVM.goOnline()` line 182 is `if let v = res.data, !v.canDriverGoOnline { ... return }`. I verified `BaseResponse<T>` is `struct BaseResponse<T: Codable>: Codable { let data: T? }` (SharedModels.swift:36-38) and that `DynamicClient.request` (DynamicClient.swift:135-224) performs NO non-nil validation on `data` — it throws only on transport failure, non-2xx, or a decode error. So a 200 carrying `{}` or `{"data": null}` decodes cleanly to `data == nil`, the `if let` falls through, and execution continues into the FCM-token guard and `driver/start` unchecked. Android fails CLOSED on that exact case (MapFragment.kt:1569-1573). Fix: insert `guard let v = res.data else { state = .Neutral; presentVerification = true; return }` before the `canDriverGoOnline` test, and assign `lastVerification = v` from that binding (collapsing the duplicated `if let v = res.data` on line 181). Leave the `catch` fail-open on :188-190 untouched — that IS intended Android parity.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/domain/model/VerificationStatus.kt:24-27 (filterNotNull with the null-element comment), :31, :35, :39, :44; app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/maps/yandex_map/MapFragment.kt:1566-1573; app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/main/ui/notifications/NotificationsFragment.kt:228-235

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/Models/VerificationStatus.swift:33-38 (VerificationStatus.init(from:)), MehrgoDriver/MVVM/MapMVVM.swift:181-187

> **Gotchas.** Do NOT "harden" this by also requiring the server's `can_go_online` — it is advisory (enforcement is OFF server-side) and both platforms deliberately ignore it; requiring it would strand every driver whose checks all pass. Do not make `BaseResponse.data` non-optional globally: dozens of call sites depend on `T?` and several endpoints legitimately return no payload (`EmptyPayload`). Fix nil handling at this one call site only. Note `hasBlocking` is computed over `checks`, not `visibleChecks`, on both platforms — keep it that way; filtering to `applicable` inside the initializer would change gate semantics.

**Acceptance** — With a proxy/stub, return 200 `{"data":{"can_go_online":false,"all_passed":false,"checks":[null,{"key":"license","applicable":true,"ok":false,"required":true,"action":"upload_license"}]}}` and swipe the Boshlash slider → the slider resets and VerificationView presents; `driver/start` must NOT appear in the network log. Repeat with 200 `{"data":null}` and with 200 `{}` → same result. Then return a healthy all-`ok` payload and confirm the driver still goes online (no regression from over-tightening).

#### 7.2 `[P2]` Show blocking upload progress while the licence photo uploads

**What to build**

CONFIRMED. A repo-wide grep for `isUploading` returns exactly three hits: the declaration (VerificationMVVM.swift:32), the set/reset pair (:83, :106), and ONE consumer — `.disabled(vm.isUploading)` on the upload Button (VerificationView.swift:198). Because that button renders a custom `Label` over `.background(Color.brandRed)` with an explicit `.foregroundStyle(.white)`, `.disabled` produces zero visible change: no dimming, no spinner, identical pixels. There is also no fallback indicator — during the upload `vm.state` is still `.Positive`, and even the post-upload `await load()` (which sets `.Loading`) cannot surface the spinner branch because `if let status = vm.status, !status.visibleChecks.isEmpty` wins the branch on line 34 while the previous status is still populated. Net result on a slow cellular link: the picker dismisses, the screen sits visually frozen for 10-30s, and the driver re-taps or backs out. Android shows a full-screen `showLoading(true)` (progressBar visible, content hidden — VerificationFragment.kt:280-283) for the whole compress+upload+reload cycle, driven at :367/:369/:376/:392. Build: overlay the ScrollView with a translucent scrim + centered `ProgressView().tint(Color.brandOrange)` gated on `vm.isUploading`, add `.allowsHitTesting(!vm.isUploading)` to the content, and swap the button's `Label` for a `ProgressView().tint(.white)` while uploading. Also gate the trigger — change line 188 to `if !vm.isUploading { showPhotoSource = true }` so a second pick cannot start a parallel multipart POST.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/main/ui/verification/VerificationFragment.kt:280-283 (showLoading), :367, :369, :376, :392

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Verification/VerificationView.swift:32-92 (body, overlay) and :185-199 (requiredCard upload Button), MehrgoDriver/MVVM/VerificationMVVM.swift:81-108

> **Gotchas.** `isUploading` must stay reset on EVERY exit path, including the early `guard let data = image.jpegData(...) else { return }` on line 82 — that guard currently returns before `isUploading = true`, so it is fine today, but if you move the flag above the guard (tempting once compression moves into the VM per the next item) you will strand the overlay forever on a nil-encode. Keep `isUploading = false` BEFORE `await load()` (current order, :106-107) or the scrim will linger through the refetch and stack on top of the status reload.

**Acceptance** — Enable Network Link Conditioner (Edge; 100% loss for the failure case). Tap upload, pick a photo: a dim scrim + orange spinner appears immediately, the red button shows a spinner, and taps on the card do nothing. Attempt a second upload tap mid-flight — no second confirmationDialog, and only one `driver/upload-license` request in the log. On success the scrim clears and the row flips to amber "under review"; on a forced failure the scrim clears and the error alert shows.

<a id="gap-7-3"></a>

#### 7.3 `[P2]` Downscale and orientation-normalize the licence JPEG before upload

**What to build**

CONFIRMED, and it is broader than one screen. `VerificationMVVM.uploadLicense` line 82 is `image.jpegData(compressionQuality: 0.6)` applied to the raw `UIImagePickerController` output — a full-resolution 12MP capture, typically 2-4 MB, uploaded over a driver's cellular link. A repo-wide grep for `jpegData|UIGraphicsImageRenderer|compressionQuality|preparingThumbnail` shows NO compression helper anywhere: the only `UIGraphicsImageRenderer` uses are map-pin rendering (YandexMap.swift:2111, :2217), and `CompleteDriverInfoMVVM.swift:187` and `:193` have the same raw-encode problem (0.7 avatar, 0.6 documents). Android sends a Compressor-processed file — `import id.zelory.compressor.Compressor` (VerificationFragment.kt:30) then `Compressor.compress(requireContext(), original)` (:361), whose defaults bound to 612x816 at quality 80, landing around 100-200 KB. Build a shared `MehrgoDriver/Utilities/ImageCompression.swift` with e.g. `func compressedJPEG(_ image: UIImage, maxLongEdge: CGFloat = 1280, targetBytes: Int = 1_000_000) -> Data?`: redraw through `UIGraphicsImageRenderer` at a size fitting `maxLongEdge` (1280 rather than Android's 612x816 so a moderator can still read licence text — Android's bound is the floor, not the target), encode at 0.8, and if the result exceeds `targetBytes` re-encode stepping quality down 0.7 / 0.6 / 0.5. The redraw also bakes in orientation, which the current path does not: `ImagePicker` sets `allowsEditing = false` and returns `info[.originalImage]` (VerificationView.swift:413), so a portrait camera capture arrives as a `.right`-oriented UIImage whose raw pixel buffer is landscape, and `jpegData` writes those pixels with an EXIF orientation tag that server-side thumbnailers frequently ignore — the moderator sees sideways licences. Call the helper from `uploadLicense` and from both `CompleteDriverInfoMVVM` sites.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/main/ui/verification/VerificationFragment.kt:30 (Compressor import), :361 (Compressor.compress), :389-395 (compress/multipart failure surfaces an error rather than crashing)

**iOS target** — New MehrgoDriver/Utilities/ImageCompression.swift; call sites MehrgoDriver/MVVM/VerificationMVVM.swift:82 and MehrgoDriver/MVVM/CompleteDriverInfoMVVM.swift:187, :193

> **Gotchas.** Use `UIGraphicsImageRendererFormat` with `scale = 1` — the default takes the device scale (3x on most driver handsets) and silently re-inflates the image back to roughly the original pixel count, defeating the whole exercise. Do not resize at the `CGImage` level without going through UIImage, or you drop the orientation normalization that is half the point. Do not compress below Android's 612x816 effective floor: licence text must stay legible for the moderator, and there is no re-request flow if it is not. Android also wraps compress+multipart in a try/catch that surfaces an error toast for corrupt / 0-byte / HEIC / revoked-Uri inputs (VerificationFragment.kt:389-395) — mirror that by returning nil from the helper and surfacing `errorMessage`, instead of silently `return`ing as line 82 does today.

**Acceptance** — Upload a 12MP portrait camera capture: the request body logs under ~500 KB (vs 2-4 MB today), and the moderator-side image renders upright with readable licence text. Repeat with a landscape capture and with a gallery HEIC — both upload upright and succeed. Confirm the same helper is used by the CompleteDriverInfo avatar/document uploads and that those still pass server-side validation.

#### 7.4 `[P2]` Distinguish a failed status load from "no checks" on the verification screen

**What to build**

CONFIRMED, with one mitigation the original claim missed. `VerificationView`'s body branches `if let status = vm.status, !status.visibleChecks.isEmpty` → `else if vm.state == .Loading` → `else EmptyStateView(systemImage: "checkmark.seal.fill", titleKey: "verification_empty")` (VerificationView.swift:34-53). `VerificationMVVM.load()` assigns `status = res.data` only in the success path (:59) and on failure sets `errorMessage` + `state = .Negative` while leaving `status` untouched (:61-65). So on a FIRST-load failure `status` is nil and `state` is `.Negative`, and after the driver dismisses the error alert they are looking at a green checkmark seal reading "Tekshiruvlar yo'q" — the app tells a blocked driver that nothing is required. The same green seal also appears on the `{"data": null}` case from gap 1 (status nil, state `.Positive`). Mitigation the report understated: the ScrollView already carries `.refreshable { await vm.load() }` (:68), so a pull-down retry exists — the defect is the misleading copy, not an unrecoverable dead end, which is why this stays P2. Fix: split the fallback branch. When `vm.state == .Negative && vm.status == nil`, render an error state (`exclamationmark.triangle`, connection-error copy) with an explicit "Retry" button calling `Task { await vm.load() }`; keep the checkmark-seal empty state only for a SUCCESSFUL response whose `visibleChecks` is empty. A second-and-later load failure keeps the previously fetched `status` and re-renders the old cards, which is acceptable.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/main/ui/verification/VerificationFragment.kt:153 (tvEmpty.isVisible = visible.isEmpty(), reached only from render() on Resource.Success at :120-122) and :125-128 (Resource.Error → showLoading(false) + error toast; render() never called, so the empty view never appears)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Verification/VerificationView.swift:32-53 (body branch), reusing the existing EmptyStateView component

> **Gotchas.** Do not key the error branch on `vm.errorMessage != nil` — `.errorAlert` nils it out the moment the alert is dismissed (:85-91), so the branch would flip back to the green seal exactly when the driver needs it. Key on `vm.state == .Negative && vm.status == nil`. Also do not clear `status` in `load()`'s catch as a shortcut: that would wipe a good previously-rendered list on a transient refresh failure and make the screen flicker to the error state on every flaky pull-to-refresh.

**Acceptance** — Airplane mode, open the verification screen cold: after the error alert is dismissed the screen shows a warning icon with connection-error copy and a working Retry button — never the green "no checks" seal. Restore network, tap Retry → the checks render. Separately, stub a 200 with `{"data":null}` → the error state (not the green seal) shows. Finally, a genuine success with `checks: []` still shows the green seal.

#### 7.5 `[P2]` Make the single Skip button dismiss the screen like Android

**What to build**

CONFIRMED. Android's one end-of-screen skip is a pure dismissal: `binding.tvSkipAll.setDebouncedClickListener { findNavController().navigateUp() }` (VerificationFragment.kt:91), with the documented intent "dismisses the verification screen" and a safety note that it is only shown when nothing mandatory is outstanding. Android tracks NO skipped-key set at all. iOS's `skipAllButton` instead loops `for check in skippable { vm.skip(check.key) }` (VerificationView.swift:344) and stops there. Because the optional section and the button both filter on `!vm.skippedKeys.contains($0.key)` (:216 and :339), pressing Skip makes the optional cards vanish AND the Skip button vanish with them, leaving the driver on a screen with nothing actionable. Fix: add `dismiss()` after the skip loop — `@Environment(\.dismiss)` is already declared at :25, and the screen is presented via `.sheet(isPresented: $vm.presentVerification, onDismiss: { vm.loadVerification() })` at MapHomeView.swift:244, so `dismiss()` correctly tears down the sheet and triggers the chip refresh. Keep `skippedKeys` as-is: it is session-local on both platforms (Android re-renders from a fresh fetch every load), so reopening re-shows the suggestions — that part already matches. **Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/main/ui/verification/VerificationFragment.kt:88-91 (tvSkipAll → navigateUp, with the "can never let a driver bypass a required check" comment), :147-151 (visibility rule)

Do NOT relax the `status.canDriverGoOnline` guard on line 341 while adding the dismiss: with dismissal wired up, showing Skip when a blocking check exists would turn it into a real bypass affordance out of the gated screen. Do not persist `skippedKeys` to UserDefaults as a "nicer" alternative — Android is session-local by construction, and persisting would permanently hide suggestions the server still wants surfaced.

**Acceptance** — With a payload containing only optional/skippable failing checks, open verification and tap Skip → the sheet dismisses back to the map and the home chip refreshes (`driver/verification-status` fires from the sheet's onDismiss). Reopen the screen → the skipped suggestions are visible again. With a payload containing a blocking check (required, or required + `action:"pending"`), the Skip button must not render at all.

### 8. Maps + external navigation (in-app map surface, external navigator deep-links, nav-control gating)

**Gaps:** 2 total — P0: 0 · P1: 1 · P2: 1

#### iOS today

External navigation is fully centralized in `MehrgoDriver/MVVM/ChooseMapMVVM.swift`: `openExternalRoute(map:through:to:)` → `buildURL` (line 238) emits per-app multi-waypoint URLs — Apple `daddr=A+to:B+to:C` via `appleDestinationChain` (282), Google universal `https://www.google.com/maps/dir/?api=1&origin&destination&waypoints=…%7C…` (300), Yandex `rtext=from~via~to&rtt=auto` (256), Yandex Navi `lat_via_N/lon_via_N` (348), and 2GIS/Waze routed to the immediate next stop via `waypoints.first ?? to` (263/272), exactly matching Android's `vias.firstOrNull() ?: dest` (MapFragment.kt:5060/5074). All 6 apps are modeled in `ExternalMap` (`Shared/DomainEnums.swift:110`) with `isBuiltIn`, `scheme`, and `appStoreURL` (itms-apps deep link + https fallback, `ChooseMapMVVM.swift:66`); the default-map overload (190) probes the bare scheme rather than the built https URL — a deliberate correctness detail — and falls back to Apple Maps when the chosen app is missing. `TripView.swift:470 canNavigate` returns false for `.accepted`, true for `.started`, and `hasDestination` for `.changedArrived`/`.changedGone`; the Map circle is HIDDEN rather than grayed (`TripView.swift:843`, 872-873). `navigationCoordinate` (499) and `navigationWaypoints` (535) share `Order.pickupCoordinate`/`dropoffCoordinate` (SharedModels.swift:518/541) with the map pins and drop already-passed mid-stops via `vm.nextStopIndex`, and the auto-open (`TripView.swift:233-239`) is gated on `autoOpenOnRideStart && scenePhase == .active && (started|changedGone) && canNavigate` — stricter than Android, which has no foreground check. There is exactly one external-nav surface (the Map circle + its `.contextMenu` at 853), so no unguarded second entry point exists — verified: `openExternalRoute`/`openAppleMaps` have no other call sites outside ChooseMapMVVM and TripView:457/514. The in-app `Shared/YandexMap.swift` is genuinely ahead of Android. The two surviving defects are both about the GATE, not the deep-link builders: `hasDestination` is resolved from an address STRING while everything downstream resolves a COORDINATE, and `installedMaps()` omits the `isBuiltIn` short-circuit that its sibling `canOpen` has.

#### Android today

Android's gate is the geometric mirror of what iOS should have: `MapFragment.kt:4686 hasNavigableTarget(ord)` returns false on empty `locations`, true for any state below `ORDER_STATE_CHANGED_ARRIVED` (approach leg is always navigable), and otherwise `ord.locations.any { it.position > 0 }` — tested by POSITION, never by an address name, with an explicit doc comment (4682-4684) that position is used so it stays robust to unsorted server data. That same predicate drives both the control's visibility (`MapFragment.kt:4875` `cvFullNavigate.visibility = if (hasNavigableTarget(ord)) VISIBLE else GONE`, and `2018` `hasNavTarget`) and the launch path (`4731` early-return inside the open function), so Android cannot show a nav button that then no-ops. Waypoints come from `externalRouteWaypoints(ord)` (4699), split at `4750` into `dest` + `vias = waypoints.dropLast(1)` and dispatched per map type at 4752-4756 (`openGoogleMap` 5008, `openYandexMap` 5023, `openYandexNavi` 5038, `open2GISMap` 5055, `openWazeMap` 5070). Android exposes only the 5 third-party apps (no Apple Maps equivalent), so gap 2 has no Android counterpart — it is an iOS-only correctness bug rather than a parity item. Android also duplicates the per-app open helpers across `MainActivity.kt:1491/1502`, `ActiveMyOrdersFragment.kt:798/809` and `OrdersFragment.kt:806/817`, which is exactly the fragmentation iOS avoided by centralizing in ChooseMapMVVM.

<a id="gap-8-1"></a>

#### 8.1 `[P1]` Gate the nav control on the dropoff COORDINATE, not on the dropoff address NAME

**What to build**

VERIFIED as written. `TripView.swift:762-764` defines `private var hasDestination: Bool { vm.order.dropoffAddress != nil }`. `dropoffAddress` (SharedModels.swift:481-487) is a pure STRING resolver: `sortedLocations.last?.name` only when `sorted.count >= 2` AND non-empty, else `addressFinish?.name` when non-empty, else nil. `navigationCoordinate` (TripView.swift:499-508) instead reads `destinationCoordinate` (TripView.swift:1259-1260) → `Order.dropoffCoordinate` (SharedModels.swift:541-555), a pure COORDINATE resolver with a DIFFERENT fallback chain (`locations.last` lat/lon rejecting the (0,0) sentinel, else `addressFinish` lat/lon rejecting (0,0)). The two disagree in both directions. FALSE-NEGATIVE: a 2-stop order whose final location has valid latitude/longitude but an empty/null `name` and no `addressFinish.name` yields `hasDestination == false` → `canNavigate` (479) false for `.changedArrived`/`.changedGone` → the Map circle is hidden (843) AND the auto-open is suppressed (237), even though `dropoffCoordinate` resolves a good B point; the driver silently loses external navigation the moment the rider boards. This is not hypothetical on this backend — driver/taximeter-originated orders are known to ship locations with coordinates but blank reverse-geocoded names. FALSE-POSITIVE: `addressFinish.name` non-empty while `addressFinish.latitude/longitude` are nil or (0,0) and `locations.count < 2` → `hasDestination` true → circle renders → `openDefaultNavigation()` hits `guard let coord = navigationCoordinate else { return }` (455) and does nothing, silently, with no toast — a dead button. FIX: `private var hasDestination: Bool { vm.order.dropoffCoordinate != nil }`. This is the iOS equivalent of Android's `ord.locations.any { it.position > 0 }` (MapFragment.kt:4689) and is guaranteed consistent with `navigationCoordinate` because both then read the same resolver. Do NOT substitute `(order.locations?.count ?? 0) >= 2` (the spelling used in FareCalculator.swift:184 and TripMVVM.swift:343) — that disagrees with `dropoffCoordinate`, which legitimately accepts a 1-location order carrying an `addressFinish` with real coordinates. ADDITIONAL, found while verifying: `hasDestination` has two more consumers, the stop-count vs `open_trip_badge` header (674) and the `open_trip_hint` block (702), while the destination ROW in `routeStops` (804) keys off `vm.order.dropoffAddress` directly. After the fix, a coordinate-only/nameless dropoff would flip the header to the stop-count branch while `routeStops` still omits the dropoff row. Make `routeStops` symmetric: emit the red destination row whenever `dropoffCoordinate != nil`, labelling it `vm.order.dropoffAddress ?? LocalizedAppString("not_showed")` — the same nameless-stop fallback already used by `Order.intermediateStops` (SharedModels.swift:500) and `MyOrderRow.swift:98`.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/maps/yandex_map/MapFragment.kt:4686-4690 (`hasNavigableTarget` — position-based, name-independent, with the rationale comment at 4672-4685); its two consumers at MapFragment.kt:4875 (`cvFullNavigate.visibility`) and MapFragment.kt:4731 (early return inside the open path, so a visible button can never no-op); waypoint selection at MapFragment.kt:4699 `externalRouteWaypoints`.

**iOS target** — C:/Users/Umar Inagamjanov/Downloads/mehrgo-driver-ios-main/MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Home/TripView.swift (`hasDestination` line 762; consumers at 674, 702, 843; `routeStops` line 776-812, destination row at 804). Read-only reference, do not change: MehrgoDriver/Shared/Models/SharedModels.swift:481 (`dropoffAddress`) and :541 (`dropoffCoordinate`).

> **Gotchas.** Android learned that the pickup-vs-dropoff nav target must flip exactly at ARRIVED, and that the geometry — not the label — decides navigability; MapFragment.kt:4682-4684 spells out that position is used because the server can return locations unsorted and names are unreliable. Do not extend the fix by letting the driver pick a B when none exists: the boss directive (echoed in TripView.swift:727-731 and 656-663) is that the driver may NEVER set the rider's destination. Also keep the `.started` branch of `canNavigate` returning an unconditional true — the approach leg must stay navigable for B-less orders, which is precisely Android's `if (ord.state < ORDER_STATE_CHANGED_ARRIVED) return true`.

**Acceptance** — 1) Craft/replay an order with 2 locations where the last has valid lat/lon and `name` = "" (or null) and `address_finish` absent: slide to arrived — the Map circle must render, tapping it must open the default navigator routed to the last location's coordinate, and with auto-open enabled the Kettik transition must launch the navigator. 2) Craft an order with `address_finish.name` set but `address_finish.latitude/longitude` null (or 0,0) and a single location: the Map circle must NOT render at `.changedArrived`, and no tap can produce a silent no-op. 3) Regression: a genuinely open-ended order (1 location, no address_finish) still hides the circle after arrival and still shows the `open_trip_badge` + `open_trip_hint`. 4) Regression: a normal named 2-stop and a 3-stop order behave unchanged, with the multi-stop waypoint chain intact in Yandex/Google.

#### 8.2 `[P2]` Make Apple Maps appear in the long-press navigator chooser

**What to build**

VERIFIED as written, and the scope is narrower than the report implied — which makes it easier to fix and easier to justify as P2. `ChooseMapMVVM.canOpen(_:)` (line 54-58) correctly short-circuits `if map.isBuiltIn { return true }` before probing, but the static `installedMaps()` (line 110-115) does NOT: it runs `UIApplication.shared.canOpenURL(URL(string: map.scheme))` over `ExternalMap.allCases`, and `.Apple`'s scheme is `"maps://"` (Shared/DomainEnums.swift:139). `MehrgoDriver/Info.plist:73-82` declares exactly `comgooglemaps`, `yandexmaps`, `yandexnavi`, `dgis`, `waze`, `tel`, `sms`, `mailto` — `maps` is absent, and since iOS 9 `canOpenURL` returns false for any undeclared custom scheme, so Apple Maps is filtered out of `installedMaps()`. CONFIRMED BLAST RADIUS: `installedMaps()` has exactly ONE call site in the whole repo — `TripView.swift:854`, the `.contextMenu` on the Map circle. The Settings picker (`View/Main/Settings/ChooseMapView.swift:35/37`) uses the instance `vm.canOpen(map)`, which has the short-circuit, so Apple Maps is correctly selectable there and the default-map open path (`openExternalRoute(through:to:)`, line 190-204) is also unaffected because it checks `map.isBuiltIn ||` first. So the only broken surface is the long-press chooser — and the consequence is worse than 'Apple missing': on a device with no third-party navigator installed the context menu renders EMPTY, a long-press that does nothing. The default short-tap still works throughout, which is why this is P2 and not P1. FIX: in `installedMaps()` change the predicate to `ExternalMap.allCases.filter { $0.isBuiltIn || (URL(string: $0.scheme).map { UIApplication.shared.canOpenURL($0) } ?? false) }` — this alone is sufficient and needs no plist change; optionally also add `<string>maps</string>` to `LSApplicationQueriesSchemes` for belt-and-suspenders. Since `.Apple` is the first case in `ExternalMap.allCases` (DomainEnums.swift:113, ordered deliberately per the comment at 111-112), it will head the menu. Finally, correct the now-stale comment at TripView.swift:851-852, which asserts the opposite ('installedMaps() already includes Apple Maps (built-in, its scheme always passes canOpenURL)') — an incorrect assumption baked into a comment is what will cause this to regress.

**Android reference** — No direct counterpart — Android exposes only the 5 third-party navigators (MapFragment.kt:4752-4756) and has no built-in-map concept, so this is an iOS-only correctness bug rather than an Android-parity gap. The closest analogue is Android's per-app install probing before launching (`open2GISMap`/`openWazeMap` at MapFragment.kt:5055/5070, duplicated in MainActivity.kt:1491/1502).

**iOS target** — C:/Users/Umar Inagamjanov/Downloads/mehrgo-driver-ios-main/MehrgoDriver/MVVM/ChooseMapMVVM.swift (`installedMaps()`, lines 110-115) — primary fix. Optional: MehrgoDriver/Info.plist (LSApplicationQueriesSchemes array, lines 72-82, add `maps`). Also update the stale comment at MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Home/TripView.swift:850-852.

> **Gotchas.** Do not 'fix' this by hardcoding an Apple Maps entry into the TripView context menu — that reintroduces the duplication the centralized `ChooseMapMVVM` exists to prevent, and the comment at TripView.swift:852 ('no extra hardcoded entry') shows the author already rejected that. Keep `installedMaps()` and `canOpen(_:)` using the SAME isBuiltIn-first rule so they cannot drift again. Note that `canOpenURL` on the built Google URL would always succeed because it is an https universal link — the existing code deliberately probes the bare `scheme` instead (see the comment at ChooseMapMVVM.swift:183-189); preserve that. Simulator install state differs from device, so verify on a real iPhone.

**Acceptance** — On a physical device during an active navigable trip, long-press the Map circle: Apple Maps must appear as the first entry alongside every installed third-party navigator, and selecting it must open Apple Maps routed from the driver's GPS through any mid-stops to the destination. Then test on a device with NO third-party map apps installed — the menu must still show Apple Maps rather than rendering empty. Confirm the Settings map picker (ChooseMapView) is unchanged and still shows Apple Maps as installed/selectable.

#### iOS today

Confirmed: `SettingsView.swift:188-193` has a "videos" row pushing `VideosView()`, which loads `video/index` via `VideosMVVM.load()` (`VideosMVVM.swift:37-57`, `BaseResponse<Array<Video>>`), groups by `Video.category` with the `otherCategoryKey = "__other__"` sentinel (`VideosMVVM.swift:29-33`), and renders each row as a 36pt red `play.fill` chip + title + chevron (`VideosView.swift:95-122`). Tapping sets `@State playing` and presents `.sheet(item:)` → `VideoPlayerView(url:)` (`VideosView.swift:55-59`), which is pure AVKit: `VideoPlayer(player: player ?? AVPlayer(url: url))` with `p.play()` in `onAppear` (`VideoPlayerView.swift:9-33`). I re-grepped the whole repo for `WKWebView`, `youtu`, `AVKit`, `AVPlayer`, `VideoPlayer` — zero WKWebView hits, and the only video code paths are `VideoPlayerView.swift`, `SupportView.swift:288-314` and `VerificationView.swift:305-315`, both of which already do the correct thing: `SupportView.FAQRow.videoURL(_:)` passes a full URL through and wraps a bare id into `https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=<id>`, then `@Environment(\.openURL)`; `VerificationView.videoButtonRow` does `openURL(URL(string: check.videoURL))` where `videoURL` decodes `video_url` (`VerificationStatus.swift:54-68`). So the AVPlayer black-screen path is real and isolated to the Settings video list. Two corrections to the report's premises: `VerificationView.swift` lives at `MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Verification/VerificationView.swift`, not under `Settings/`; and there are only TWO `Localizable.strings` bundles — `ru.lproj` and `uz.lproj` (`en.lproj` contains only `InfoPlist.strings`), so there is no kk/ky bundle to translate into. There is no `YouTubeHelper` anywhere in `MehrgoDriver/Shared/` — the id/URL normalization exists once, as a `private static func` inside `SupportView.FAQRow`.

#### Android today

Android deliberately deleted in-app playback. `common/YoutubeHelper.kt` documents that both attempts failed on-device on 2026-07-20 (PierfrancescoSoffritti IFrame player → instant UNKNOWN error on every video including an embedding-enabled one; full-screen WebView on m.youtube.com with a desktop UA also would not play), and exposes only `extractId(url)` (4 branches: `youtu.be/`, `v=`, `/embed/`, else last path component) and `openExternally(context, videoId)` which tries `vnd.youtube:<id>` first and falls back to the https watch URL on `ActivityNotFoundException`. Both `VideoFragment.kt:49-65` (Settings video list detail, reached from `VideosFragment.onVideoClick` passing `video.url` + `video.title`) and `InstructionFragment.kt:94-110` (contextual "?" help video) render the same layout: Glide-loaded `https://i.ytimg.com/vi/<id>/hqdefault.jpg` poster in a 190dp / 16dp-corner `MaterialCardView` with a scrim, a 64dp translucent circle holding a 36dp `ph_play_circle` tinted `@color/app_color`, plus a secondary bordered `btnWatchOnYoutube` reading `@string/open_in_youtube_app` ("YouTube ilovasida ochish") — poster and button share one click listener (`fragment_video.xml`, `fragment_instruction.xml`). `domain/model/Video.kt` is `Video(id: Int, title: String, url: String, poster: String, type: Int)` — no `category` — and `VideoAdapter.onBind` Glide-loads `item.poster` per row. Endpoint is the same `video/index` (`ApiService.kt:324`). The report's claim that four screens all pass `Constants.VIDEO_SIGN_UP` is WRONG: there are SEVEN "?" entry points, each passing a different position key — `LoginFragment.kt:171` and `SignUpFragment.kt:170` pass `VIDEO_SIGN_UP="registratsiya"`, but `PasswordRecoveryFragment.kt:141` passes `VIDEO_PASSWORD_RECOVERY="parolni_tiklash"`, `HomeFragment.kt:275` passes `VIDEO_ORDERS="buyurtmalar"`, `MyOrdersFragment.kt:120` passes `VIDEO_MY_ORDERS`, `NotificationsFragment.kt:93` passes `VIDEO_CHAT`, `SettingsFragment.kt:131` passes `VIDEO_ADDITIONAL` (all 11 keys at `Constants.kt:462-472`). Android's `domain/model/Instruction.kt` is `Instruction(id, name, position, video, type: Type(id, name))` — the title field is `name`, and there is no `body`.

The only divergences found are the two below; both are real, neither is a missing feature.

Android's earnings window math is in `presentation/main/ui/driver_earnings/DriverEarningsFragment.kt`: `periodStart()` line 206-212 hard-codes `PeriodType.WEEK -> date.with(TemporalAdjusters.previousOrSame(DayOfWeek.MONDAY))` and `periodEnd()` line 214-219 uses `periodStart(date).plusDays(6)` — locale-independent, always Mon–Sun; `loadSummary()` (line 220+) formats both with `DateTimeFormatter.ISO_LOCAL_DATE` and posts them as `from`/`to`. `PeriodType.DAY`/`MONTH` use `date` and `withDayOfMonth(1)`/`lastDayOfMonth()`, which have no week dependency. The history list surface is `app/src/main/res/layout/adapter_order_history.xml:15-18` — `cardBackgroundColor="@color/gray_light_black_light"`, `cardCornerRadius="20dp"`, `cardElevation="0dp"`, `strokeWidth="0dp"` — a deliberately flat soft-gray card, sitting on `fragment_orders_history.xml:57` root `android:background="@color/white_black"` (white screen). The file's own header comment states the intent: "soft low-contrast card on a white screen" for client-app parity. `OrderHistoryAdapter` is shared by the main history screen and both My-Orders finished/canceled tabs, so the surface treatment is uniform across all three Android history lists.

#### 10.1 `[P2]` Pin the Earnings "Week" window to Monday so it matches Android

**What to build**

CONFIRMED — `grep -rn "firstWeekday|Calendar(identifier"` over the whole iOS repo returns ZERO hits, so nothing anywhere pins the week start. `EarningsMVVM.windowForPeriod()` line 160 takes `let cal: Calendar = Calendar.current` and the `.Week` case at line 167 does `cal.dateInterval(of: .weekOfYear, for: referenceDate)?.start`, which honours the DEVICE region's firstWeekday. Android hard-codes Monday (`DriverEarningsFragment.kt:208` `previousOrSame(DayOfWeek.MONDAY)`, end = `plusDays(6)` at line 216). On an en_US-region device the iOS week starts SUNDAY, so the same driver sends a different `from`/`to` to `driver-earnings/summary` and sees different Week totals/bars than on Android. Note the blast radius is region-gated: uz_UZ / ru_RU / kk_KZ regions already resolve firstWeekday = 2, so most production drivers are unaffected — this is a parity/QA-repeatability bug, not a live money bug (the server computes the amounts; only the window differs). Fix: add a single stored `private let cal: Calendar = { var c = Calendar(identifier: .gregorian); c.firstWeekday = 2; c.timeZone = .current; return c }()` on EarningsMVVM and use it in `windowForPeriod()` in place of `Calendar.current`, then use the same instance for `canGoNext` line 128 (`Calendar.current.isDate(referenceDate, equalTo: Date(), toGranularity: unit)`) — `unit` returns `.weekOfYear` for `.Week` (line 151-157), so it carries the identical firstWeekday dependency and would otherwise disagree with the shifted window and mis-enable/disable the next arrow at the Sun/Mon boundary. `shiftPeriod()` line 121 also uses `Calendar.current.date(byAdding: unit, …)`; adding weeks is firstWeekday-independent so it is safe either way, but switching it to the same instance costs nothing and keeps one calendar in the file. `.Day` (line 162) and `.Month` (line 170) need no change. Do NOT touch `HistoryMVVM` — its `.Week` at line 198-199 is a rolling `cal.date(byAdding: .day, value: -7, to: now)` filter with no Android counterpart at all.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/main/ui/driver_earnings/DriverEarningsFragment.kt:206-219 (periodStart/periodEnd), :220+ (loadSummary formats from/to as ISO_LOCAL_DATE)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MVVM/EarningsMVVM.swift — windowForPeriod() `.Week` case lines 166-169 and the `let cal` on line 160; canGoNext line 126-129; optionally shiftPeriod line 121

> **Gotchas.** Android sends only date-granularity `from`/`to` (ISO_LOCAL_DATE, no time), while iOS sends `df.dateFormat = "yyyy-MM-dd"` off Date objects plus a `tz` query item — keep the `tz` param, it is intentional and Android has no equivalent. The `end` computation `start + 7 days - 1 second` already equals Android's `start.plusDays(6)` end-of-day, so only the START needs fixing; do not also change the end arithmetic or you will introduce an off-by-one the other way. Setting `firstWeekday` on a `Calendar` copy does NOT affect `Calendar.current` elsewhere, so this is safely local. Do not use `Locale(identifier:)` to force the week start — that would also shift month/day formatting in `periodLabel` (line 131), which deliberately follows `LanguageManager.shared.current`.

**Acceptance** — Set the iOS simulator/device Region to United States, open Earnings, choose Week. The period label (line 139-141) must read Monday–Sunday, and the `from`/`to` in the os_log line at EarningsMVVM.swift:69 must match what the Android app logs for the same driver on the same day. Tap prev/next across a Sunday→Monday boundary and confirm the next arrow disables exactly on the current Mon–Sun week (canGoNext) rather than one day early or late. Re-check with Region = Uzbekistan to confirm no regression.
