### 11. Resilience / crash-safety / error-handling patterns

**Gaps:** 8 total — P0: 2 · P1: 3 · P2: 3

#### iOS today

Verified: iOS is genuinely hardened on classic Swift crash vectors. `grep -rn "try!|as! |fatalError|preconditionFailure"` over `MehrgoDriver/**/*.swift` returns ZERO hits, and there is no `array[0]` on any server payload — `Geometry.isInside` (Shared/Extensions/Geometry.swift:17,27) is the only `[i]` indexing and it is guarded by `polygon.count >= 3` plus a per-vertex `pi.count >= 2, pj.count >= 2` continue; `RouteService.swift:44,:57` and `YandexMap.swift:558,:1588,:1946` all use `routes?.first`, i.e. the fix Android had to retrofit into `RouteNavControllerService.getRoute` was never needed here. One correction to the claimed state: the "zero force unwraps" claim is NOT literally true — `MultipartBuilder` has eight `String.data(using: .utf8)!` (DynamicClient.swift:389-402); they are safe in practice (UTF-8 encoding of a Swift String cannot fail) so this is not a gap, but the blanket claim should not go into the plan. `FlexDecode` (SharedModels.swift:19-32) is real and widely applied — `Order`(:396), `Branch`(:203), `Tariff`(:886), `Address`(:597), `OrderService`(:653), `OrderLocation`(:758), `Car`(:800), `HistoryOrderRef`(:1276), `Region`(:971), `ClientBonusSettings`(:274), `DriverEarningsSummary`(:1138), `EarningsDay`(:1183), `GpsBatch`(:91-98) all have hand-written `init(from:)` routing nested objects through `try?`. `ClientError` (DynamicClient.swift:33-65) is a proper typed funnel with `.noConnection/.timeout/.unauthorized/.server(status,message,body)/.decoding`, separates `NSURLErrorTimedOut` from no-network, and centralizes 401 through `unauthorizedSubject` → `RootMVVM.handleSessionRevoked` (:125-144) with the `authFlow401Paths` allowlist (:119-123) and the `isPendingActivation` carve-out. The error-report pipeline is real and matches Android's eight `ErrorRequest` fields: `DynamicClient.queueError` (:356-370) → `ErrorRequestStore` (Persistence/ErrorRequestStore.swift) → UserDefaults → `flush()` POST `user/report`, and it IS better than Android in two ways — `redactingLocationParams` (:344) strips lat/long/accuracy before persisting, and `flush()` removes only the sent prefix (:66) so a concurrently-appended row survives. `TripMVVM.swift:931` already has the `guard event.order.id == self.order.id` socket-cancel id guard. Nothing in the eight claimed gaps could be refuted by finding an existing implementation — no alternate spelling, no helper in another file, no `httpAdditionalHeaders`, no `nestedUnkeyedContainer` anywhere in the repo — but several claims had wrong mechanisms or overstated impact, corrected below.

#### Android today

Android is the reference for the two observability items. `HeaderInterceptor.kt:36-62` computes `APP_VERSION = sanitizeHeader("${BuildConfig.VERSION_NAME}+${BuildConfig.VERSION_CODE}")`, `OS_VERSION = Build.VERSION.RELEASE`, `DEVICE_BRAND = Build.MANUFACTURER`, `DEVICE_MODEL = Build.MODEL` once in a companion object, with `sanitizeHeader(raw) = raw.filter { it.code in 32..126 }.take(64)`, and stamps `X-Platform: android` plus those four on every authorized request — the in-code comment names this a MOBILE.md contract so support can see each driver's app version and device in the admin panel. The same interceptor (:66-88) builds `ErrorRequest(url, headers.value(0), sentRequestAtMillis, receivedResponseAtMillis, duration, socketStatus, code, body)` on any non-2xx and pushes it into `ErrorRequestManager`; `MyTrackingService.kt:498` reads that list and uploads it on every go-online (service create), and `:1723` clears it, so Android's queue drains on a schedule iOS has no equivalent of. Android's Kotlin error funnel emits the localized `Helper.getServerError()` / `Helper.getConnectionError()` rather than a raw platform exception string. Android also runs real crash triage (crash_triage_v60, crash_triage_2026_07) off symbolicated Crashlytics reports — the workflow iOS cannot currently reproduce.

<a id="gap-11-1"></a>

#### 11.1 `[P0]` Give User a hand-written init(from:) — a single type mismatch on user/me hard-blocks the splash

**What to build**

CONFIRMED. `User` (SharedModels.swift:51-122) declares CodingKeys (:74-88) but has NO `init(from decoder:)` — the whole struct uses synthesized decoding, which rethrows on any type mismatch. Live risks on `user/me`: `id: Int` (:52) is a strict non-optional decode and throws on `"12"`; `balance: Double?` (:59) and `rating: Double?` (:67) throw on `"150000"`; `today: TodayStats?` (:160-163) is fully synthesized (`count: Int?`, `price: Double?`) and throws on string numbers; `workStatus: WorkStatus?` (:166-168) has `status: String?` which throws on a number; `orders: Array<Order>?` (:63) and `tariffs: Array<Tariff>?` (:62) throw if ANY element fails, and both element types decode `id` strictly (`Order.init` :398 `try c.decode(Int.self, forKey: .id)`, `Tariff.init` :888 same). VERIFIED blast radius: `SplashMVVM.loadUser()` (SplashMVVM.swift:73-105) catches the resulting `ClientError.decoding` in its generic catch and sets `errorMessage` + `state = .Negative` — the driver is stopped at the splash screen and cannot reach the app at all. It also breaks `BalanceMVVM.refreshBalance()` (BalanceMVVM.swift:56-70, sets `.Negative`), `RootMVVM.didSignIn`, the profile screen and active-order restore. The file's own header (:13-18) documents that this backend ships numbers as JSON strings and that it already killed WebSocket order pushes once, so this is known-live behaviour simply not yet applied to `User`. Fix: write `init(from decoder:)` for `User` in the exact style of `Order` (:396) / `Branch` (:203): `self.id = FlexDecode.int(c, .id) ?? 0`; `self.balance = FlexDecode.double(c, .balance)`; `self.rating = FlexDecode.double(c, .rating)`; every String field `try? c.decode(String.self, forKey:)`; every nested object (`status`, `branch`, `today`, `car`, `workStatus`) `try? c.decode(X.self, forKey:)`; `orders`/`tariffs` through the lossy-array wrapper from item 5. Also give `TodayStats` its own `init(from:)` with `FlexDecode.int(c,.count)` / `FlexDecode.double(c,.price)`, and `WorkStatus` a `try?`-based one. CORRECTION to the original write-up: do NOT add an explicit `encode(to:)` — `User`'s CodingKeys map 1:1 onto stored properties, so Encodable synthesis survives adding only `init(from:)` (this is exactly why `Order` needs no `encode(to:)` while `OrderService` :700 does — it has extra keys with no stored property). And no memberwise init is needed: `grep` finds zero `User(...)` construction sites in app or test code. Cleanest option is to declare the `init(from:)` in an `extension User` (the pattern `DriverNotification` uses at :1072) so both the memberwise init and Encodable synthesis stay untouched.

**Android reference** — Conceptual parity only — Android uses Gson, which is lenient about string/number coercion by default, so it never needed this. Compare `domain/model/User.kt` and the `Order.kt` model the iOS header comment says it mirrors.

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/Models/SharedModels.swift — struct User :51-122, struct TodayStats :160-163, struct WorkStatus :166-168

> **Gotchas.** Declaring `init(from:)` inside the struct body suppresses the memberwise initializer; declare it in an `extension User` instead (see DriverNotification :1064-1085) to avoid that entirely. `UserManager.save/load` (Persistence/UserManager.swift:36-62) round-trips User through JSONEncoder/JSONDecoder into UserDefaults, so whatever you change must still encode symmetrically — keep CodingKeys 1:1 with stored properties and Encodable stays synthesized. Note `workStatus` is deliberately camelCase (:81-86) while the rest is snake_case; don't 'fix' it. Making `id` `?? 0` means a malformed payload now yields a User with id 0 rather than a thrown error — verify nothing keys off `user.id` for identity in a way that 0 would corrupt (it is `Identifiable`).

**Acceptance** — Point the app at a stubbed `user/me` that returns `"balance": "150000"`, `"rating": "4.8"`, `today.count` as `"3"`, and one array element with `"id": "77"`. Before the fix the splash sticks on its error state; after, the app reaches Home with balance 150000, rating 4.8 and the order/tariff lists intact. Then relaunch to confirm the cached User still decodes back out of UserDefaults.

#### 11.2 `[P0]` Route every numeric model field through FlexDecode — a string interval price silently changes the fare

**What to build**

CONFIRMED, and the money path is exactly as claimed. `DistanceInterval` (SharedModels.swift:921-926) is fully synthesized — `id: Int` strict, `start/end/price: Double?` — so `decodeIfPresent` THROWS on `"1000"`. `Tariff.init(from:)` reads the ladder as `self.distanceIntervals = try? c.decode(Array<DistanceInterval>.self, forKey: .distanceIntervals)` (:891, key is `distances`), so ONE string-typed price anywhere in the ladder nils the ENTIRE array, silently, with no log. `FareCalculator.compute` then hits `if let intervals = tariff?.distanceIntervals, !intervals.isEmpty` (FareCalculator.swift:124), the branch is skipped, and line 131 runs `distanceCharge = (chargeableInsideMeters / 1000.0) * priceInCity` — a flat per-km rate instead of the tiered ladder in `distanceChargeWithIntervals` (:292-317). The driver is paid a wrong number and nothing fails. Same class as the Android 25k→24k receipt bug, harder to spot. Fix: `init(from:)` for `DistanceInterval` with `id = FlexDecode.int(c,.id) ?? 0`, `start/end/price = FlexDecode.double(c, ...)`. Then sweep the remaining synthesized-numeric models, VERIFIED still synthesized: `Contact` :451-456 (`bonus: Double?` — a string bonus nils the whole Contact via `Order.init` :399 `try?`, losing rider name+phone on the trip screen), `TodayStats` :160, `AddressCategory` :607, `OrderAddress` :1014 (`count: Int?`), `AddressInBranch` :1022 (`latitude/longitude`), `PageMeta` :1296 (`pageCount` — see item 5's pagination note), `UpdateApp` :1328 (`versionCode: Int?`, key `mobile_version_code_ios` — a string breaks the force-update gate), `SubscriptionPlan` :1361 (`price`, `validityPeriod`), `SubscriptionPurchased` :1390 (`validTo`, `expiresIn`), `OrderCancelReason` :1007, `Instruction` :1088, `Video` :1099, `Introduce` :1305, `OrderHistoryItem` :1219, `DriverNotification` :1074, `SubscriptionTariff` :1384, plus the strict `id` decodes inside existing custom inits: `Order` :398, `Address` :599, `Tariff` :888, `OrderService` :655 — route all of them through `FlexDecode.int(c, .id) ?? 0`, exactly what `Region` :973 and `Branch` :205 already do. DEPRIORITIZE within this item: `DirectionLocations`/`RouteEntry`/`RouteStep` (:1404-1422) come from the OSRM-shaped route server, not the Yii backend, and ship real JSON numbers — convert them for uniformity but they are not a live risk.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/domain/model/Order.kt (Tariff.distances / DistanceInterval) — Gson coerces string↔number so Android never loses the ladder; the fare math to match is in the taximeter/fare path referenced by fare_receipt_deferred.md.

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/Models/SharedModels.swift — DistanceInterval :921 (primary), Contact :451, TodayStats :160, AddressCategory :607, OrderAddress :1014, AddressInBranch :1022, PageMeta :1296, UpdateApp :1328, SubscriptionPlan :1361, SubscriptionPurchased :1390, plus the strict `id` lines at :398, :599, :655, :888; behaviour verified against MehrgoDriver/Shared/FareCalculator.swift:124,:131,:292-317

> **Gotchas.** The ladder key is `distances`, NOT `distance_intervals` (Tariff CodingKeys :877) and the commission key is the misspelled `comission` (:883) — don't normalize either. `distanceChargeWithIntervals` treats `start`/`end` as METRES and already sorts by `start` ASC (:303) and uses `interval.price ?? fallbackPricePerKm` per-row (:314), so once elements decode, a single nil price degrades gracefully — the catastrophic case is only the whole-array nil. Because the failure is silent, add an `os_log(.error, ...)` when `distanceIntervals` comes back nil while the raw key was present, otherwise this exact bug recurs undetected. `Tariff` also has a memberwise init at :901 used by FareCalculatorTests — keep it compiling.

#### 11.3 `[P1]` Add the Crashlytics dSYM upload build phase so crash reports arrive symbolicated

**What to build**

CONFIRMED. `project.yml:70-71` links `FirebaseCrashlytics`, `MehrgoDriver/GoogleService-Info.plist` exists, and `MehrgoDriverApp.swift:32-33` calls `FirebaseApp.configure()` gated on that plist — so Crashlytics auto-registers and DOES collect crashes. But `grep -c shellScript MehrgoDriver.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` returns 0 and `project.yml` has no `postCompileScripts`/`postBuildScripts` key anywhere, so dSYMs are never uploaded and every crash in the Firebase console sits in `Missing dSYM` as raw hex addresses. `grep -rn Crashlytics --include=*.swift MehrgoDriver` is empty, so there is also no user/order context on any report. Net effect: iOS cannot run the triage workflow Android ran twice (v56, v60). Add to the MehrgoDriver target in project.yml a `postCompileScripts` entry running `${BUILD_DIR%/Build/*}/SourcePackages/checkouts/firebase-ios-sdk/Crashlytics/run` with `inputFiles` `${DWARF_DSYM_FOLDER_PATH}/${DWARF_DSYM_FILE_NAME}/Contents/Resources/DWARF/${TARGET_NAME}` and `$(SRCROOT)/$(BUILT_PRODUCTS_DIR)/$(INFOPLIST_PATH)`, then re-run `xcodegen generate`. No other build setting needs changing: `DEBUG_INFORMATION_FORMAT = "dwarf-with-dsym"` is already set for Release (project.pbxproj:1190; Debug is plain `dwarf` at :1071, which is correct) and `ENABLE_BITCODE: NO` (project.yml:14) means dSYMs are produced locally. Second half, same PR: add `Crashlytics.crashlytics().setUserID(String(user.id))` after sign-in and `setCustomValue(order.id, forKey: "order_id")` at trip start — that is what turns 'a crash happened' into 'a crash happened to this driver on this order', the context the Android triage notes leaned on. DOWNGRADED from the claimed P0: this is observability tooling, not a crash/money/data-loss defect — it does not itself break anything for a driver. It is P1 because everything else on this list gets much harder to validate in production without it.

**Android reference** — No file-level Android ref — this is the enabling infrastructure behind the Android triage work recorded in crash_triage_v60.md and crash_triage_2026_07.md.

**iOS target** — project.yml (targets.MehrgoDriver — add postCompileScripts), then regenerate MehrgoDriver.xcodeproj; MehrgoDriver/MVVM/RootMVVM.swift (didSignIn) and MehrgoDriver/MVVM/TripMVVM.swift (trip start) for the custom keys

> **Gotchas.** The project is regenerated by xcodegen — editing project.pbxproj directly is thrown away on the next `xcodegen generate`; the change MUST go in project.yml. The `${BUILD_DIR%/Build/*}/SourcePackages/checkouts/...` path is the SPM (not CocoaPods) location and depends on the resolved package directory name `firebase-ios-sdk` — verify it after `xcodegen generate` rather than trusting it. `setUserID` sends a driver identifier to Firebase; use the numeric user id only, never phone number or name. Do not attach the auth token as a custom key.

**Acceptance** — Archive a Release build and confirm the build log shows the Crashlytics run script executing and uploading. Force a test crash from a TestFlight build; within ~10 minutes the Firebase console should show a fully symbolicated stack with Swift function names and line numbers, no `Missing dSYM` banner, and (after the second half lands) the `order_id` custom key and user id on the report.

<a id="gap-11-4"></a>

#### 11.4 `[P1]` Stop reporting cancelled Tasks as "no internet connection"

**What to build**

CONFIRMED, with the mechanism corrected. `DynamicClient.request`'s transport catch (DynamicClient.swift:167-181) special-cases only `NSURLErrorTimedOut` and converts everything else — including `URLError(.cancelled)` / NSURLErrorDomain `-999`, which is what `URLSession.data(for:)` actually throws on Task cancellation — into `ClientError.noConnection`, whose `errorDescription` is `LocalizedAppString("error_no_connection")`. The `multipart` transport catch (:255-260) is worse: it has no timeout case at all and blanket-throws `.noConnection`. Consequence CONFIRMED: the `catch is CancellationError` blocks at HistoryMVVM.swift:103,:132 and MyOrdersMVVM.swift:72,:111,:142 are DEAD CODE — they never match — and the cancelled call falls into the generic `catch { errorMessage = error.localizedDescription }`, showing a spurious 'Internetga ulanish yo'q'. CORRECTION to the original diagnosis: the trigger is NOT SwiftUI's `.task` cancellation for these screens. Those MVVMs wrap their work in an unstructured `Task { @MainActor in ... }` (HistoryMVVM.swift:92, MyOrdersMVVM.swift:59,:99,:130), and unstructured Tasks do NOT inherit cancellation from the enclosing `.task`. The real trigger is the four explicit supersede calls — `HistoryMVVM.refresh()` :85, `MyOrdersMVVM.refreshActive()` :58 and `refreshHistory()` :92, `MapMVVM.loadVerification()` :135 / goOnline :172 — i.e. pull-to-refresh or a period switch (`setPeriod` → `refresh()`) while a load is in flight. That still fires in ordinary use, and `MyOrdersMVVM.loadMoreHistory`'s catch additionally sets `historyLoadFailed = true` (:146), putting a Retry affordance on screen for a request the app itself cancelled. Separately CONFIRMED as a genuine `.task`-cancellation victim: `BalanceMVVM.refreshBalance()` (BalanceMVVM.swift:56-70) awaits the client DIRECTLY from `.task` (BalanceView.swift:65), so navigating away mid-load sets `state = .Negative` and the balance screen shows an error for a cancellation. Fix in ONE place — both transport catches in DynamicClient — before the timeout check: map `nsError.domain == NSURLErrorDomain && nsError.code == NSURLErrorCancelled` to `throw CancellationError()`, and also `if Task.isCancelled { throw CancellationError() }`. Add `try Task.checkCancellation()` immediately before `activeSession.data(for: req)` (:167) and before `session.upload(for:from:)` (:254) so an already-cancelled Task never fires the request and never queues an error report for it. Then add `catch is CancellationError { }` to the remaining generic catches that assign `errorMessage` or a failure state — at minimum BalanceMVVM :66, HomeMVVM, TripMVVM, SubscriptionsMVVM, ProfileMVVM, NotificationsMVVM, EarningsMVVM, VerificationMVVM, SettingsMVVM, VideosMVVM.

**Android reference** — Kotlin coroutine parity: Android's `safeResourceFlow` / use-case funnel rethrows `CancellationException` instead of mapping it to `Helper.getConnectionError()` — see the exception-transparency fixes recorded in crash_triage_v60.md and code_review_full_client_2026_07.md.

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/DynamicClient.swift — request transport catch :167-181 and multipart transport catch :253-260 (primary fix); then MehrgoDriver/MVVM/BalanceMVVM.swift:66, and the generic `catch { errorMessage = ... }` blocks across MehrgoDriver/MVVM/*.swift

> **Gotchas.** Order matters: check cancelled BEFORE timed-out, and check both before the blanket `.noConnection`. `Task.isCancelled` alone is insufficient — the unstructured-Task sites are cancelled explicitly, so the URLError(-999) mapping is the load-bearing half. Don't 'simplify' by removing the existing post-await `if Task.isCancelled { return }` guards (HistoryMVVM :97,:127; MyOrdersMVVM :68,:105,:137; MapMVVM :144) — they still protect the success path from a stale response overwriting fresher state. Note `MyOrdersMVVM.loadMoreHistory` sets `historyLoadFailed` in its generic catch, so that flag needs clearing/skipping on the cancellation path too, not just `errorMessage`.

**Acceptance** — On the History screen, pull-to-refresh twice in quick succession (or switch period while a load is in flight) — no error banner should appear and the final list must render. On My Orders, pull-to-refresh mid-load and confirm no 'Internetga ulanish yo'q' and no Retry footer. Open the Balance screen and navigate back immediately while it loads, then return — it must not be stuck in its Negative/error state. Confirm via Console.app that no `queued error` line is logged for a cancelled request.

<a id="gap-11-5"></a>

#### 11.5 `[P1]` Decode server arrays element-by-element so one bad row doesn't drop the whole list

**What to build**

CONFIRMED — no lossy-array helper exists anywhere (`grep -rn "nestedUnkeyedContainer|unkeyedContainer"` over the whole repo returns nothing). Every server array is all-or-nothing: `BaseResponse<Array<Order>>` on `order/my-orders` (MyOrdersMVVM.swift:62-70), `OrderHistoryResponse.items` (SharedModels.swift:1214), `SubscriptionData.items` (:1356), `Order.services` / `Order.locations` (:413,:416, `try?` → whole collection degrades to nil, wiping every stop or every add-on), `Tariff.distanceIntervals` (:891, the money path in item 2), `HistoryOrderRef.locations` / `.services` (:1283,:1289), `User.orders` / `User.tariffs` (:63,:62). Every element type also decodes `id` strictly, so a single `"id": "77"` is enough to trigger it. CORRECTION to the claimed impact on history pagination: the original write-up says `hasMore` falls to `!items.isEmpty == false` and pagination stops permanently. Verified — that fallback only applies when `_meta.pageCount` is absent (HistoryMVVM.swift:181-184, MyOrdersMVVM.swift:176-179), and `_meta` is the Yii2 pagination envelope that is normally present. The real observed failure is different and arguably worse: a bad row makes the whole `BaseResponse` decode throw, so the driver gets an error banner AND an empty page AND (in `loadMoreHistory`) a `historyLoadFailed` Retry state — a hard failure, not a silent truncation. Fix: add a `LossyArray.decode(_:_:_:)` helper using `nestedUnkeyedContainer`, decoding each element with `try?` and, on failure, advancing the cursor with a throwaway `AnySkip: Decodable` (skipping the advance is an infinite loop). Log each skipped element at `.error` with the container key so support can see partial-parse damage. Use it in `Order.init` (:413,:416), `Tariff.init` (:891), the new `User.init`, `HistoryOrderRef.init` (:1283,:1289), and give `OrderHistoryResponse` (:1213) and `SubscriptionData` (:1355) custom inits. For the top-level `BaseResponse<Array<Order>>` case add a `LossyList<T>: Decodable` wrapper and request `BaseResponse<LossyList<Order>>` for `order/my-orders` and the other flat-list endpoints (`order-cancel-issue`, `service/order`, `cars/index`, `branch/index`, `notification/index`, `video/index`, `instruction/index`).

**Android reference** — Gson's default behaviour — Android's list parses tolerate a malformed element rather than dropping the collection, which is why the Android driver never saw an empty active-orders list from one bad row. See the Android list-parse guard notes in crash_triage_2026_07.md (orders[0]/routes[0]/locations[0] empty-list handling).

**iOS target** — New file MehrgoDriver/Shared/Models/LossyDecoding.swift; then MehrgoDriver/Shared/Models/SharedModels.swift (Order.init :413,:416; Tariff.init :891; User; HistoryOrderRef.init :1283,:1289; OrderHistoryResponse :1213; SubscriptionData :1355) and MehrgoDriver/MVVM/MyOrdersMVVM.swift:62-70

> **Gotchas.** The `AnySkip` fallback MUST consume a value from the unkeyed container or `while !u.isAtEnd` never terminates — this is the one way to turn a parse bug into a hang. `nestedUnkeyedContainer(forKey:)` itself throws when the key holds a non-array (e.g. the backend sends `{}` instead of `[]`), so wrap it in `try?` and return nil, matching current behaviour. Item 2 must land in the same PR or before: lossy arrays hide malformed rows, so without FlexDecode on `DistanceInterval` you'd convert a loud whole-array failure into a silently SHORTER fare ladder — a worse money bug. Keep the skip logging at `.error` so it survives Release (the codebase's own convention, see DynamicClient :192-197).

**Acceptance** — Stub `order/history` with 10 items where item 3 has `"id": "abc"` — the list must render 9 rows, log one skip with the offending key, show no error banner, and `hasMore` must still follow `_meta.pageCount`. Stub `order/my-orders` with one malformed order and confirm the other active orders still appear. Stub a `Tariff` with one malformed interval and confirm the ladder keeps the remaining intervals rather than nilling out.

#### 11.6 `[P2]` Send the X-Platform / X-App-Version / X-Device-* diagnostic headers

**What to build**

CONFIRMED on both sides. `DynamicClient.applyHeaders` (DynamicClient.swift:287-297) sets only Content-Type, Accept, Accept-Language and Authorization, and the multipart path (:241-245) duplicates the same four inline. `grep -rn "X-Platform|X-App-Version|X-Device|X-OS-Version|sysctlbyname|hw.machine|utsname|httpAdditionalHeaders" --include=*.swift MehrgoDriver` returns ZERO hits — nothing sets these anywhere, including on the URLSessionConfiguration. Android's `HeaderInterceptor.kt:36-62` stamps five headers on every authorized request, described in-code as a MOBILE.md contract so support can see each driver's app version + device in the admin panel, so iOS drivers are invisible in that panel. Add to `applyHeaders` — and extract ONE helper used by both it and the multipart block, since the two currently duplicate: `X-Platform: ios`; `X-App-Version: "<CFBundleShortVersionString>+<CFBundleVersion>"` matching Android's exact `VERSION_NAME+VERSION_CODE` format (currently MARKETING_VERSION 1.5.0 / CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION 30 per project.yml:15-16 ⇒ `1.5.0+30`); `X-OS-Version: UIDevice.current.systemVersion`; `X-Device-Brand: "Apple"`; `X-Device-Model:` the hardware identifier from `uname`/`sysctlbyname("hw.machine")` (e.g. `iPhone14,5`). Port Android's `sanitizeHeader` verbatim — `raw.filter { it.code in 32..126 }.take(64)` — because the backend truncates at 64 chars and expects ASCII. Compute all five once as `static let`, matching the Android companion-object pattern. DOWNGRADED from the claimed P1: this is pure support/observability with zero driver-facing impact — nothing breaks today, support just can't see iOS device info. Worth doing, but it should not outrank the correctness items.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/HeaderInterceptor.kt:36-47 (companion object computing APP_VERSION/OS_VERSION/DEVICE_BRAND/DEVICE_MODEL + sanitizeHeader) and :56-60 (the five addHeader calls)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/DynamicClient.swift — applyHeaders :287-297 and the duplicated multipart header block :241-245

> **Gotchas.** `UIDevice.current.model` returns the literal "iPhone" for every device — useless; you must read `hw.machine` via `sysctlbyname` (or `uname`) to get `iPhone14,5`. `DynamicClient` is `@MainActor` but these are process-constants, so compute them in a `static let` (a nonisolated one) rather than per request. `X-Platform` must be lowercase `ios` to mirror Android's lowercase `android` — the admin panel likely string-matches. Apply to BOTH the `request` and `multipart` paths or document uploads stay anonymous; the current duplication is exactly how one gets missed. These are diagnostic fields only — never put the auth token or phone number in a custom header.

**Acceptance** — Proxy or Console-log a request from a debug build and confirm all five headers are present with `X-App-Version: 1.5.0+30` and `X-Device-Model` like `iPhone14,5`. Upload a document (multipart path) and confirm the same five headers appear there too. Have support confirm an iOS driver's device/version now shows in the admin panel.

<a id="gap-11-7"></a>

#### 11.7 `[P2]` Fix the error-report queue: self-feeding, unbounded, and rarely flushed

**What to build**

CONFIRMED, all three defects. (1) SELF-FEEDING: `ErrorRequestStore.flush()` (Persistence/ErrorRequestStore.swift:44-72) POSTs `user/report` through `DynamicClient.request`; on non-2xx, `DynamicClient.request` calls `queueError` (:189 → :356) which appends a NEW report describing the failed flush — and because `flush()` only removes the sent prefix on SUCCESS (:66, inside the do-block), a server-side outage makes the queue strictly grow on every flush attempt, forever. Fix: early-return in `queueError` when `url.path` ends in `user/report`. (2) UNBOUNDED: `append(_:)` (ErrorRequestStore.swift:32-35) has no cap and `persist()` writes the whole array to UserDefaults on every append; `queueError` stores the FULL `String(data: body, encoding: .utf8)` (:365), and a proxy HTML error page is tens of KB. `loadFromDisk()` runs from `init()` (:27) on first touch, so a driver with a long offline stretch pays a launch-time deserialization cost and risks plist bloat. Truncate `responseBody` to ~2 KB with an ellipsis marker in `queueError`, and cap the queue in `append` at ~100 rows dropping the OLDEST. (3) RARELY FLUSHED: CONFIRMED only two callers exist repo-wide — `AppDelegate.swift:163` inside a `BGTaskScheduler` `BGAppRefreshTask` handler (opportunistic; iOS may not run it for days) and `SupportMVVM.swift:61`. Android flushes on every go-online from `MyTrackingService.kt:498`. Add `Task { await ErrorRequestStore.shared.flush() }` to `MapMVVM.goOnline()` after `driver/start` succeeds, and to the foreground hook that already calls `RootMVVM.validateSessionIfDue()` (RootView.swift:63, `scenePhase == .active`).

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/services/MyTrackingService.kt:498 (reads ErrorRequestManager list and uploads on service create / go-online) and :1723 (clearAll); HeaderInterceptor.kt:66-88 builds the ErrorRequest rows

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/DynamicClient.swift (queueError :356-370 — add the user/report skip + responseBody truncation) and MehrgoDriver/Persistence/ErrorRequestStore.swift (append :32 — add the cap); MehrgoDriver/MVVM/MapMVVM.swift (goOnline, after driver/start succeeds) and MehrgoDriver/MVVM/RootMVVM.swift:87 validateSessionIfDue

> **Gotchas.** Do NOT 'fix' the prefix-removal logic at :66 — the comment at :46-52 explains it is deliberate and correct (both types are @MainActor, the POST is a suspension point, and clearing the whole queue would drop a concurrently-appended row). Cap by dropping the OLDEST (`removeFirst`), not the newest — recent failures are the diagnostically useful ones. RELATED CAUTION worth checking while you are in here (not itself a claimed gap): a 401 on the flush POST goes through `Self.unauthorizedSubject.send(path)` (:200) and `user/report` is NOT in `RootMVVM.authFlow401Paths` (:119-123), so a stale-token flush can trip `handleSessionRevoked` and force-sign-out the driver — verify or add the carve-out. Also do not flush on go-online BEFORE `driver/start` succeeds; a flush failure must never block going on shift.

**Acceptance** — Point `user/report` at an endpoint returning 500, trigger several flushes, and confirm the queue size stays flat (not growing by one per attempt) and never exceeds 100 rows. Confirm a queued row whose response body was a large HTML page is stored truncated to ~2 KB. Go offline, cause several failed calls, go online and swipe to go on shift — the queue must drain immediately rather than waiting for a background refresh.

<a id="gap-11-8"></a>

#### 11.8 `[P2]` Localize decode failures and report bad 2xx bodies

**What to build**

CONFIRMED, both halves. (1) `ClientError.decoding(String)` (DynamicClient.swift:46) is constructed as `ClientError.decoding(error.localizedDescription)` at :224 (request) and :281 (multipart), and `errorDescription` returns that string verbatim (`case .decoding(let m): return m`, :54) while every OTHER case correctly returns a `LocalizedAppString(...)`. Since every MVVM does `errorMessage = error.localizedDescription`, an Uzbek/Russian/Kazakh/Kyrgyz driver is shown Foundation's raw English "The data couldn't be read because it isn't in the correct format." — untranslated and leaking implementation detail. Android's funnel emits the localized `Helper.getServerError()` instead. Fix: change the case to `decoding(detail: String)`, have `errorDescription` return `LocalizedAppString("error_server")` (the string the `.unknown` case already uses, :55), and keep `detail` available for `os_log` and the error queue. (2) `queueError` is called ONLY on non-2xx (:189 and :268) — verified, those are its only two call sites — so a 200 response with an unparseable body, exactly the failure `FlexDecode` exists for and exactly what items 1/2/5 are about, never reaches `user/report` and is invisible to support. Add a `queueError(url:code:body:started:)` call inside both decode-failure catches (:222-225, :279-282) using the real HTTP status, and prefix `responseBody` with the DecodingError's `codingPath` (e.g. `DECODE_FAIL@data.orders[3].id: <body>`) so the exact offending field is identifiable from the admin panel.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/HeaderInterceptor.kt:66-88 (queues on non-2xx only, same limitation) plus the use-case funnel's `Helper.getServerError()` / `Helper.getConnectionError()` localized-message convention documented in CLAUDE.md

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/DynamicClient.swift — ClientError.decoding case :46 and errorDescription :54; the decode catches at :221-225 (request) and :278-282 (multipart); queueError :356

> **Gotchas.** Land this AFTER items 1/2/5 or you will hide the very decode failures those items are meant to eliminate behind a generic localized string — do the reporting half first so you can measure the before/after rate. Extract the `codingPath` by casting to `DecodingError` and switching on its cases (`.keyNotFound/.typeMismatch/.valueNotFound/.dataCorrupted` each carry a Context with `codingPath`); `error.localizedDescription` alone does NOT include the path, which is why the current logs are useless. Keep the raw detail in `os_log` — the existing decode-failure log at :223 is the only breadcrumb today. Apply the truncation and the `user/report` self-feed skip from item 7 to this new queueError call site too, and make sure the flush POST's own decode failure cannot queue a report.

**Acceptance** — Switch the app to Russian, stub any endpoint to return a 200 with `{"data": {"balance": {}}}`, and confirm the driver sees the localized server-error string, not English Foundation text. Then confirm a row appears in the error queue with responseCode 200 and a body prefixed `DECODE_FAIL@data.balance`, and that it uploads to `user/report` on the next flush.

### 12. Theming / system chrome / app shell (light-dark theme, in-place language switch, shared button + card styles, splash, nav chrome)

**Gaps:** 5 total — P0: 0 · P1: 2 · P2: 3

#### iOS today

Verified: iOS has a genuine theming + localization shell, and the claimed-state description holds up. `Persistence/ThemeManager.swift` stores Day/Night/System under `"app-theme"` and exposes `preferredColorScheme`; `MehrgoDriverApp.swift:98` applies it as `.preferredColorScheme` AND `MehrgoDriverApp.swift:106-109` pushes it onto every `UIWindow.overrideUserInterfaceStyle` via the private `applyInterfaceStyle(_:)` (line 135), so the UIKit nav-bar appearance proxy (configured lines 51-84 with an adaptive dynamic `UIColor` for titles) and hosted map views resolve the same trait — real, and better than a naive port. `Persistence/LanguageManager.swift` stores `"app-language"`, publishes `refreshToken: UUID`, mirrors to `AppleLanguages` (line 88, with a documented App Review Guideline 4 rejection behind it), adopts the device language on fresh install without writing `AppleLanguages`, exposes `preview(_:)` for live re-localization without committing, and `LocalizedAppString(_:)` for non-SwiftUI lookups; `ChooseLanguageView.onDisappear` (lines 69-76) does restore the persisted language on back-out, and `LanguageMVVM.apply()` correctly calls `user/change-language` FIRST and persists only on success. Shared styles exist as claimed: `Utilities/PrimaryButton.swift` (amber pill, r=14, 18pt padding, in-button `ProgressView`), `SecondaryButton.swift`, and `Shared/Extensions/View+Modifiers.swift` with a `dyn(light,dark)` helper (line 19), `cardStyle`/`orderCard`, `GroupedCard` (in `SettingsView.swift:368`), `SettingsIconChip`, `AndroidSectionHeader`, `ToastCenter`/`ToastHost` (mounted above `RootView` outside its `.id` so toasts survive rebuilds). The map claim is confirmed: `YandexMap(` is instantiated exactly ONCE in the whole app (`MapHomeView.swift:88`), `nightMode` is wired from `colorScheme == .dark` (line 116), and `MapHomeView.swift:30` is the ONLY `@Environment(\.colorScheme)` declaration anywhere in the repo — so there is no unstyled second map, but also no existing colorScheme plumbing to lean on elsewhere. Confirmed absent: no `whiteBlack`/`white_black` token exists; `Color(.systemBackground)` appears exactly 90 times across 34 files; `Info.plist` declares no `UIUserInterfaceStyle` (only `UIStatusBarStyleDefault`).

#### Android today

Android is fully dark-adapted and is the correct source of truth for every value in this report — I verified each claimed hex directly. `app/src/main/res/values/colors.xml` vs `values-night/colors.xml`: `white_black` #FFFFFF→#1B1E24 (values:38 / night:32), `gray_light_black_light` #F4F5F8→#0F1115 (values:39 / night:33), `gray_middle_black_middle` #E5E7EB→#2A2D33 (values:40 / night:34), `app_color` #F59E0B→#FBBF24 and `app_color_pressed` #D97706→#F59E0B (values:16-17 / night:12-13), `red` #EF4444→#F87171, `green` #10B981→#34D399, `blue_middle` #2563EB→#60A5FA, `amber_deep` #D97706→#F59E0B, `green_deep` #047857→#10B981, `gray_full` #6B7280→#9CA3AF, `black_middle` #374151→#D1D5DB, `black_white` →#F3F4F6. Soft washes do widen in dark: `app_color_soft` #1F→#26, and `green_soft`/`red_soft`/`blue_soft`/`amber_soft` are all #33 (20%) in night (night:38-41). The CTA contract is `color/text_submit_state.xml` (disabled=`gray_full`, enabled=`white_black`) over `drawable/bg_submit_state.xml` (disabled=`gray_middle_black_middle`, enabled=`app_color`, radius_lg) — confirming the enabled label really is dark slate in dark mode. Language switching is in-place: `presentation/main/ui/change_language/ChangeLanguageFragment.kt` calls the server first, then `LanguageManager.saveLanguage(newLang)` (:137) and `Activity.recreate()`, and its own comments (:110-159) document that the nav pop happens BEFORE the recreate, with `recreateWithCrossfade` (:159) and the map's `flMapLoadingMask` covering the GL rebuild so the driver lands back where they were rather than on a splash. `common/Localisation.kt` + `common/shared_pref/ThemeManager.kt` back the process-level locale/theme.

#### 12.1 `[P1]` Add a whiteBlack surface token and stop using Color(.systemBackground) for cards

**What to build**

CONFIRMED by count: `Color(.systemBackground)` appears exactly 90 times across 34 Swift files (plus 1 `secondarySystemBackground`), and no `whiteBlack`/`white_black` token exists anywhere in the repo. In dark mode `systemBackground` resolves to pure #000000 while `Color.canvasBackground` is `dyn(0xF4F5F8, 0x0D0F14)` (View+Modifiers.swift:46) — the card is DARKER than the page it sits on, inverting elevation. Android is the opposite and I verified the exact values: card `white_black` #FFFFFF/#1B1E24 over canvas `gray_light_black_light` #F4F5F8/#0F1115. Fix: in the `extension Color` block of View+Modifiers.swift add `static let whiteBlack: Color = dyn(0xFFFFFF, 0x1B1E24)` (note `dyn` is `private static` at line 19 — it is in the same extension so no access change is needed), then replace every card/sheet/row `Color(.systemBackground)` with `Color.whiteBlack`. Second half of the same bug confirmed at View+Modifiers.swift:120: `orderCard` uses `.shadow(color: Color.black.opacity(0.08), radius: 8, y: 2)`, invisible on a near-black canvas, so dark-mode list cards lose BOTH fill contrast and separation. IMPLEMENTATION TRAP found while verifying: `cardStyle`/`orderCard` are plain `func`s in an `extension View`, which CANNOT read `@Environment(\.colorScheme)` — the originally suggested `colorScheme == .dark ? 1 : 0` stroke will not compile there, and MapHomeView.swift:30 is the only `@Environment(\.colorScheme)` in the entire app. Either convert `orderCard` into a `ViewModifier` struct that owns the environment value, or (simpler, preferred) keep it a func and make the shadow colour itself `dyn`-driven so it is visible in light and effectively a no-op in dark, plus an unconditional `.overlay(RoundedRectangle(...).stroke(Color.grayMiddleBlack, lineWidth: 1))` — `grayMiddleBlack` is already `dyn(0xE5E7EB, 0x303845)`, so it is near-invisible in light and a real separator in dark. Also align `canvasBackground` dark 0x0D0F14 → Android's exact 0x0F1115.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/res/values/colors.xml:38-40 (white_black #FFFFFF, gray_light_black_light #F4F5F8, gray_middle_black_middle #E5E7EB) vs app/src/main/res/values-night/colors.xml:32-34 (#1B1E24, #0F1115, #2A2D33); memory note dark_theme_tokens.md

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/Extensions/View+Modifiers.swift:14-82 (add `whiteBlack`, fix `canvasBackground`), :101-106 (`cardStyle`), :115-121 (`orderCard`); MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Settings/SettingsView.swift:376 (`GroupedCard`); MehrgoDriver/Utilities/SlideToActButton.swift:43; MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Settings/ChooseLanguageView.swift:96 (headerCard); then sweep the remaining files (Auth/*, View/Main/Home/*, View/Main/Settings/*, View/Main/MyOrders/*, View/Main/Verification/VerificationView.swift, View/Root/LanguageView.swift, View/Root/IntroduceView.swift)

> **Gotchas.** View-extension funcs cannot read @Environment — see the trap above. Do NOT blind-replace all 90 hits: some `systemBackground` uses are full-screen page backgrounds where the canvas token is correct instead. `dyn` is `private static` inside `extension Color`, so new tokens must be declared in that same extension. Highest-density files are EarningsView.swift (8 hits), TripView.swift (9), OrderHistoryDetailView.swift (7), TripFinishView.swift (7), OrderOfferSheet.swift (6) — review those individually rather than sed-ing.

Every list card (My Orders, Notifications, available orders), settings group, and auth card must be VISIBLY LIGHTER than the page behind it, with a discernible edge between adjacent stacked cards. Compare side-by-side with the Android build in dark mode — the card/canvas relationship must match.

<a id="gap-12-2"></a>

#### 12.2 `[P1]` Language apply re-runs the full splash instead of returning the driver to Settings

**What to build**

CONFIRMED and MATERIALLY WORSE than claimed — hence P2→P1. Chain verified end to end: `ChooseLanguageView` (:53-63) awaits `vm.apply()`, which on success calls `LanguageManager.shared.set(selected)` (LanguageMVVM.swift:83); `set` bumps `refreshToken` (LanguageManager.swift:62); that re-identifies `RootView()` via `.id(languageManager.refreshToken)` (MehrgoDriverApp.swift:92). Note the ordering — `set` fires INSIDE `apply()`, i.e. BEFORE the `dismiss()` at :61, so the rebuild happens while the picker is still on screen. The rebuild constructs a fresh `@StateObject RootMVVM` (RootView.swift:15), whose `init()` calls `recompute()` (RootMVVM.swift:53-54), and `recompute()` sends an AUTHENTICATED driver to `route = .splash` (RootMVVM.swift:194). So changing the language does not merely drop the driver on the map home as originally claimed — it tears down the entire stack INCLUDING `MainView`'s `@StateObject HomeMVVM` (MainView.swift:24), re-runs SplashView, and blocks on the `mobile/version-driver` + `user/me` round trip before landing on `.main`. For a mid-shift driver with a trip panel open that is a multi-second blackout plus a full home-state rebuild, not a cosmetic misplacement. Fix in two steps: (a) make the language rebuild NOT go through splash — have `recompute()` skip `.splash` and go straight to `.main` when the session was already validated in this process (a `hasCompletedSplash` marker consulted by `recompute`), which alone removes the network stall and the white-splash flash from gap 4; (b) then restore the pushed route — persist the intended destination (a `pendingSettingsPath` on RootMVVM, or a `NavigationPath` in `@SceneStorage`) and re-push Settings after the rebuild so the driver lands where they left. If (b) is too invasive for this pass, ship (a) and re-push Settings as a single destination.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/main/ui/change_language/ChangeLanguageFragment.kt:110-164 — comments explicitly describe popping back BEFORE the recreate, `LanguageManager.saveLanguage` at :137, `recreateWithCrossfade { act.recreate() }` at :159, and the map's own flMapLoadingMask covering the GL rebuild; memory notes instant_locale_switch.md, recreate_crossfade.md

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MVVM/RootMVVM.swift:53-58 (`init` → `recompute`) and :168-197 (`recompute`, the `route = .splash` branch at :194); MehrgoDriver/MehrgoDriverApp.swift:92 (`.id(languageManager.refreshToken)`); MehrgoDriver/View/Root/RootView.swift:15,21,41; MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Settings/ChooseLanguageView.swift:53-63; MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Settings/SettingsView.swift:208 (the NavigationLink that pushes the picker)

> **Gotchas.** RootView.swift:36-40 carries a comment explicitly warning that `recompute()` must not also run on appear because it would 'bounce an authenticated user back through .splash' — the language-change path does exactly that via `init`, so whatever guard you add must not break the cold-launch path that comment protects. Do NOT try to fix this by moving `set()` after `dismiss()`; the rebuild still happens, just one frame later. `ToastHost` is deliberately mounted OUTSIDE the `.id` (MehrgoDriverApp.swift:96) so a failure toast survives — keep it there. `ChooseLanguageView.onDisappear` (:69-76) reverts the preview when `didCommit == false`; if you re-push Settings after a rebuild, make sure that revert cannot fire against the newly committed language.

**Acceptance** — Signed in with an order in progress, open Settings → Language, switch uz↔ru, tap Apply. The app must re-localize in place with NO splash screen and NO network stall, and must return the driver to the Settings screen (not the map home). With the network throttled, confirm the language change issues no `mobile/version-driver` or `user/me` request. Verify the active trip panel and its state survive.

#### 12.5 `[P2]` Splash renders invisible white-on-white status bar in Night theme

**What to build**

CONFIRMED on every input. SplashView.swift:31 fills the screen with a hard `Color.white`; Base.lproj/LaunchScreen.storyboard has `appearance="light"` (line 3) and `backgroundColor white="1"` (line 22); Info.plist declares NO `UIUserInterfaceStyle` and sets `UIStatusBarStyle = UIStatusBarStyleDefault`, i.e. glyphs follow the resolved trait. With a stored Night theme, MehrgoDriverApp has already set `.preferredColorScheme(.dark)` (:98) and `window.overrideUserInterfaceStyle = .dark` (:109 → :135-148), so status-bar glyphs render WHITE on the white splash. This is NOT a one-frame flash: SplashMVVM.start() spans the `mobile/version-driver` version check plus `user/me`. WORSE THAN CLAIMED, found while verifying gap 5: because a language change re-runs `RootMVVM.recompute()` which routes an authenticated driver back to `.splash` (RootMVVM.swift:53-54 and :187-195), this white splash also reappears mid-session on every language change, not only at cold launch. Fix: promote the existing `applyInterfaceStyle(_:)` at MehrgoDriverApp.swift:135 from `private static` to `static` and reuse it — `.onAppear { MehrgoDriverApp.applyInterfaceStyle(.Day) }` / `.onDisappear { MehrgoDriverApp.applyInterfaceStyle(ThemeManager.shared.current) }` on SplashView — rather than writing a second window walker. Preferred alternative (closer to Android, memory note splash_single_reload_mask.md): keep the theme trait and darken the splash instead — add a Dark variant to Assets.xcassets/Images/logoSplash.imageset and swap `Color.white` for `Color.whiteBlack` from gap 1.

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/View/Root/SplashView.swift:26-51 (the `Color.white` at :31); MehrgoDriver/MehrgoDriverApp.swift:135 (drop `private` from `applyInterfaceStyle`); optionally MehrgoDriver/Assets.xcassets/Images/logoSplash.imageset/Contents.json + MehrgoDriver/Base.lproj/LaunchScreen.storyboard:3,22

> **Gotchas.** LaunchScreen.storyboard is rendered by the OS BEFORE any app code runs — you cannot fix its trait from Swift. If you take the 'force light for the splash' route, storyboard and SplashView agree; if you take the 'dark splash' route the OS launch image stays white and you get a white→dark pop at handoff. SplashView's comment at :20-25 explicitly states it is pixel-matched to the storyboard, so whichever route you pick must change BOTH. `applyInterfaceStyle` is `@MainActor` — keep the annotation when changing access level. Restore the real theme in `onDisappear`, not in the `onReady` callback, or the forceUpdate/blockedApps routes leave the window pinned to light.

### 13. Push notifications / order offers / background delivery

**Gaps:** 7 total — P0: 0 · P1: 1 · P2: 6

#### iOS today

Verified: the iOS push stack is real and largely well-built. `MehrgoDriver/AppDelegate.swift` sets `UNUserNotificationCenter.current().delegate` and `Messaging.messaging().delegate` in `didFinishLaunching` (:39-42), registers exactly four categories in `registerNotificationCategories()` (:185-222) — `NEW_ORDER` and `NEW_PRIVATE_ORDER` both carrying `OPEN`(.foreground) + `SKIP`, plus `CANCEL_MY_ORDER` and `NEW_NOTIFICATION` — and forwards `willPresent` (:116-128) and `didReceive` with `response.actionIdentifier` (:132-143) into `Shared/PushNotificationCenter.swift`. `routeTap` (:115-156) handles `order_new`/`order_new_for_nurse` → `orderTapSubject`, `DOCUMENT_APPROVED`/`DOCUMENT_REJECTED` → `verificationChangedSubject` + `.verification`, `DRIVER_BONUS_CREDITED` → `.earnings`, `NEW_DRIVER_NOTIFICATION` → `.notification`. Token sync (`Persistence/FcmTokenManager.swift` + `syncToken()` POST `device-token/register`, PushNotificationCenter.swift:96-112) is at parity and is re-fired from LoginMVVM:103, SignUpVerifyMVVM:79, PasswordVerifyMVVM:73 — confirmed. Background delivery is solved the iOS way and is genuinely good: Info.plist:49-56 declares `location`, `audio`, `remote-notification`, `fetch`, `processing`; `LocationTracker.startWhileOnline()` (:199-209, with `allowsBackgroundLocationUpdates = true` at :112) keeps the process alive on shift so `WebSocketManager` keeps delivering; `HomeMVVM.postLocalNotificationIfBackgrounded` (:742-795) posts a `.timeSensitive`, `relevanceScore 1.0`, `.defaultRingtone` alert with pickup → dropoff → price and OPEN/SKIP. iOS correctly does NOT sheet on broadcast `order_new` (HomeMVVM.swift:664-688). Two claims in the incoming report were WRONG and are corrected below: (a) `order_new`/`order_new_for_nurse` are SOCKET keys on Android (Constants.kt:376-377) with no branch in `MyFirebaseMessagingService.buildTapIntent`, so there is no server FCM producer of offer pushes — the payload-typing bug is latent, not live; and (b) both `PushRoute` consumers (`MapHomeView.handlePushRoute` :634-652 and `RootMVVM.handlePushRoute` :241-244) DISCARD the `balanceId`/`id` associated values, so a mis-parsed id costs nothing today. Also verified: `HomeMVVM.skipOfferFromNotification` (:823-846) DOES exist and correctly calls `order/order-skip` with `LocationTracker.orderActionLocationQueryItems` — the defect is reachability, not the call. Verification refresh is partly self-healing: MapHomeView reloads verification on `willEnterForegroundNotification` (:250-251) and VerificationView does `.task { await vm.load() }` (:67).

#### Android today

Android's offer notifications are built from the WEBSOCKET, not FCM: `MyTrackingService.kt` creates the directed-offer notification with `.setTimeoutAfter(time)` where `time = socketOrderResponse.data.branch.acceptWaiting ?: 20` (:299, :1087) and the broadcast-pool one with `.setTimeoutAfter(10_000)` (:1140), and the overlay `AutoOfferService` self-stops on `CountDownTimer.onFinish`. `MyFirebaseMessagingService.onMessageReceived` (:95-120) fires for every message regardless of a `notification` block, always calls `showNotification`, and broadcasts `ACTION_VERIFICATION_STATUS_CHANGED` for `DOCUMENT_APPROVED`/`DOCUMENT_REJECTED`. `buildTapIntent` (:164-206) derives the key as `data["key"] ?: data["type"]` — the `?: data["type"]` exists solely for the Paylov withdrawal decision (`FCM_TYPE_PAYLOV_WITHDRAWAL = "paylov_withdrawal"`, Constants.kt:492), routed to `R.id.paylovHistoryFragment` (MainActivity.kt:585); it routes bonus/notification/document keys and has NO order_new branch. FCM data is a `Map<String,String>`, and Android forwards ids as Strings verbatim (`intent.putExtra(EXTRA_BALANCE_ID, it)`), which is direct proof the wire type is String. IMPORTANT correction to the incoming report: `resolveSoundResource()` (:214-217) looks up `res/raw/new_chat` — and `app/src/main/res/raw/` does NOT contain it (only audio_notification, audio_order, audio_order_cancel, audio_private_order, brand_icon, finished_track, service_disabled, service_enabled, started_track). Android therefore falls back to `R.raw.audio_notification`; it does NOT honour the backend's `new_chat.wav` today.

<a id="gap-13-1"></a>

#### 13.1 `[P1]` Make offer-notification OPEN/SKIP work when MainView is not mounted (cold launch / lock-screen SKIP)

**What to build**

CONFIRMED — two coupled holes, both verified in source. (a) `HomeMVVM.openOfferFromNotification(orderId:isPrivate:)` (HomeMVVM.swift:851-853) is literally `if pendingOffer?.id == orderId { return }` and nothing else — the doc comment above it promises "the recommended-orders feed will refresh" but no such code exists. Tapping an offer banner after the socket dropped, or from a cold start, does nothing at all. (b) `orderTapSubject` has exactly ONE subscriber, `MainView.swift:71`, and it is an unbuffered `PassthroughSubject`. Contrast `routeSubject`, which RootMVVM deliberately stashes for the `.splash → .main` cold-launch bridge (`subscribePushRoutes` :153-164 → `handlePushRoute` :241-244 → `consumePushRoute` :247-255, with a TTL guard). There is no equivalent for order taps, so a tap delivered before MainView mounts is emitted into the void. NOTE the claim that SKIP "never" reaches the server is too strong: when the app is merely backgrounded and MainView is still mounted, `.onReceive` fires and `skipOfferFromNotification` runs fine. The hole is cold launch and the background launch iOS performs for a non-foreground SKIP action. Fix in three parts: (1) Move the skip call out of the view layer — `HomeMVVM.skipOfferFromNotification` (:823-846) already performs `GET order/order-skip` with `LocationTracker.shared.orderActionLocationQueryItems` correctly, so lift that body into a `PushNotificationCenter.skipOrder(id:)` and call it directly from `routeTap` when `actionIdentifier == "SKIP"`, still emitting on `orderTapSubject` so a mounted HomeMVVM can clear `pendingOffer`. (2) Buffer the handoff: add `private(set) var pendingOrderTap: OrderNotificationTap?` set in `routeTap` alongside the send, plus a `consumeOrderTap()` with the same TTL discipline RootMVVM already uses; consume it from `MainView.task` (:66-70) on mount. (3) Implement `openOfferFromNotification`: if `pendingOffer?.id == orderId` return; otherwise re-fetch `order/list` from HomeMVVM itself and call the existing `presentOfferFromList(order)`.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/services/AutoOfferService.kt (overlay accept/skip works with no Activity mounted); app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/services/MyTrackingService.kt:299, :1087 (offer + acceptWaiting)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/PushNotificationCenter.swift (routeTap :115-156, add skipOrder + pendingOrderTap); MehrgoDriver/MVVM/HomeMVVM.swift (openOfferFromNotification :851-853, skipOfferFromNotification :823-846); MehrgoDriver/View/Main/MainView.swift:66-78

> **Gotchas.** `presentOfferFromList` has signature `func presentOfferFromList(_ order: Order)` (HomeMVVM.swift:198) — it takes NO `hasCountdown` argument and already hardcodes `pendingOfferHasCountdown = false` at :209, so pass the order only. Do NOT plan to read `MapMVVM.recommendedOrders`: MapMVVM is view-owned (`@StateObject private var vm: MapMVVM = MapMVVM()`, MapHomeView.swift:27), not a singleton, and HomeMVVM has no reference to it — HomeMVVM must hit `order/list` itself (there is no single-order endpoint in the repo; the only order paths are list/my-orders/history/accept/order-skip/start/complete/cancel). Never present the re-fetched offer WITH a countdown: the original accept window's remaining time is unknowable at tap time and a fake ring will let the driver Accept into a server rejection. Copy RootMVVM's TTL guard onto the buffered tap or a tap stashed in a prior session will pop an offer sheet for a long-dead order. The SKIP action is registered WITHOUT `.foreground` (AppDelegate.swift:191-195), so the app may be launched into the background to service it — the skip network call must not depend on any SwiftUI view existing, which is exactly why part (1) is required.

**Acceptance** — Force-quit the app, have the backend/socket push a directed offer so the local notification lands, then from the lock screen tap SKIP: assert `order/order-skip?order_id=…&lat=…&long=…` hits the server (proxy log or backend log) and the offer is released to the next driver. Repeat with OPEN from a force-quit state and assert the offer sheet appears with the correct order and NO countdown ring. Warm case: background the app, tap OPEN, confirm the existing sheet still appears (no double-present).

<a id="gap-13-2"></a>

#### 13.2 `[P2]` Coerce FCM data-payload values leniently (String/NSNumber), not raw as? Int / as? Bool

**What to build**

CONFIRMED PRESENT, BUT THE CLAIMED IMPACT WAS WRONG — do not sell this as P0. `routeTap` reads `userInfo["order_id"] as? Int` (PushNotificationCenter.swift:121), `userInfo["is_private"] as? Bool` (:122), `userInfo["balance_id"] as? Int` (:145), `userInfo["notify_driver_id"] as? Int` (:148). FCM/APNs delivers the `data` dictionary as STRINGS, so all four would nil out on a server push. What I verified changes the severity twice over: (1) there is NO server producer of an offer push — `order_new`/`order_new_for_nurse` are socket keys on Android (Constants.kt:376-377) and `MyFirebaseMessagingService.buildTapIntent` has no branch for them; the only producer of that payload is iOS's own `postLocalNotificationIfBackgrounded` (HomeMVVM.swift:764-768) which puts real Ints, so the offer branch works today. (2) The bonus/notification routes ALREADY WORK — both consumers of `PushRoute` (`MapHomeView.handlePushRoute` :634-652 and `RootMVVM.handlePushRoute` :241-244) ignore the associated values entirely (`case .notification: showNotifications = true`), so `balanceId: 0` / `id: 0` opens the correct screen with no visible defect. This is therefore latent hardening, not a live break. Still worth doing because it is six lines and it is a prerequisite for two things already on the roadmap (deep-linking to a specific balance row / notification, and any backend change that starts pushing offers over FCM — at which point this silently becomes a missed-offer P0). Add to PushNotificationCenter: `private func int(_ v: Any?) -> Int? { (v as? Int) ?? (v as? NSNumber)?.intValue ?? Int((v as? String) ?? "") }` and `private func bool(_ v: Any?) -> Bool? { (v as? Bool) ?? (v as? NSNumber)?.boolValue ?? (v as? String).map { ["1","true","yes"].contains($0.lowercased()) } }`. Keep `as? Int` first so the local-notification path is bit-identical. Then `guard let orderId = int(userInfo["order_id"])`, `let isPrivate = bool(userInfo["is_private"]) ?? (key == Constants.socketOrderNewPrivate)`, `balanceId = int(userInfo["balance_id"]) ?? 0`, `notifyId = int(userInfo["notify_driver_id"]) ?? 0`.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/services/MyFirebaseMessagingService.kt:164-206 (data is Map<String,String>; ids forwarded as String extras); app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/Constants.kt:376-377 (order_new is a SOCKET key), :482-488 (FCM keys)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/PushNotificationCenter.swift (routeTap, lines 115-156)

> **Gotchas.** Do NOT delete the `as? Int` fast path — iOS's own local notification puts genuine Ints in `content.userInfo` and that is the only path exercised in production today; a String-only parser would regress the one thing that currently works. `is_private` must keep its `?? (key == Constants.socketOrderNewPrivate)` fallback: the private-offer notification is identified by category/key, not by the flag. Note also that fixing the ids alone changes nothing user-visible until `handlePushRoute` actually consumes them.

**Acceptance** — Send a test FCM data-only push with `{"key":"DRIVER_BONUS_CREDITED","balance_id":"73440"}` (string) via the Firebase console / curl to a device build; tap it and assert the os_log line reads `routing tap to earnings(balanceId: 73440)` rather than `balanceId: 0`. Repeat with `order_id` as a string and confirm the log shows `order tap (id=73440...)`. Then background the app, let a real socket offer post the local notification, tap it, and confirm the Int path still logs the correct id (no regression).

<a id="gap-13-3"></a>

#### 13.3 `[P2]` Withdraw stale offer notifications when the accept window expires or the order is taken

**What to build**

CONFIRMED — grepped the entire iOS repo for `removeDeliveredNotifications`, `removePendingNotificationRequests`, `removeAllDelivered` and `withIdentifiers`: ZERO hits. `postLocalNotificationIfBackgrounded` posts with identifier `"offer-\(order.id)"` (HomeMVVM.swift:786) and nothing ever removes it, so a missed 20 s offer sits in Notification Center indefinitely; tapping it later hits the currently-empty open path (gap above), so the driver gets silence. Android auto-expires both offer notifications with `.setTimeoutAfter`: `acceptWaiting` seconds for the directed offer (MyTrackingService.kt:299 resolves `branch.acceptWaiting ?: 20`, applied at :1087) and a flat 10 000 ms for the broadcast-pool one (:1140). Add `UNUserNotificationCenter.current().removeDeliveredNotifications(withIdentifiers: ["offer-\(id)"])` plus `removePendingNotificationRequests(withIdentifiers:)` in three places in HomeMVVM: (1) scheduled right after the `add(req)` completion, at `.now() + Double(order.branch?.acceptWaiting ?? Constants.defaultAcceptWaitTime)` — use 10 s for the `isPrivate == false` broadcast case to match Android; (2) inside `handle(_:)` (:664-739) on `.accepted` / `.cancelled` / `.cancelledPrivate` when `event.order.id` matches; (3) in `clearOffer()` (:178) and `handleAccepted(_:)` (:289).

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/services/MyTrackingService.kt:299 (acceptWaiting ?: 20), :1087 (setTimeoutAfter(time)), :1140 (setTimeoutAfter(10_000))

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MVVM/HomeMVVM.swift (postLocalNotificationIfBackgrounded :742-795, handle(_:) :664-739, clearOffer :178, handleAccepted :289)

> **Gotchas.** `Constants.defaultAcceptWaitTime` is 20 (Constants.swift:52) and `order.branch?.acceptWaiting` is optional — mirror the same fallback OrderOfferMVVM.init uses (:51) so the notification and the sheet ring expire together. Deliver the removal off a cancellable work item, not a bare `asyncAfter` closure capturing self, or a second offer arriving inside the window will have its notification pulled by the first offer's stale timer if ids ever collide. The `.accepted` socket event is also this driver's own accept echo, so keying the removal on `event.order.id` is required (an unconditional clear would nuke a concurrently-arriving second offer's banner). Removing a delivered notification does NOT dismiss an already-expanded banner, only the Notification Center entry — that is acceptable and matches setTimeoutAfter.

**Acceptance** — Background the app, let a directed offer arrive, do nothing for `acceptWaiting + 5` s, then open Notification Center: the `offer-<id>` entry must be gone. Separately, let an offer arrive, accept it from inside the app, and confirm the banner disappears from Notification Center immediately. Third case: let an offer arrive and have the rider cancel it — the entry must vanish on the `order_cancelled` socket event.

<a id="gap-13-4"></a>

#### 13.4 `[P2]` Give server-sent pushes a branded sound instead of the system default

**What to build**

PREMISE OF THE ORIGINAL CLAIM WAS FALSE — rewrite before it goes in the plan. The report said "Android honours the backend's new_chat.wav". It does not: `resolveSoundResource()` (MyFirebaseMessagingService.kt:214-217) does `resources.getIdentifier("new_chat", "raw", packageName)` and `app/src/main/res/raw/` contains NO new_chat file (verified: audio_notification, audio_order, audio_order_cancel, audio_private_order, brand_icon, finished_track, service_disabled, service_enabled, started_track). Android therefore always falls back to `R.raw.audio_notification`. So do NOT bundle `new_chat.caf` and do NOT ask the backend to rename to `new_chat.caf` — you would be building to a file neither platform ships. The residual, real (and small) delta: Android sets a custom channel sound (`audio_notification`) for every server push, while on iOS the APNs `sound` name the backend sends resolves against nothing in the bundle (`MehrgoDriver/Sounds/` holds audio_notification.mp3, audio_order.mp3, audio_private_order.mp3, service_disabled.mp3, service_enabled.mp3, started_track.mp3), so server pushes play the generic system ding. Correct fix: convert the existing `audio_notification` to `.caf` (`afconvert -f caff -d LEI16 audio_notification.mp3 audio_notification.caf`), add it as a bundled resource in `project.yml`, and have the backend send `"sound": "audio_notification.caf"` in the APNS block. Decide with the backend owner whether `new_chat` is a real asset that was never delivered to either app — if it is, ship it to BOTH platforms in the same change instead of iOS-only.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/services/MyFirebaseMessagingService.kt:209-217 (resolveSoundResource + fallback); app/src/main/res/raw/ (no new_chat present)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Sounds/ (add audio_notification.caf), project.yml (resource entry). No Swift change — APNs resolves the sound by filename.

> **Gotchas.** APNs matches the FULL filename including extension: `"sound":"audio_notification"` will NOT resolve `audio_notification.caf`. Sounds must be at the bundle root or in Library/Sounds and under 30 s or iOS silently substitutes the default. This does not affect the ride-offer path at all — that uses `content.sound = .defaultRingtone` on a LOCAL notification (HomeMVVM.swift:781) and is already louder/better than Android; scope this strictly to server-sent pushes. Note `SoundPlayer` already prefers `.caf` over `.mp3` (Sounds lookup, SoundPlayer.swift:128), so adding the .caf is consistent with the existing convention.

**Acceptance** — Send a real server push (bonus or notification) to a device with the app backgrounded and the ringer on; confirm the branded tone plays instead of the default ding. Verify the file is actually in the built .app (`unzip -l` the .ipa / inspect the bundle) — a project.yml entry that silently fails to copy is the usual failure mode here.

<a id="gap-13-5"></a>

#### 13.5 `[P2]` Handle data-only (silent) pushes via didReceiveRemoteNotification

**What to build**

CONFIRMED ABSENT (grepped the whole repo for `didReceiveRemoteNotification`, `content-available`, `contentAvailable`: zero hits in Swift), BUT IMPACT IS SMALLER THAN CLAIMED — downgrade from P1. AppDelegate implements only `willPresent` (:116-128) and `didReceive` (:132-143), so a `content-available: 1` data-only push never reaches app code. Android's `onMessageReceived` (:95-120) fires for every message and broadcasts `ACTION_VERIFICATION_STATUS_CHANGED` for DOCUMENT_APPROVED/REJECTED with no user interaction. Why this is P2 and not P1: iOS already self-heals on return — `MapHomeView` calls `vm.loadVerification()` on `UIApplication.willEnterForegroundNotification` (:250-251) and `VerificationView` does `.task { await vm.load() }` (:67), so the chip and screen are correct the moment the driver looks at them; a background refresh only updates state nobody is currently viewing. Also unverified whether the backend sends `content-available` at all — Android would show a generic notification for such a push, and no code in either repo proves the backend emits silent pushes. CONFIRM WITH BACKEND FIRST; if it emits no data-only pushes this item is dead code. If it does, add:

```swift
func application(_ application: UIApplication,
                 didReceiveRemoteNotification userInfo: [AnyHashable: Any],
                 fetchCompletionHandler completionHandler: @escaping (UIBackgroundFetchResult) -> Void) {
    Task { @MainActor in
        PushNotificationCenter.shared.handleForegroundArrival(userInfo: userInfo)
        completionHandler(.newData)
    }
}
```

and rename `handleForegroundArrival` (PushNotificationCenter.swift:162-168) to `handleSilentArrival` since it would then serve background arrivals too. `UIBackgroundModes` already contains `remote-notification` (Info.plist:53) — verified — so no plist change.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/services/MyFirebaseMessagingService.kt:95-120 (onMessageReceived fires for every message; broadcasts ACTION_VERIFICATION_STATUS_CHANGED)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/AppDelegate.swift (add the delegate method next to willPresent :116); MehrgoDriver/Shared/PushNotificationCenter.swift:162-168

> **Gotchas.** iOS throttles background delivery aggressively (Low Power Mode, low-usage apps, and Background App Refresh disabled all suppress it) — never make a correctness path depend on this firing; it is a nice-to-have on top of the foreground refetch, not a replacement for it. The completion handler must be invoked exactly once and within ~30 s or the app is penalised. `handleForegroundArrival` currently only branches on the document keys; if you widen its remit be careful it never presents UI from a background launch. Do not silently make this method the offer path — offers arrive over the socket, not FCM.

**Acceptance** — With backend confirmation that silent pushes are emitted: background the app, have moderation approve a document, and assert the os_log `foreground/silent document decision → verification refresh` line appears while the app is still backgrounded, and that opening the app shows the updated chip with no additional network round-trip.

CONFIRMED — `routeTap` derives the key exclusively from `userInfo["key"] as? String` (PushNotificationCenter.swift:119), and a repo-wide grep for `paylov`/`Paylov`/`PAYLOV`/`withdraw`/`Withdraw` across all Swift returns ZERO hits, so the feature is entirely absent on iOS. Android reads `data["key"] ?: data["type"]` (MyFirebaseMessagingService.kt:171) with an explicit comment that the Paylov withdrawal decision arrives WITHOUT a `key`, as `{ type: "paylov_withdrawal", request_id, status }`, and routes it to `R.id.paylovHistoryFragment` (MainActivity.kt:585) via `Constants.FCM_TYPE_PAYLOV_WITHDRAWAL` (Constants.kt:492). Minimum fix (do this regardless, it is three lines): change the derivation to `let key = (userInfo["key"] as? String) ?? (userInfo["type"] as? String) ?? ""`, add `static let pushTypePaylovWithdrawal: String = "paylov_withdrawal"` to `Shared/Constants.swift` in the `// MARK: - APNs payload keys` block (:110-117, next to pushKeyDriverBonusCredited/pushKeyNewDriverNotification/pushKeyDocumentApproved/pushKeyDocumentRejected), and add a `case paylovWithdrawal` to the `PushRoute` enum (:173-178) plus a branch in the route switch (:142-153). The full fix needs the Paylov withdrawal-history screen, which does not exist on iOS; until it is ported, map `paylovWithdrawal` to the existing Earnings screen rather than letting it default to `.home`.

> **Gotchas.** `type` is ALSO used as a document-type field on the document pushes (Android's `FCM_KEY_DATA_DOCUMENT_TYPE = "type"`, Constants.kt:488) — so the `?? type` fallback must stay strictly a FALLBACK evaluated only when `key` is absent, exactly as Android does; reading `type` first would misroute every document decision. Adding a case to `PushRoute` breaks exhaustiveness in BOTH switch consumers (`MapHomeView.handlePushRoute` :634-652 and any RootMVVM handling) — update both or the build fails. Since neither consumer reads `PushRoute` associated values today, do not bother threading `request_id` through until the screen exists. This is only worth doing at all if the backend actually targets iOS devices with Paylov pushes — confirm the withdrawal feature is live for iOS drivers before spending the effort.

<a id="gap-13-7"></a>

#### 13.7 `[P2]` Drive the offer countdown from a wall-clock deadline (and consider a Live Activity for the lock screen)

**What to build**

CONFIRMED — (a) `OrderOfferMVVM` sets `remaining = hasCountdown ? waiting : 0` in `init` (:47-60) and decrements it from a 1 Hz `Timer.scheduledTimer` in `startTimer()` (:150-163); there is no `Date` anywhere in the file (grepped: no `deadline`, no `Date()`, no `scenePhase`, no `timerInterval`). If the process is suspended — Low Power Mode, location updates paused, or an offer sheet up when the app suspends — the timer stops and `remaining` freezes, so on resume the driver sees e.g. "14 s left" on a window the server closed minutes ago and Accept then fails server-side. Fix: store `private let deadline: Date = Date().addingTimeInterval(TimeInterval(waiting))` in `init` and compute `remaining = max(0, Int(deadline.timeIntervalSinceNow.rounded(.up)))` on every tick AND on `scenePhase == .active`, auto-skipping at 0 through the existing `skipped = true` path. (b) The backgrounded notification body carries pickup/dropoff/price but NO time information (HomeMVVM.swift:750-762), so a driver on the lock screen cannot see how much of the window is left; Android shows a depleting ring over other apps via the overlay. iOS has no system-overlay equivalent, and the correct mechanism is a Live Activity — the infrastructure is already in the project and working (`WaitWidget/WaitActivityWidget.swift` with `ActivityConfiguration(for: WaitActivityAttributes.self)` :31, `Shared/WaitActivityAttributes.swift`, `Shared/WaitActivityManager.swift` with request/update/end at :89/:108/:153). Add an `OfferActivityAttributes` with a `ClosedRange<Date>` and render `Text(timerInterval:countsDown:)` so the lock screen counts down live; start it in `postLocalNotificationIfBackgrounded` and end it on accept/skip/expiry.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/services/MyTrackingService.kt:299, :1087 (acceptWaiting-driven timeout); app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/services/AutoOfferService.kt (CountDownTimer ring + stopSelf on finish)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MVVM/OrderOfferMVVM.swift (init :47-60, startTimer :150-163) for (a); WaitWidget/WaitActivityWidget.swift + MehrgoDriver/Shared/WaitActivityAttributes.swift + MehrgoDriver/Shared/WaitActivityManager.swift + HomeMVVM.postLocalNotificationIfBackgrounded :742-795 for (b)

> **Gotchas.** `remaining` is load-bearing beyond the ring: the sheet's dismiss guard is `remaining > 0`, and the no-countdown path deliberately keeps it at 0 so the sheet is dismissible (see the comment at :52-55) — a deadline computed unconditionally in `init` would trap the from-list sheet open. Compute the deadline ONLY when `hasCountdown` is true. `deinit` invalidates the timer and stops the player (:63-66); keep that. Part (b) is a genuinely separate, larger piece of work — split it into its own ticket rather than bundling it with the four-line deadline fix, and check the deployment target supports ActivityKit before committing (WaitActivityManager already gates this, follow its pattern). A Live Activity started from a background socket arrival is subject to the same process-alive assumption as everything else here — it will not start if the app was killed.

**Acceptance** — For (a): let a directed offer arrive, background the app for 30 s past `acceptWaiting`, foreground it — the sheet must be gone (auto-skipped) rather than showing a stale positive count. Also verify the from-list offer sheet (hasCountdown false) still has no ring and can be swiped away. For (b): with the app backgrounded and an offer live, the lock screen must show a counter that visibly decrements without unlocking, and must disappear on accept, skip, or expiry.

## 9. Detailed gap reports — pass 2 (surfaces the first pass did not cover)

Pass 1 analysed the deep systems (fare, socket, meter, crash-safety). It did not cover the screen-level surfaces below, because the areas were chosen by hand and an agent cannot find what it was never pointed at. Pass 2 closes that, using the same adversarial method.
