### 15. Order pool — available/recommended orders, orders-on-map browse, and locally-persisted (Room/Core Data) order records

**Gaps:** 4 total — P0: 0 · P1: 1 · P2: 3

#### iOS today

VERIFIED as described. `MapMVVM.recommendedOrders` (MVVM/MapMVVM.swift:25) is the single source of truth: seeded by `loadRecommended()` (:75-98, `guard isOnline else { recommendedOrders = [] }` at :83 and re-checked post-await at :97), maintained incrementally in `subscribeSockets()` (:291-314 — `removeAll {$0.id == …}` at :298/:300, dedupe+`insert(at: 0)` at :306-308), resynced by `subscribeSocketStatus()` (:322+), and wiped on `goOffline` (:281). It renders in View/Main/Home/RecommendedOrdersView.swift (sheet at MapHomeView.swift:242, `ordersButton` + live red count badge at :684-708, `.refreshable`, two empty states `no_active_orders` / `start_work_first`), rows via View/Main/MyOrders/MyOrderRow.swift (call site :106), with driver→pickup road distance from Shared/PickupDistanceStore.swift; tap → `HomeMVVM.presentOfferFromList` (:196-213, busy-trip pre-gate + 350 ms deferral + `hasCountdown = false`). `MapHomeView.mapPlacemarks` (:349-352) confirmed to return pins ONLY for `homeVM.activeOrder` — no pool pins; note Android has no orders-on-map surface either (`fragment_orders_map.xml` is a plain RecyclerView, `rvOrders` at :111 — `OrdersMapFragment` is misnamed), so that is a non-gap on both sides. Persistence/CalculationStore.swift exposes only `upsert/get/appendLocations/setTrackedTime/setWaitedTimeUntilGone/setWaitState/setDistance/delete` with a private per-order `fetch(orderId:)` (:180) — no fetch-all. IMPORTANT CORRECTION for the next pass (not filed as a gap here, per scope): Android DOES ship a locally-persisted browsable order list that iOS lacks — `CalculationsDao.getCalculations(): Flow<List<Calculation>>` (CalculationsDao.kt:22) → `GetCalculationsUC` → `presentation/main/ui/orders_in_locale/OrdersInLocaleFragment.kt`, reachable in a default install from BOTH SettingsFragment.kt:195 and MapSettingsFragment.kt:156. Localization ships only uz + ru `Localizable.strings` (MehrgoDriver/Localization/{uz,ru}.lproj); en.lproj contains InfoPlist.strings only.

#### Android today

The broadcast pool is `GET order/list`-backed and rendered by `OrderAdapter` against `adapter_order.xml`, which carries the extras iOS omits: `tvAddPrice`/`ivAddPrice` (:90/:99), `vExtrasDivider` (:317), `tvInfo` (:326) and `chipGroup` (:339). The driver-created taximeter order is real and reachable by default: `mcvTachometer` exists in BOTH `fragment_home.xml:205` (HomeFragment.kt:281) and `fragment_orders_map.xml:133` (OrdersMapFragment.kt:112), each gated on `MyTrackingService.isServiceRunning.value == true` else `pls_start_job`; it fetches `GET tarif?expand=name_with_group&is_taximeter=1` (ApiService.kt:377), shows `DialogBshChooseTariffBinding` with `setCreateEnabled(selectedTariff != null)` (OrdersMapFragment.kt:405-419), and POSTs `order-new/create` (ApiService.kt:382) with a single driver-position location and `wait_taking_your_location` guard (:359-393). `Tariff` (domain/model/Tariff.kt) is a FOUR-field catalog shape — `{id, name, @SerializedName("starting_price") startingPrice: Int, icon}` — not the order-embedded tariff. The district demand board is `GET order/new-address` (ApiService.kt:176) → `OrderAddressUC` → `OrderAddressAdapter`, whose model is `OrderAddress(id, name, orders: String, cars: Int, deliveries: Int)`; it is wired only into HomeFragment (`getOrderAddress()` at :293), and HomeFragment immediately redirects to MapFragment (HomeFragment.kt:133-134) — so a default install never renders it.

(3) `OrderCreateRequest.price`/`startingPrice`/`distance` are `Double` on iOS but `Int`/`Double` on Android — JSONEncoder will emit `9000` for a whole Double, so the wire shape matches, but verify against the live backend before shipping. Rows render through `struct HistoryRow` at `View/Main/MyOrders/MyOrderRow.swift:207`, and `View/Main/MyOrders/OrderHistoryDetailView.swift` is pushed from `HistoryView.swift:62` — a rich detail screen (heroCard/routeCard/statsCard/detailsCard/servicesCard fetched from `service/order`, breakdownCard, dispatcherCard, noteCard, orderIdFooter). CONFIRMED ORPHANED: `grep -rn "MyOrdersView" --include=*.swift` over all 128 Swift files returns only `MyOrdersView.swift:2` (comment) and `:12` (the `struct` declaration); it is compiled into the target (4 hits in `MehrgoDriver.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj`) but nothing presents it. `MyOrdersMVVM` is referenced only from that dead view (`MyOrdersView.swift:15`). Secondary surfaces are sheets off `MapHomeView` (`showSettings`/`showNotifications`/`showEarnings`/`showRecommendedOrders` at :45-49, presented :230-254, deep-link reset :638-643). `MyOrdersMVVM` contains no `WebSocketManager`/Combine subscription of any kind (every `orderSubject` consumer in the repo: HomeMVVM:650, MapMVVM:292, TripMVVM:927). `OrderHistoryDetailView` never reads `item.date` — grep for `date` in that file matches exactly one line, the stale `// MARK: - Hero (price + status + date)` comment at :92.

#### Android today

Android ships a real 3-tab My Orders host: `presentation/main/ui/my_orders/MyOrdersFragment.kt` + `MyOrdersStateTabAdapter.kt` + `MyOrdersViewModel.kt`, with `fragments/ActiveMyOrdersFragment.kt`, `fragments/FinishedMyOrdersFragment.kt` and `fragments/CanceledOrdersFragment.kt`, reachable from the bottom nav. `ActiveMyOrdersFragment.kt` fetches via `getActiveMyOrders()` (:146, :188, and again at :704) and keeps the list live off the socket: `orderDataReceiver` (:110-120) re-fetches when `response.key == ORDER_CANCELLED_PRIVATE` (:115-117) and `socketListenerReceiver` (:100-108) re-fetches on socket reconnect, both registered in `onStart` (:149-158) against `ACTION_SEND_ORDER_DATA_BY_BROADCAST` / `ACTION_SEND_SOCKET_LISTENER_BY_BROADCAST` and unregistered in `onStop` (:160-164). History detail lives separately at `presentation/main/ui/orders_history/OrderHistoryDetailFragment.kt`, which puts the trip timestamp in the hero via `tvDate.text = item.date.datetime.replace("y ", " ")` (:57). Android's history list itself has no period filter — that capability exists only on iOS.

#### 16.1 `[P0]` Row tap in MyOrdersView creates a SECOND TripMVVM for an order the trip panel already owns — duplicate fare ticker, duplicate Live Activity, conflicting LocationTracker state

**What to build**

Kept at P0 — this is a fare/GPS data-integrity defect, not just a UI bug. `MyOrdersView.swift:45` declares `.navigationDestination(item: $tripOrder) { TripView(order: $0) }` and the Active row Button at :121-137 sets `tripOrder = order`. `TripView.init(order:)` builds a FRESH `TripMVVM` via `_vm = StateObject(wrappedValue: TripMVVM(order: order))` (TripView.swift:17/35-37). `TripMVVM` drives process-wide singletons and timers from that instance: `LocationTracker.shared.markOrderStarted()` (:247, :422), `markArrivedAtPickup()` (:261, :471), `startWaiting()` (:262, :479), `WaitActivityManager.shared.start(...)` (:267, :485), `GpsBatchUploader.shared.flushNow()` (:626), a 1 s `fareTimer` (:143, scheduled :1187), and its own `WebSocketManager.shared.orderSubject` + `fareUpdateSubject` subscriptions (:926-927, :986) started from `init` via `subscribeSockets()` (:158). Meanwhile `MainView.swift:59-63` ALREADY renders `TripPanel(order:).id(order.id)` for `HomeMVVM.activeOrder`, and `TripPanel` builds its own `TripView(order:)` at :228. Tapping the same order in the list therefore yields two live TripMVVMs: two fare tickers advancing the same meter, two `WaitActivityManager.start` calls, two socket consumers acting on the same `fareUpdateSubject`/order events, and interleaved `LocationTracker` state transitions. FIX: never push TripView from a list — route the tap through the single existing owner. Give `MyOrdersView` an `onSelectOrder: (Order) -> Void` (or an `@EnvironmentObject var homeVM: HomeMVVM`), have the row Button call it after the existing `LocationTracker.shared.isTracking` guard (:126-130), and wire it at the presenter as `{ homeVM.activeOrder = $0; showMyOrders = false }` — `MainView`'s `.id(order.id)` at :61 already guarantees a fresh VM per order. Then DELETE `@State private var tripOrder: Order?` (:17) and the `navigationDestination` at :45.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/main/ui/my_orders/fragments/ActiveMyOrdersFragment.kt — the active-row tap navigates to the single map/trip destination rather than constructing a parallel trip controller

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/View/Main/MyOrders/MyOrdersView.swift:17 (`tripOrder` state), :45 (`navigationDestination`), :121-137 (Active row Button); presenter wiring in MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Home/MapHomeView.swift

> **Gotchas.** REFUTED SUB-CLAIM: the original report predicted a hard `fatalError` from a missing `@EnvironmentObject homeVM` (TripView.swift:24, read at :44). That will NOT happen on the sheet-from-MapHomeView path — MainView injects HomeMVVM onto its entire ZStack at MainView.swift:151 and SwiftUI sheets inherit the presenter's environment, so MyOrdersView and anything it pushes already resolve it. The explicit `.environmentObject(vm)` at MainView.swift:99 is belt-and-braces, not evidence of a broken chain. Do not justify this fix on a crash — the duplicate-singleton corruption is the real reason. Also, `TripView` is written to be hosted by `TripPanel` (its resize/tap hot-zone lives in the parent — see comments at TripView.swift:169 and :328 and MainView.swift:230-240), so pushing it into a NavigationStack is architecturally wrong even ignoring the VM duplication.

**Acceptance** — Tapping an active order in My Orders dismisses the sheet and raises the existing trip panel for that order; instrumenting `TripMVVM.init`/`deinit` shows exactly one live instance at all times; `LocationTracker` waiting state and the fare meter advance at the normal single rate; `WaitActivityManager.start` is called once per waiting session.

### 17. Notifications inbox (in-app list + detail) — read/unread, badges, pagination, live socket insertion, push deep-link

**Gaps:** 1 total — P0: 0 · P1: 0 · P2: 1

#### iOS today

CONFIRMED at parity or ahead. `MVVM/NotificationsMVVM.swift:43-47` GETs `notification/index` into `BaseResponse<Array<DriverNotification>>`, and its `init` (:28-34) subscribes to `WebSocketManager.shared.notificationSubject` so a `notification_new` frame (published at `Shared/WebSocketManager.swift:168-175`, key from `Shared/Constants.swift:72`) is inserted at index 0 via `prepend` (:59-69) — Android has no equivalent live insertion. `View/Main/Notifications/NotificationsView.swift` adds a 5-row `loadingPlaceholder` skeleton, a localized `EmptyStateView` ("no_notifications"/"no_notifications_subtitle"), `.refreshable`, and `.navigationDestination(item:)` passing the whole `DriverNotification` into `NotificationDetailView`, whose `bodyText` decodes HTML via `NSAttributedString` and then normalizes font/colour (Android's `Html.fromHtml` keeps default styling); `Shared/Models/SharedModels.swift:1031` + the custom decoder at :1064-1085 tolerate either `image` or Android's `photo_url`, and `strippedHistoryDate` normalizes the "2026y" artefact. Entry point is `notificationsButton` in `View/Main/Home/MapHomeView.swift:710-717`, opening the sheet bound at :230. IMPORTANT CORRECTION to the incoming report's premise: the FCM push path is NOT missing on iOS — `Shared/PushNotificationCenter.swift:147-149` maps `Constants.pushKeyNewDriverNotification` ("NEW_DRIVER_NOTIFICATION", `Constants.swift:113`) with `notify_driver_id` to `PushRoute.notification(id:)`, `AppDelegate.swift:211-215` registers a `NEW_NOTIFICATION` category with an OPEN action, and `AppDelegate.swift:116-128` returns `[.banner, .sound, .list, .badge]` from `willPresent`, so a server FCM banners even in the foreground. Both platforms discard the carried id and merely open the list (`MapHomeView.swift:647` `case .notification: showNotifications = true` vs Android `MainActivity.kt:581 -> R.id.navigation_notifications`), so deep-link depth is at parity — the spec's "pre-select notify_driver_id" (`Mehrgo_Driver_iOS_Spec.md:1947`) is unimplemented on BOTH. Confirmed iOS-only dead code: `View/Main/Common/NotificationBadge.swift` is defined and referenced nowhere (the red capsule at `MapHomeView.swift:694-704` is the ORDERS badge, hand-rolled, not this component), and `HomeMVVM.swift:23` `unreadNotifications` is bumped at :658 but never rendered and never reset; `NotificationsMVVM.prepend` also has no id-dedupe, so a socket replay would duplicate `Identifiable` ids in the `ForEach`. Neither platform implements read/unread state or pagination.

#### Android today

CONFIRMED. `presentation/main/ui/notifications/NotificationsFragment.kt` (290 lines) + `NotificationsViewModel.kt` (38 lines) do a single unpaginated `notification/index` fetch (`data/remote/ApiService.kt:321`) into a `NotificationAdapter` list — no pull-to-refresh, no skeleton, no read/unread flag, and critically NO socket subscription in the list itself (the fragment only observes `MySocketListener.isSocketListener` for the offline icon at :64-71), so a `notification_new` arriving while the screen is open does not appear until the user re-enters. `common/services/MyTrackingService.kt:465-476` observes `MySocketListener.listenerNotificationData` and, on key `NOTIFICATION_NEW` (`Constants.kt:381` = "notification_new"), calls `createNotificationForNotification(data.message, data.text)` at :1215-1242 — an `IMPORTANCE_HIGH` / `PRIORITY_HIGH` channel with custom sound `R.raw.audio_notification`, `BigTextStyle`, title = message, text = `Html.fromHtml(moreMessage)`. Separately `common/services/MyFirebaseMessagingService.kt:187-196` handles the FCM key `NEW_DRIVER_NOTIFICATION` (`Constants.kt:483`), stashing `notify_driver_id` (`Constants.kt:487`) into the tap intent, which `MainActivity.kt:569-605` routes to `R.id.navigation_notifications` — also discarding the id. So Android has TWO independent alert producers for the same event class, which is exactly the double-alert the report suspects.

#### 17.1 `[P2]` Socket-driven local alert for notification_new (backend question first; likely won't-do)

**What to build**

UNREFUTED but low value. I grepped the entire iOS repo for UNMutableNotificationContent / UNNotificationRequest / UNUserNotificationCenter.add — the ONLY schedulers are HomeMVVM.postLocalNotificationIfBackgrounded (HomeMVVM.swift:742-799, ride offers) and postTripNoticeIfBackgrounded (HomeMVVM.swift:801-815, trip lifecycle). Nothing schedules anything for notification_new: WebSocketManager.swift:168-175 only publishes NotificationEvent on notificationSubject, whose two consumers are NotificationsMVVM.swift:29 (list prepend) and HomeMVVM.swift:656-660 (the dead unreadNotifications counter). So iOS genuinely raises no alert from the socket frame, while Android does (MyTrackingService.kt:1215).

STEP 1 — DECIDES WHETHER THERE IS ANY WORK. Ask the backend whether every notification_new socket broadcast is also sent as an FCM push with key NEW_DRIVER_NOTIFICATION. The evidence that it IS is now stronger than the original report stated: iOS already carries the full receiving half of that contract — Constants.swift:113 pushKeyNewDriverNotification, PushNotificationCenter.swift:147-149 routing NEW_DRIVER_NOTIFICATION + notify_driver_id to PushRoute.notification, AppDelegate.swift:211-215 registering a NEW_NOTIFICATION category with an OPEN action, and AppDelegate.swift:116-128 returning [.banner,.sound,.list,.badge] from willPresent so the push banners even in the foreground. A category and a route are not written for a push that never arrives. If the backend confirms FCM accompanies the socket frame, CLOSE THIS AS WON'T-DO on iOS and file the inverse bug on ANDROID: suppress the redundant MyTrackingService.kt:465-476 socket notification so drivers stop getting the same broadcast twice (once from FCM, once from the service).

STEP 2 — ONLY IF the backend confirms notification_new is socket-ONLY with no FCM. Do NOT put this in NotificationsMVVM: that object is created by NotificationsView's @StateObject and therefore only exists while the sheet is presented, so it would alert only when the driver is already looking at the list. Put it in HomeMVVM.subscribeSockets (HomeMVVM.swift:656-660), inside the existing notificationSubject sink that already bumps unreadNotifications — HomeMVVM lives as long as the home screen, which is the whole authenticated session. Post a UNMutableNotificationContent with title = event.message, body = the HTML-stripped event.text (reuse the NSAttributedString decode from NotificationDetailView.bodyText, factored into a shared helper rather than duplicated), categoryIdentifier "NEW_NOTIFICATION" (already registered, gives the OPEN button for free), threadIdentifier "driver-notice", sound .default, and interruptionLevel .active — NOT .timeSensitive, these are informational broadcasts, not the ~20 s ride-offer window that justifies breaking Focus. userInfo = ["key": Constants.pushKeyNewDriverNotification, "notify_driver_id": event.id] so PushNotificationCenter.routeTap already handles the tap with no new code. Guard on UIApplication.shared.applicationState != .active exactly like postLocalNotificationIfBackgrounded:743. Use identifier "driver-notice-\(event.id)" so a socket replay of the same id coalesces instead of stacking.

CORRECTION to the original report's step-2 rationale: it claimed "the socket is suspended when iOS backgrounds the app, so in practice this fires rarely." That is not what the code does — there is no scenePhase-driven disconnect. MehrgoDriverApp.swift:111-125 only schedules BG tasks on .background; the only disconnect() call sites are MapMVVM.swift:276 (going offline), RootMVVM.swift:289 (logout) and SettingsMVVM.swift:36/62/71. With allowsBackgroundLocationUpdates keeping the process alive on shift, the socket keeps delivering while backgrounded, so the alert would in fact be reachable. That makes step 2 real work if step 1 says socket-only — but it does not raise the priority, because the whole feature is informational broadcast copy, not money or safety.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/services/MyTrackingService.kt:465-476 (socket observer) and :1215-1242 (createNotificationForNotification, IMPORTANCE_HIGH + R.raw.audio_notification); the competing FCM producer is app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/services/MyFirebaseMessagingService.kt:187-196

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MVVM/HomeMVVM.swift:656-660 (existing notificationSubject sink in subscribeSockets, :649) — mirror the pattern at MehrgoDriver/MVVM/HomeMVVM.swift:742-799. Source event: MehrgoDriver/Shared/WebSocketManager.swift:168-175. Tap handling already exists at MehrgoDriver/Shared/PushNotificationCenter.swift:147-149; category already registered at MehrgoDriver/AppDelegate.swift:211-215. Do NOT implement in MehrgoDriver/MVVM/NotificationsMVVM.swift:28-34 — that object only lives while the sheet is presented.

> **Gotchas.** 1) Do the backend check FIRST — if FCM already accompanies the frame, implementing this makes iOS double-alert exactly like Android does today, turning a non-gap into a regression. 2) HomeMVVM already subscribes to notificationSubject, so adding a second subscription elsewhere would double-post; extend the existing sink. 3) interruptionLevel .timeSensitive requires the Time Sensitive Notifications entitlement and is wrong here regardless — reserve it for offers. 4) prepend() at NotificationsMVVM.swift:59-69 has no id-dedupe; if you add a notification-id set for local-alert coalescing, reuse it there too, since the in-list duplicate is the same defect. 5) unreadNotifications (HomeMVVM.swift:23) is still never rendered or reset and NotificationBadge.swift is still unreferenced — do not assume incrementing it produces any visible signal.

If FCM accompanies it: this item is closed WON'T-DO on iOS, and an Android ticket exists to delete the MyTrackingService socket notification; verified on an Android device by confirming a single tray entry per broadcast. If socket-only: with the app backgrounded on an active shift, a server-sent driver notification produces exactly ONE banner with the plain-text (no visible HTML tags) body; tapping it opens the notifications sheet; with the app foregrounded no banner appears; sending the same notification id twice leaves one tray entry and one list row.

All 11 `subscription*` keys plus `no_subscriptions`, `subscriptions_subtitle`, `loading_more` and `retry` exist in both `uz.lproj` and `ru.lproj`. NOTE for planners: iOS's list-load *error* handling is thin (see the one remaining item), but Android's is thinner — Android's `errorVisible()` is behaviourally identical to `screenVisible()` and its `addLoadStateListener` is attached to a null adapter, so it never fires. iOS is at or above Android parity across this entire area.

#### Android today

Android's screen is `presentation/main/ui/subscriptions/SubscriptionsFragment.kt` + `SubscriptionsViewModel.kt`, with `presentation/main/adapter/SubscriptionAdapter.kt` (a `PagingDataAdapter`) and `domain/use_case/main/data_source/SubscriptionsDataSource.kt` (Paging3 `PagingSource`, `FIRST_PAGE_INDEX = 1`, `nextKey = nextPage + 1`, stops when `nextPage > _meta.pageCount`), purchase via `PurchaseSubscriptionUC`; models in `domain/model/SubscriptionData.kt`. Error handling is broken, not merely absent: `SubscriptionsFragment.kt:68` calls `subscriptionAdapter?.addLoadStateListener { ... }` while `subscriptionAdapter` is still null (it is first assigned at line 63 inside the `collectLatest` block, which has not emitted at registration time), so the `LoadState.Error -> errorVisible()` branch and the `tvSubscriptionsEmpty` toggle are unreachable dead code — and `errorVisible()` (lines 150-155) does nothing but hide the progress bar and show `content`, exactly like `screenVisible()`. On a failed first page Android therefore shows an empty list with no error and no empty-state text. Android also re-creates the adapter and reassigns `binding.rvSubscriptions.adapter` on every paging emission (lines 62-65), which resets scroll position.

<a id="gap-18-1"></a>

#### 18.1 `[P2]` Subscriptions list-load failures are silent on iOS: refresh-during-load drops loaded pages and a failed page leaves a permanent spinner

**What to build**

NOT AN ANDROID PORT — read the priority literally. Android has no error state for this screen either; its `LoadState.Error -> errorVisible()` branch is dead code (registered on a null adapter at SubscriptionsFragment.kt:68) and `errorVisible()` is behaviourally identical to `screenVisible()`, so Android on a failed first page shows an empty list with no message whatsoever. iOS is already better. This item is iOS-side hardening; schedule it only after every real parity gap in the plan. Two verified defects, in value order.

(a) REFRESH RACE, DATA LOSS — SubscriptionsMVVM.swift:28-36. `load(reset:)` runs `page = 1; plans.removeAll(); hasMore = true` BEFORE the `if state == .Loading { return }` guard. Concretely: infinite scroll fires `load()` for page 3; while it is in flight the driver pull-to-refreshes; `plans` is wiped and `page` reset to 1, then the function returns; the in-flight page-3 response appends page 3's items to the now-empty array and sets `page = 4`. The list is left showing ONLY page 3, with pages 1-2 unreachable until another refresh. Fix: move `if state == .Loading { return }` above the `if reset { ... }` block (or, better, capture the requested page in the Task and discard the response if `page` no longer matches on completion, which also fixes the same race for `.onAppear`-triggered loads).

(b) STALLED PAGINATION, PERMANENT SPINNER — SubscriptionsView.swift:43-45. When a page-2+ load fails, `hasMore` stays `true` (it is only assigned in the success path), so `loadMoreFooter` renders a spinner forever, and the trigger is the last row's `.onAppear`, which will not re-fire for an already-appeared row. Pagination silently dies under an animating spinner. The codebase already solved exactly this: MyOrdersView.swift:193-199 branches `if vm.historyLoadFailed { historyRetryFooter } else { loadMoreFooter }`, with `historyRetryFooter` at MyOrdersView.swift:256-270 (arrow.clockwise + `Text("retry")`). Add a `@Published var loadFailed: Bool` to SubscriptionsMVVM (set true in the `catch`, false at the top of the `Task do` block) and mirror that branch. The `retry` key already exists in both uz.lproj:502 and ru.lproj:501 — no new strings needed.

(c) OPTIONAL, LOWEST VALUE — SubscriptionsView.swift:23-33 branches only on `plans.isEmpty` then `state == .Loading`, so a first-page failure falls through to `EmptyStateView(systemImage: "ticket", titleKey: "no_subscriptions", subtitleKey: "subscriptions_subtitle")`; `vm.errorMessage` is assigned at SubscriptionsMVVM.swift:56 and read by no view in the repo (grep-verified). Cheapest correct fix is one line in the VM, not a new view: add `ToastCenter.shared.show(error.localizedDescription, style: .error)` to `load()`'s catch, matching the pattern this same file already uses in `purchase(id:)` at line 78 and the pattern EarningsMVVM.swift:98 / VideosMVVM.swift:54 use for their list loads. Only build a dedicated error branch with a Retry button if design asks for one. Either way, clear `errorMessage` at the top of `load()` so a stale message cannot survive a successful reload.

**Android reference** — C:/Users/Umar Inagamjanov/Desktop/teamwork_uz_ai/app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/main/ui/subscriptions/SubscriptionsFragment.kt:62-90 (dead addLoadStateListener) and :150-155 (no-op errorVisible) — cited as counter-evidence, NOT as a model to copy

**iOS target** — C:/Users/Umar Inagamjanov/Downloads/mehrgo-driver-ios-main/MehrgoDriver/MVVM/SubscriptionsMVVM.swift (load(reset:), lines 28-60) and C:/Users/Umar Inagamjanov/Downloads/mehrgo-driver-ios-main/MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Settings/SubscriptionsView.swift (body, lines 20-51; footer at 43-45; empty-state branch at 23-33). Pattern to copy: C:/Users/Umar Inagamjanov/Downloads/mehrgo-driver-ios-main/MehrgoDriver/View/Main/MyOrders/MyOrdersView.swift:193-199 and :256-270

> **Gotchas.** Do not 'fix' (a) by adding a `guard !reset` — `.onAppear { vm.load(reset: true) }` at SubscriptionsView.swift:71 and `.refreshable { vm.load(reset: true) }` at line 55 both legitimately reset, and on this screen `.onAppear` re-fires on every return from the settings stack, so reset must stay reachable. The `.refreshable` block polls `vm.state != .Loading` for up to 3s (30 x 100ms) to hold the spinner; if you add a `loadFailed` flag, make sure the catch path still leaves `state != .Loading` or the pull-to-refresh spinner hangs for the full 3s on every failure. `purchase(id:)` calls `load(reset: true)` on success (line 75) — whatever guard ordering you choose must not turn that into a no-op, or a freshly bought plan will not show its active badge. `hasMore` is also the sole gate at line 34, so if you set it `false` on error the retry footer must reset it to `true` before calling `load()`.

**Acceptance** — 1) With the app in airplane mode, open Subscriptions cold: the driver sees a failure signal (toast or error state), not 'Obunalar yo'q'. 2) Scroll to page 3 of a multi-page plan list, then pull-to-refresh while the next page is still loading: the list ends up showing page 1 onward, never a list whose first row is a page-3 plan. 3) Kill connectivity mid-scroll after page 1 has rendered: the footer switches from the animating spinner to a tappable Retry, and tapping it with connectivity restored appends the next page and resumes normal infinite scroll. 4) Fail a load, then fix connectivity and pull-to-refresh: no stale error text or toast from the previous attempt appears. 5) Buy a plan while online: the confirm alert, the `subscription_activated` toast, and the refreshed list with a green `subscription_active` capsule all still work unchanged.

#### 19.1 `[P0]` No permission-regression gate after first run — a driver who revokes/downgrades location silently stays "online" with no GPS

**What to build**

CONFIRMED, not refuted. Whole-repo grep for `authStatus` / `authorizationStatus` / `CLAuthorizationStatus` / `didChangeAuthorization` returns exactly five call sites: the declaration + delegate in LocationTracker.swift (:54, :116, :122-140, :204, :637-641), a one-shot hard-denial check in LoginMVVM.swift:48, and AccessPermissionsView.swift:63/:74. NO other view or MVVM observes it. Every post-onboarding lifecycle branch reads the PERSISTED boolean instead: RootMVVM.swift:219 (continueAfterSplash), :266 (didSignIn), :273 (didCompleteDriverInfo), :281 (didUnblockDriver) all test `!AccessPermissionsManager.shared.completed`, and `AccessPermissionsManager` (Persistence/AccessPermissionsManager.swift) has exactly one writer — `set(true)` from AccessPermissionsView:235/:343 — so once true it is never cleared anywhere in the repo. `MainView.task` (View/Main/MainView.swift:66-69) calls `LocationTracker.shared.requestPermission()`, and that method (LocationTracker.swift:121-127) branches ONLY on `.notDetermined` and `.authorizedWhenInUse`; `.denied`/`.restricted` fall through to nothing. `startWhileOnline()` (:200-209) guards only `.denied`/`.restricted` — `.authorizedWhenInUse` passes the guard and starts updates that die the moment the app backgrounds, which is the silent-failure case. Net: a driver who downgrades Always→While-Using (or denies) in Settings after onboarding still flips the online toggle, `driver/start` succeeds, the socket connects, and no fixes arrive in the background — GPS batch uploader ships nothing, taximeter freezes, no orders, no explanation.

BUILD:
1. `LocationTracker`: add `var needsPermissionRecovery: Bool { authStatus == .denied || authStatus == .restricted || authStatus == .authorizedWhenInUse }` — Always is required for shift tracking, mirroring the existing `canProceed` rule at AccessPermissionsView.swift:88-90 (`locationState == .granted` maps to `.authorizedAlways` only, per the switch at :62-70).
2. `MapHomeView`: persistent warning banner under the top bar bound to `@ObservedObject LocationTracker.shared`, shown while `needsPermissionRecovery`. Localized keys already exist in all lproj files: `permission_denied_required` (hard-denied), `permission_partial_required` (While-Using), CTA `open_settings` → `UIApplication.shared.open(URL(string: UIApplication.openSettingsURLString)!)`.
3. Hard-gate going online: MapMVVM.swift:229 (`goOnline()` success branch) and :256 (`goOnlineForActiveOrder()`) both call `startWhileOnline()` unguarded. Return early BEFORE the `driver/start` POST in `goOnline()` and surface the same alert `LoginMVVM` already uses (`showLocationSettingsPrompt`, LoginMVVM.swift:29/:48-52, bound in LoginView.swift:64-68). Do not let the toggle latch on. For `goOnlineForActiveOrder()` — a driver mid-trip — do not block the state flip (they must keep their order), but do raise the banner.
4. Notifications need an explicit foreground re-poll: `PushNotificationCenter.shared.refreshAuthStatus()` (Shared/PushNotificationCenter.swift:59) is called today only from AccessPermissionsView (:188 scenePhase, :211 onAppear). Add a scenePhase-driven call at `MainView` level. Location needs no polling — `didChangeAuthorization` (LocationTracker.swift:637) already fires on return from Settings.
5. On transition to `.denied`/`.restricted`, call `AccessPermissionsManager.shared.set(false)` so the next cold launch routes back through `.accessPermissions` — matching Android's re-route-on-entry. Note `set(false)` already compiles (the setter is not one-way), it simply has no caller today.

**Android reference** — common/CheckPermissions.kt:17-79 (`isGrantedAllPermission`, requires ACCESS_BACKGROUND_LOCATION); presentation/main/ui/home/HomeFragment.kt:150-164 (live re-check on every entry → `action_navigation_home_to_accessPermissionsFragment`); presentation/maps/yandex_map/MapFragment.kt:437-447 (same gate); presentation/auth/ui/login/LoginFragment.kt:73-88; common/shared_pref/AccessPermissionsManager.kt (flag gates ONLY the overlay branch, never location)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/LocationTracker.swift:121-127 + :200-209 (add `needsPermissionRecovery`, tighten guard); MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Home/MapHomeView.swift (banner); MehrgoDriver/MVVM/MapMVVM.swift:229 and :256 (online gate); MehrgoDriver/View/Main/MainView.swift:66-69 (notification refresh on scenePhase); MehrgoDriver/Persistence/AccessPermissionsManager.swift:31-35 (`set(false)` on hard denial); MehrgoDriver/MVVM/LoginMVVM.swift:29,48-52 + MehrgoDriver/View/Auth/LoginView.swift:64-68 (alert pattern to reuse)

Do NOT call `CLLocationManager.locationServicesEnabled()` on the main thread (the existing comment at LocationTracker.swift:200-203 documents why). Clearing `AccessPermissionsManager` on `.denied` must not fire while the driver has an ACTIVE order — RootMVVM only re-reads the flag on the next cold launch, but a mid-trip clear combined with any future in-session recompute would eject them from the trip; scope the clear to the `.denied`/`.restricted` transition only, never `.authorizedWhenInUse` (which the banner alone should handle). The delegate is `nonisolated` and hops to `@MainActor` via a Task (:637-641), so a banner bound to `@Published authStatus` updates one runloop later — do not assume synchronous reads right after returning from Settings.

**What to build**

CONFIRMED. `MehrgoDriverApp.swift:92` applies `.id(languageManager.refreshToken)` to `RootView`. `LanguageManager.set()` (Persistence/LanguageManager.swift:57-66) bumps `refreshToken = UUID()`, and that is exactly what the Settings path calls: `LanguageMVVM.apply()` (MVVM/LanguageMVVM.swift:83) → `LanguageManager.shared.set(selected)`, invoked from View/Main/Settings/ChooseLanguageView.swift:53-60. The new identity destroys `RootView`, which owns `@StateObject private var vm: RootMVVM = RootMVVM()` (RootView.swift:15), so a fresh `RootMVVM.init()` runs `recompute()` (RootMVVM.swift:53, 168-197) and for an authenticated driver every branch falls through to `route = .splash` (:194). That re-runs `SplashMVVM.start()` (MVVM/SplashMVVM.swift:27-38): `GET mobile/version-driver`, `GET user/me`, a hard-coded `try? await Task.sleep(nanoseconds: 1_200_000_000)`, then `continueAfterSplash` re-evaluates force-update / blocked-apps / blocked-driver / permissions — so a version bump published while the driver is on shift bounces them to `ForceUpdateView` the moment they switch language. The RootView comment at :36-40 acknowledges this rebuild path explicitly, so it is known-but-unaddressed, not accidental.

ADDITIONAL CONFIRMED CONSEQUENCE (not in the original claim, same root cause): the rebuild also destroys `MapHomeView`'s `@StateObject MapMVVM`, whose `isOnline` initializes to `false` (MapMVVM.swift:23) and is never rehydrated from the server. `WebSocketManager.shared` is a singleton and stays connected, and the server still has the driver registered from the earlier `driver/start` — so an on-shift driver with NO active order lands back on "Ishni boshlash" while being server-side online, and re-sliding re-POSTs `driver/start`, which can 400 on the backend's 16-hour daily shift cap (see the comment at MapMVVM.swift:209-215). Drivers WITH an active order self-heal via `goOnlineForActiveOrder()` (MapHomeView.swift:167, :228); drivers merely waiting for offers do not.

BUILD (preserve the route across the locale rebuild):
1. Hoist `RootMVVM` out of `RootView` into `MehrgoDriverApp` as `@StateObject private var root: RootMVVM = RootMVVM()`, passed down as `@ObservedObject`/`.environmentObject` (MainView/MapHomeView already consume it via `.environmentObject(vm)` at RootView.swift:23, so the injection point is unchanged).
2. Move `.id(languageManager.refreshToken)` off `RootView` (MehrgoDriverApp.swift:92) and onto the `content` subtree inside RootView, so the hierarchy re-renders in the new locale but `RootMVVM` — and therefore `route` — survives.
3. Keep `.environment(\.locale, languageManager.locale)` at App level (already present, MehrgoDriverApp.swift:~99); that plus `LocalizedAppString()` (LanguageManager.swift:107-114) is what actually re-resolves strings. The `.id` only forces UIKit-hosted subviews to redraw.
4. Sanity-guard `recompute()` so it can never demote a live session: `if route == .main && UserManager.shared.isAuthenticated { return }`.
Expected result: switching language in Settings re-renders in place (Android's `Activity.recreate()` behavior) instead of relaunching through splash, and the online state survives.

**Android reference** — presentation/main/ui/change_language/ChangeLanguageFragment.kt:140-164 — awaits `user/change-language`, then `Activity.recreate()` (crossfaded); no relaunch through `FirstActivity`. presentation/auth/ui/language/LanguageFragment.kt:100-112 (`confirm()` → `LanguageManager.saveLanguage`) is the first-run twin and is behaviorally identical to iOS.

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MehrgoDriverApp.swift:88-99 (move `.id(languageManager.refreshToken)` off RootView at :92; hoist RootMVVM); MehrgoDriver/View/Root/RootView.swift:15,20-41,67-100 (`@StateObject vm` → injected; apply the locale `.id` to `content`); MehrgoDriver/MVVM/RootMVVM.swift:168-197 (`recompute` guard); MehrgoDriver/MVVM/LanguageMVVM.swift:63-96 (`apply`); MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Settings/ChooseLanguageView.swift:53-60; MehrgoDriver/MVVM/MapMVVM.swift:23 (`isOnline` not rehydrated)

> **Gotchas.** The `.id(vm.route)` on the NavigationStack (RootView.swift:41) is load-bearing for the sign-up→verify→fill-data flow (its comment at :26-40 explains that without it a finished registration stays stuck on the pushed form) — do NOT remove or merge it with the locale `.id`; they must stay two separate identities. Hoisting `RootMVVM` into `MehrgoDriverApp` moves it out of the `.id` scope, which is the point, but verify `pendingPushRoute` cold-launch stashing still works: `subscribePushRoutes` (RootMVVM.swift:151-163) only stashes while `route == .splash`, and with the MVVM no longer re-inited per language change that guard now behaves differently across a locale switch. The 120 s `pendingPushRouteTTL` (:43) also stops being re-armed by a rebuild. Finally, `LanguageManager.set()` writes `AppleLanguages`, whose effect on Info.plist strings only takes hold on the next process launch — do not "fix" that by forcing a relaunch, it is a known iOS limitation.

**Acceptance** — 1) Sign in, reach the map, go online (no active order), open Settings → language, pick another language, Apply: the UI re-renders in the new language in place — no splash logo, no 1.2 s delay, no `mobile/version-driver` call in the network log — and the online toggle is still ON. 2) Repeat with an ACTIVE order: the trip panel stays mounted with the same order id and the fare/timer keep ticking (no reset). 3) With the backend reporting a versionCode higher than `Constants.appVersion`, changing language does NOT push `ForceUpdateView`. 4) A driver on the first-run `.language` route still advances to `.introduce` on Continue (the `recompute` guard must not break the unauthenticated path). 5) Cold-launch after the change comes up in the new language.

#### 19.3 `[P2]` Onboarding slides are hardcoded on iOS — the server-driven `slider/index` content Android renders cannot be changed from the dashboard

**What to build**

CONFIRMED. Repo-wide grep for `slider` across MehrgoDriver/ returns only the SF Symbol `slider.horizontal.3` (HistoryMVVM.swift:41) and SlideToActButton/UI comments — the endpoint is never called. `IntroduceMVVM.load()` (MVVM/IntroduceMVVM.swift:25-31) assigns `slides = fallback` and sets `state = .Positive`, with an in-code comment stating the omission is deliberate because the server copy is generic marketing text; `fallback` (:41-61) is three local `Introduce` items built from `intro_offers_*`, `intro_taximeter_*`, `intro_ios_*` with `image: nil`, and `IntroduceView` picks SF Symbols by index (`iconName(for:)`, IntroduceView.swift:134-141). Android's `IntroduceFragment` (presentation/auth/ui/introduce/IntroduceFragment.kt:71-96) collects `IntroduceUC` → `GET slider/index` → `BaseResponse<List<Introduce>>`, renders `header`+`title`+`description`+ a Glide image from `Constants.IMAGE_URL + url`, and auto-skips straight to login when the list is empty (:79-83).

RECOMMENDATION: partially port — add a server override with the current local copy as fallback; do NOT replace the local slides outright. The real cost is operational (ops cannot update driver onboarding copy for iOS without an App Store release), not visual.

IF YOU BUILD IT (~half a day): in `IntroduceMVVM.load()`, `try await DynamicClient.shared.request(path: "slider/index", method: .GET, type: BaseResponse<Array<Introduce>>.self)`; on a non-empty array use the server slides; on an EMPTY array call `complete()` + `onDone()` immediately to mirror Android's auto-skip; on ANY error fall back to the existing local `fallback`. The `Introduce` struct (Shared/Models/SharedModels.swift:1305-1310) currently declares only `id: Int`, `title: String?`, `description: String?`, `image: String?` — it has NO `header` and no CodingKeys, so it will silently decode `image` as nil against the server's `url` key: add `header: String?` and a `CodingKeys` mapping `image = "url"` to match Android's `@SerializedName("url")` (domain/model/Introduce.kt). In `SlideContent`, render `AsyncImage(url: URL(string: Constants.imageURL + image))` inside the medallion when `image` is non-nil, else keep the SF Symbol.

**Android reference** — presentation/auth/ui/introduce/IntroduceFragment.kt:71-96 (collect + empty-list auto-skip at :79-83); domain/use_case/auth/IntroduceUC.kt (`GET slider/index` → `BaseResponse<List<Introduce>>`); domain/model/Introduce.kt (`id`, `header`, `title`, `description`, `@SerializedName("url") imageUrl`); presentation/auth/adapter/IntroduceAdapter.kt (Glide + `Constants.IMAGE_URL`)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MVVM/IntroduceMVVM.swift:25-61 (`load()` + `fallback`); MehrgoDriver/Shared/Models/SharedModels.swift:1305-1310 (`struct Introduce` — add `header`, add CodingKeys mapping `image` ← `url`); MehrgoDriver/View/Root/IntroduceView.swift:134-141 (`iconName(for:)`) and the `SlideContent` medallion; MehrgoDriver/Shared/Constants.swift:25 (`Constants.imageURL`, note the lowercase-i spelling)

> **Gotchas.** `Introduce` is also `Identifiable` + `Hashable` (SharedModels.swift:1305, :1558) and is used as a ForEach identity — server ids must be unique or the pager will glitch; the local fallback uses ids 1/2/3, so a mixed state (server load failing mid-session) could collide. `Constants.imageURL` is `"https://\(BASE_URL)"` with NO trailing slash — check whether the server's `url` field is absolute or relative before concatenating (Android's Glide call is the reference). The empty-list auto-skip must call `IntroduceManager.shared.set(true)` BEFORE `onDone()`, otherwise the next cold launch re-enters `.introduce` (RootMVVM.swift:189). Do not block first launch on a network call: the fetch must have a short timeout and fall back locally, or a driver on a bad connection stares at a blank onboarding screen — Android tolerates this because it shows a loading state, iOS's `load()` is currently synchronous. `LocalizedAppString` (not `NSLocalizedString`) is required for the fallback strings so they follow the in-app language.

**Acceptance** — 1) Backend returns 3 slides: the carousel shows server `header`/`title`/`description` and remote images, not SF Symbols. 2) Backend returns `[]`: onboarding is skipped straight to login and `introduce-completed` is persisted, so a relaunch goes to login too. 3) Airplane mode / 500 response: the three local slides render as they do today, within ~2 s. 4) A server slide with a null/blank `image` falls back to the SF Symbol medallion rather than an empty box. 5) Changing the copy in the dashboard is visible on next app open with no new build.

**Gaps:** 3 total — P0: 1 · P1: 0 · P2: 2

#### iOS today

iOS has exactly one settings surface, `SettingsView` (MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Settings/SettingsView.swift), presented as a sheet from the single call site at MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Home/MapHomeView.swift:235 — verified by grepping `SettingsView(` repo-wide, so Android's duplicate SettingsFragment/MapSettingsFragment problem genuinely does not exist here. Structure confirmed: `profileHeader` (:113, avatar + fullName + `ID:` + `DevEnvironmentBadge` gated on `Constants.isUsingDevURL`) → `walletCard` (:145, balance with `.asSumString` trailing / earnings / subscriptions) → `activityCard` (:177, orders_history + videos only) → `preferencesCard` (:200, language / map_type / theme — note the section comment at :199 claiming "theme row removed — app is light-only now" is STALE, the `ChooseThemeView` row is live at :218) → `helpCard` (:234, support + `privacyPolicyRow` :249) → `aboutCard` (:280, version label only, no actionable rows) → `signOutButton` (:297) → iOS-only `deleteAccountButton` (:319). `Constants.privacyPolicyURL` (Shared/Constants.swift:30) is `https://mehrgo.uz/politics.html`, byte-identical to Android's `PRIVACY_POLICY_URL` (SettingsFragment.kt:44). Support is confirmed non-chat/non-webview: `SupportView.callCard` (:46) dials `UserManager.shared.user?.branch?.dispatcherNumber` via `SupportMVVM.callDispatcher()` (SupportMVVM.swift:52), disabled at 0.6 opacity when nil; `faqSection` (:113), `faqSkeleton` (:168), `faqEmpty` (:190), `errorLogCard` (:207) and `FAQRow` (:252) are all compiled-but-unreferenced — the `body` VStack (:22-26) contains `callCard` alone. Localization reality check: there are only TWO `Localizable.strings` files (uz.lproj, ru.lproj); `en.lproj` ships only `InfoPlist.strings` and `developmentLanguage: uz` is set in project.yml — any plan saying "add to all three .lproj" is wrong.

#### Android today

Android's settings exist twice (`presentation/main/ui/settings/SettingsFragment.kt` and `presentation/maps/map_settings/MapSettingsFragment.kt`), and both carry the rows iOS lacks. `tvUpdateApp` (SettingsFragment.kt:141, MapSettingsFragment.kt:80) is always visible — no gating — and fires `market://details?id=$APPLICATION_ID` with an `ActivityNotFoundException` fallback to the play.google.com URL. `clOrdersInLocale` (SettingsFragment.kt:194) navigates to `OrdersInLocaleFragment`, which collects `OrdersInLocaleViewModel.getCalculations()` (backed by `CalculationsDao.getCalculations()` = `SELECT * FROM calculations ORDER BY id DESC`, CalculationsDao.kt:21) into a 20-per-page reveal list; `CalculationAdapter.onBind` renders ONLY `ID: <orderId>`, a cached distance sum over `item.locations`, `Helper.formatTime(trackedTime)` and `Helper.formatTime(waitedTime)` — there is no fare on the row. Tapping copies every GPS point (`lat/lon/acy` lines) to the clipboard and toasts `gps_track_copied`. Critically, `DeleteCalculationUC` (domain/use_case/locale/DeleteCalculationUC.kt:6) is declared but called from NOWHERE in `app/src` — Android never prunes the table, so the screen is a permanent forensic log. The "?" button is `flHelp` (SettingsFragment.kt:126) → `InstructionFragment` with arg `VIDEO_ADDITIONAL` = `"qoshimcha"` (Constants.kt:472); that fragment fetches `instruction/index`, picks the single item where `item.position == videoPosition`, and renders a YouTube poster + "watch on YouTube" handoff (in-app IFrame embed is dead per the comment at InstructionFragment.kt:88) — it is NOT an FAQ accordion. Android's `domain/model/Instruction.kt` fields are `id, name, position, video, type` — no `title`, no `body`.

#### 20.2 `[P2]` instruction/index is fetched on every Support appear but never rendered — and the dead FAQ UI cannot simply be switched on, the model does not match the payload

**What to build**

The gap is real but the originally-claimed one-line fix is WRONG — do not ship it as written. Verified facts: `SupportMVVM.load()` (SupportMVVM.swift:33) GETs `instruction/index` into `@Published var instructions` on every `.onAppear` and every pull-to-refresh, and `Instruction` is referenced nowhere else in the iOS repo except SharedModels.swift:1088/1551 and SupportView — so the fetch is pure waste today. BUT: (a) iOS `Instruction` (SharedModels.swift:1088) declares `title: String?` and `body: String?` with no CodingKeys, and `DynamicClient` uses a plain `JSONDecoder()` with no key strategy (DynamicClient.swift:219), while the server field Android binds is `name` (domain/model/Instruction.kt) and Android has no `body` field at all — every optional silently decodes to nil, so enabling `faqSection` as-is renders a list of `"—"` placeholders (FAQRow.swift line :265 falls back to `"—"`). Fix the model first: add `CodingKeys` mapping `title` ← `"name"`, and confirm against a live `instruction/index` response whether any `body`/description field exists — if it does not, delete the `body` block from FAQRow (:282) rather than shipping an accordion that expands to nothing. (b) Android's UX is NOT an FAQ list: `flHelp` opens `InstructionFragment`, which filters to the ONE item with `position == "qoshimcha"` and shows a YouTube poster + external handoff. So decide the target explicitly: either (i) match Android — a single help-video entry point keyed on `position`, in which case most of `faqSection` is unnecessary and only `FAQRow.videoURL(_:)` (:310) is reusable, or (ii) go beyond Android and list all instructions as an accordion, which is a product decision, not a parity fix. (c) The strings `faq` and `no_faq` ALREADY exist in uz.lproj:391/396 and ru.lproj:390/395 — the 'verify and add if missing' step is already satisfied. Whichever path is chosen, if the decision is to keep Support call-only, then DELETE `faqSection`/`faqSkeleton`/`faqEmpty`/`FAQRow`/`errorLogCard` and the `load()` network call instead of leaving a per-appear request feeding dead state.

First, the `name` vs `title` mismatch is silent — `Instruction` decodes successfully with all-nil fields, so this ships as a screen full of em-dashes rather than a crash or a decode error; validate against a real response before wiring any UI. #### 20.3 `[P2]` No iOS screen for locally-stored taximeter trips — and iOS deletes the rows at trip end, so a list view alone would always be empty

**What to build**

The absence is CONFIRMED — repo-wide grep for `LocalOrders`, `OrdersInLocale`, `fetchAll` returns nothing, and `CalculationStore` (Persistence/CalculationStore.swift) exposes only `upsert/get/appendLocations/setTrackedTime/setWaitedTimeUntilGone/setWaitState/setDistance/delete`, all keyed by a single `orderId` via the private `fetch(orderId:in:)` at :180. But the original framing understated the work by one critical step. iOS DELETES each Calculation row when the trip ends or is cancelled (TripMVVM.swift:762, :880, :944 and HomeMVVM.swift:733), whereas Android NEVER deletes — `DeleteCalculationUC` (domain/use_case/locale/DeleteCalculationUC.kt:6) has zero call sites in `app/src`, so Room accumulates every trip forever. Adding `fetchAll()` + a list view on iOS therefore yields a screen that is permanently empty except for one in-flight or orphaned trip. So the port is THREE changes, in order: (1) decide retention — either stop deleting at trip end (matching Android, and accepting unbounded Core Data growth, which is exactly why Android's table is a forensic log) or, better, mark rows finished and prune beyond N/30 days; (2) add `fetchAll() async -> [CalculationDTO]` to CalculationStore using an `NSFetchRequest(entityName: "Calculation")` with `NSSortDescriptor(key: "createdAt", ascending: false)` — the `createdAt` attribute already exists (MehrgoDriver.xcdatamodeld/MehrgoDriver.xcdatamodel/contents:4, optional Date, set on insert at CalculationStore.swift:62), and note Android sorts by `id DESC` because its table stores no timestamp, so iOS's createdAt sort is a strict improvement; (3) add `LocalOrdersMVVM` + `LocalOrdersView` built from the existing `GroupedCard` (SettingsView.swift:368), rows showing exactly what Android shows — `ID: <orderId>`, distance, tracked time, waited time — with `UIPasteboard.general.string` on tap and a `ToastCenter.shared.show` confirmation, then add the row to `activityCard` (SettingsView.swift:177) next to orders_history.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/main/ui/settings/SettingsFragment.kt:194 (clOrdersInLocale); presentation/main/ui/orders_in_locale/OrdersInLocaleFragment.kt (20-per-page reveal, count pill, empty state, clipboard copy + `gps_track_copied` toast); presentation/main/adapter/CalculationAdapter.kt:47-72 (row = ID + cached distance sum + trackedTime + waitedTime); data/locale/CalculationsDao.kt:21 (`ORDER BY id DESC`); domain/use_case/locale/DeleteCalculationUC.kt:6 (declared, never called)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Persistence/CalculationStore.swift (add `fetchAll()`; the private `fetch` at :180 is orderId-only); MehrgoDriver/MVVM/TripMVVM.swift:762,880,944 + MehrgoDriver/MVVM/HomeMVVM.swift:733 (the `delete(orderId:)` calls that must change for the list to ever have content); new MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Settings/LocalOrdersView.swift + MehrgoDriver/MVVM/LocalOrdersMVVM.swift; row added to SettingsView.swift:177 activityCard

> **Gotchas.** Do NOT put a computed fare on the row — the original spec asked for one, but Android's CalculationAdapter shows no fare, only ID/distance/tracked/waited, and inventing a fare here risks a number that disagrees with the server receipt (see the known fare-receipt divergence). Retention is the load-bearing decision: touching TripMVVM's delete calls changes trip-teardown behavior, so confirm with product that saved trips should persist post-finish before writing any UI. `CalculationStore` is an `actor` and `CalculationDTO` is not `Identifiable` — either add conformance or key the ForEach on `orderId`. `locationsJSON` rows can hold hundreds of points; decode and sum off the main actor and cache per row, exactly as Android does with `distanceCache` (CalculationAdapter.kt:25), or scrolling will stutter. Also note the Core Data entity has a uniqueness constraint on `orderId`, so a not-deleted row from a re-opened order will be updated rather than duplicated.

**Acceptance** — After completing two trips: Settings → the new row lists both, newest first, each showing `ID: <orderId>`, a plausible distance, tracked time and waited time; tapping a row copies the full `lat/lon/acy` point list to the pasteboard and shows a confirmation toast; the list shows an empty state when no trips are stored; and completing a trip no longer wipes its row from Core Data (or wipes it only under whatever pruning policy was agreed).

**Gaps:** 3 total — P0: 0 · P1: 1 · P2: 2

#### iOS today

A whole-repo `grep -rn "MetaEvents\."` returns exactly three call sites: `MVVM/CompleteDriverInfoMVVM.swift:240`, `MVVM/TripMVVM.swift:781` (`breakdown.total`), and `View/Root/RootView.swift:47` (ATT). iOS additionally has `AppEventsLogger` bootstrap via `ApplicationDelegate.shared.application(_:didFinishLaunchingWithOptions:)` at `AppDelegate.swift:34`, the `open url` handler at `AppDelegate.swift:63`, and Info.plist keys `FacebookAppID`/`FacebookClientToken`/`FacebookAutoLogAppEventsEnabled`/`FacebookAdvertiserIDCollectionEnabled`, the `fb917175901413661` URL scheme (line 108), `NSUserTrackingUsageDescription` (112) and two Meta `SKAdNetworkItems` (115-123) — none of which Android needs. The README's "Firebase is disabled" claim is REFUTED: `MehrgoDriver/GoogleService-Info.plist` is committed (not in .gitignore) with real values (`PROJECT_ID mehrgo-driver`, `GCM_SENDER_ID 1074705751957`, `GOOGLE_APP_ID 1:1074705751957:ios:bc6281f86de100ebcfa09c`), is referenced four times in project.pbxproj (64/278/413/766, i.e. in Copy Bundle Resources), `FirebaseApp.configure()` runs live at `MehrgoDriverApp.swift:33` behind a `Bundle.main.path(forResource:)` check, and FirebaseMessaging/FirebaseCrashlytics/FirebaseAnalytics are all real SPM dependencies in `project.yml:69-73`. Two nuances worth knowing but NOT gaps: (a) the plist's `IS_ANALYTICS_ENABLED = false` key is legacy and ignored by modern Firebase — runtime control is `FIREBASE_ANALYTICS_COLLECTION_ENABLED` in Info.plist, which is absent, so Analytics collection defaults ON; (b) iOS logs TripCompleted from `TripMVVM`, Android from `MapFragment.kt:2899-2902` — one live call site each, behaviourally equivalent, not a gap.

#### Android today

Android's funnel lives in `app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/MetaEvents.kt` — an `object` with `logCompletedRegistration(context)` firing `AppEventsConstants.EVENT_NAME_COMPLETED_REGISTRATION` (lines 17-20) and `logTripCompleted(context, fareUzs)` firing the custom `"TripCompleted"` with the fare as `valueToSum` and a `Bundle` carrying `EVENT_PARAM_CURRENCY = "UZS"` (lines 23-29). Call sites: `presentation/auth/ui/complete_driver_info/CompleteDriverInfoFragment.kt:884` and `presentation/maps/yandex_map/MapFragment.kt:2899-2902`. `common/App.kt:76` calls `AppEventsLogger.activateApp(this)`; the SDK self-initializes from `AndroidManifest.xml:86-96` meta-data (`com.facebook.sdk.ApplicationId`/`ClientToken`/`AutoLogAppEventsEnabled`/`AdvertiserIDCollectionEnabled`). Firebase is wired via the `com.google.gms.google-services` and `com.google.firebase.crashlytics` Gradle plugins (`app/build.gradle:9-10`, versions 4.4.4 / 3.0.6 in root `build.gradle:19-20`) with `firebase-crashlytics:20.0.3`, `firebase-analytics:23.0.0`, `firebase-messaging:24.1.0` (`app/build.gradle:82-84`) and `facebook-core:18.0.3` (line 154). `app/google-services.json` is committed. Two build-system properties are the source of the parity deltas below: the google-services plugin HARD-FAILS the build when google-services.json is absent, and `minifyEnabled false` (`app/build.gradle:29`) plus the Crashlytics plugin mean Android crash stacks arrive readable with zero extra configuration.

<a id="gap-21-1"></a>

#### 21.1 `[P1]` iOS never uploads dSYMs to Crashlytics — all production crashes arrive unsymbolicated

**What to build**

CONFIRMED by direct verification: `grep -c ShellScript MehrgoDriver.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` returns 0, and the only build phases in the whole project are PBXFrameworksBuildPhase (line 323), PBXResourcesBuildPhase (762) and PBXSourcesBuildPhase (785). There is no CI to compensate — the repo root contains only MehrgoDriver/, MehrgoDriver.xcodeproj/, MehrgoDriverTests/, WaitWidget/, README.md, project.yml and .gitignore; no .github/, no Fastfile, no shell scripts anywhere.

IMPORTANT SHARPENING: dSYM *generation* is already correct — `DEBUG_INFORMATION_FORMAT = "dwarf-with-dsym"` is set on the project-level Release config (project.pbxproj:1190), and neither target-level config overrides it (the only other occurrence, line 1071, is the project-level Debug config set to plain `dwarf`). `ENABLE_BITCODE: NO` (project.yml settings.base) means the archive dSYMs are the real ones, so no App Store Connect re-download dance is needed. The ONLY missing piece is the upload step.

Edit `project.yml` (NOT project.pbxproj — it is XcodeGen-generated and will be overwritten), under `targets: MehrgoDriver:`, as a sibling of the existing `dependencies:` block:

    postCompileScripts:
      - name: Firebase Crashlytics — upload dSYMs
        script: |
          "${BUILD_DIR%/Build/*}/SourcePackages/checkouts/firebase-ios-sdk/Crashlytics/run"
        inputFiles:
          - ${DWARF_DSYM_FOLDER_PATH}/${DWARF_DSYM_FILE_NAME}/Contents/Resources/DWARF/${TARGET_NAME}
          - $(SRCROOT)/$(BUILT_PRODUCTS_DIR)/$(INFOPLIST_PATH)
        basedOnDependencyAnalysis: false

`postCompileScripts` is a valid XcodeGen target key (alongside `preBuildScripts`/`postBuildScripts`). Then run `xcodegen generate`. Use the SPM checkout path shown above, NOT the CocoaPods `${PODS_ROOT}/FirebaseCrashlytics/run` form from most tutorials — this project resolves Firebase via SPM (`project.yml packages: Firebase: url: https://github.com/firebase/firebase-ios-sdk, from: 11.0.0`); there is no Podfile and .gitignore explicitly notes CocoaPods is legacy here.

**Android reference** — app/build.gradle:10 applies `com.google.firebase.crashlytics` (plugin 3.0.6, root build.gradle:20) with `firebase-crashlytics:20.0.3` (app/build.gradle:82); combined with `minifyEnabled false` (app/build.gradle:29) Android crash stacks are readable in the Crashlytics console with no per-build upload step required.

**iOS target** — C:/Users/Umar Inagamjanov/Downloads/mehrgo-driver-ios-main/project.yml — target `MehrgoDriver`, add `postCompileScripts:` as a sibling of the `dependencies:` block (which begins at the line `    dependencies:` immediately after the `settings:` block, ~line 66); regenerated output lands in C:/Users/Umar Inagamjanov/Downloads/mehrgo-driver-ios-main/MehrgoDriver.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj

> **Gotchas.** 1) Never hand-edit project.pbxproj — it is XcodeGen output; the change will vanish on the next `xcodegen generate`. 2) `basedOnDependencyAnalysis: false` is required or Xcode skips the phase on incremental builds and you get a false 'it's wired' signal. 3) The SPM checkout path only resolves after packages have been fetched at least once; a clean-clone first build will log a 'file not found' from the script — that is expected and harmless, it succeeds from the second build. 4) Verify by symbol, not by eye: after regenerating, `grep -c ShellScript project.pbxproj` must be non-zero (it is 0 today). 5) `WaitWidget/` exists on disk but has NO target in project.yml, so there is no second target needing its own upload phase — but if the widget is ever added as an app extension, it needs the same phase. 6) Crashlytics is auto-initialized by FirebaseCore component registration when the framework is linked — a whole-repo `grep -rn Crashlytics --include=*.swift` returns zero hits and that is CORRECT, not a bug; do not add manual init.

**Acceptance** — After `xcodegen generate`: (a) `grep -c ShellScript MehrgoDriver.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` > 0 and the phase is visible under MehrgoDriver → Build Phases in Xcode; (b) an archive build's log shows the Crashlytics `run` script executing and reporting an uploaded UUID; (c) `mdfind`/Xcode Organizer confirms a .dSYM is produced for the Release archive; (d) a deliberately-crashed TestFlight build appears in the Firebase Crashlytics console for project `mehrgo-driver` with a fully symbolicated Swift stack (function names, not hex addresses) within ~5 minutes.

README.md:43 reads: '**Drop in your `GoogleService-Info.plist`** at `MehrgoDriver/GoogleService-Info.plist` (not checked in), then uncomment `FirebaseApp.configure()` in `MehrgoDriverApp.init()` and `Messaging.messaging()` references in `AppDelegate` / `PushNotificationCenter`.' README.md:128 (under '## Known Stubs') reads: '**Firebase** — `MehrgoDriverApp.init()` and `AppDelegate` have `import FirebaseCore` / `Messaging.messaging()` calls commented out. Rewrite both to state the actual design: `MehrgoDriver/GoogleService-Info.plist` ships in the repo for Firebase project `mehrgo-driver` and is in Copy Bundle Resources; `FirebaseApp.configure()` runs at MehrgoDriverApp.swift:32-36 behind a `Bundle.main.path(forResource:"GoogleService-Info", ofType:"plist")` existence check; and `AppDelegate.swift:40`, `AppDelegate.swift:81` and `Shared/PushNotificationCenter.swift:81` each guard their Messaging call with `if FirebaseApp.app() != nil`, so a plist-less checkout still launches. Move the Firebase bullet out of '## Known Stubs' entirely — it is not a stub. Also correct the parenthetical '(not checked in)' on line 43.

**iOS target** — C:/Users/Umar Inagamjanov/Downloads/mehrgo-driver-ios-main/README.md line 43 (Bootstrap step 5) and line 128 (the '**Firebase**' bullet under '## Known Stubs', which begins at line 120)

> **Gotchas.** README.md:22 is CORRECT and must be left alone — it accurately lists 'Firebase iOS SDK (FirebaseMessaging, FirebaseCrashlytics, FirebaseAnalytics)' as an SPM dependency. README.md:104 (the M9 Polish row mentioning Crashlytics) is a build-status table row, not part of this fix. If gap #1 lands, the Bootstrap section should also gain a one-line note that dSYM upload is automatic via the postCompileScripts phase.

#### 21.3 `[P2]` Missing GoogleService-Info.plist degrades to a log line — a misconfigured build could silently ship with no push, no crash reporting, no analytics

**What to build**

CONFIRMED — MehrgoDriverApp.swift:32-36 is exactly:

    if Bundle.main.path(forResource: "GoogleService-Info", ofType: "plist") != nil {
        FirebaseApp.configure()
    } else {
        os_log(.error, "APP-MehrgoDriverApp: GoogleService-Info.plist missing — Firebase disabled, push notifications + driver/start will not work")
    }

and every downstream Firebase call is individually guarded (`AppDelegate.swift:40`, `AppDelegate.swift:81`, `Shared/PushNotificationCenter.swift:81`), so a plist-less build launches cleanly, sends no FCM token, reports no crashes, and nothing ever surfaces to QA.

PREFERRED FIX — build-time, not runtime, because it reproduces Android's behaviour exactly and costs nothing at launch. Add to `project.yml` under target `MehrgoDriver`, alongside the `postCompileScripts` from gap #1:

    preBuildScripts:
      - name: Require GoogleService-Info.plist
        script: |
          test -f "$SRCROOT/MehrgoDriver/GoogleService-Info.plist" || { echo "error: GoogleService-Info.plist missing — Firebase (push, Crashlytics, Analytics) would be silently disabled"; exit 1; }
        basedOnDependencyAnalysis: false

then `xcodegen generate`. Leave the runtime guard in MehrgoDriverApp.swift exactly as-is — with the build gate in place it becomes pure belt-and-braces, and replacing it with `preconditionFailure` would trade a silent misconfiguration for a launch crash, which is strictly worse for a shipping app. Do NOT implement both the `#if DEBUG` / `#else preconditionFailure` variant and the build script; pick the build script.

**Android reference** — app/build.gradle:9 (`id 'com.google.gms.google-services'`, plugin 4.4.4 declared at root build.gradle:19) — the plugin fails the Gradle build with 'File google-services.json is missing. The Google Services Plugin cannot function without it.' app/google-services.json is committed at the module root.

**iOS target** — PRIMARY: C:/Users/Umar Inagamjanov/Downloads/mehrgo-driver-ios-main/project.yml — target `MehrgoDriver`, add `preBuildScripts:` as a sibling of `dependencies:`. SECONDARY (leave unchanged): C:/Users/Umar Inagamjanov/Downloads/mehrgo-driver-ios-main/MehrgoDriver/MehrgoDriverApp.swift:32-36

> **Gotchas.** 1) Priority is correctly P2, not higher: the plist IS currently committed (verified present, 884 bytes, and NOT matched by .gitignore) and IS in Copy Bundle Resources (project.pbxproj:64/278/413/766) — so this is regression-hardening against a future accidental removal, not a live defect. It pairs naturally with gap #2, whose stale README text ('not checked in') is precisely what could lead someone to delete it. 2) `$SRCROOT` checks the source tree, not the built bundle — a plist present on disk but dropped from Copy Bundle Resources would still pass; if you want the stronger check, test `$BUILT_PRODUCTS_DIR/$CONTENTS_FOLDER_PATH/GoogleService-Info.plist` in a postCompileScript instead. 3) The `else` branch's os_log message is accurate and should stay — it is the only signal on a dev checkout that predates the build gate. 4) Do not 'fix' the plist's `IS_ANALYTICS_ENABLED = false` key while in this file — it is a legacy key ignored by modern Firebase (runtime control is `FIREBASE_ANALYTICS_COLLECTION_ENABLED` in Info.plist, absent ⇒ collection ON); changing it does nothing and invites confusion.

**Acceptance** — Temporarily rename MehrgoDriver/GoogleService-Info.plist and build: the build FAILS with the 'error: GoogleService-Info.plist missing' message surfaced in the Xcode issue navigator (matching Android's google-services plugin failure). Restore the file and the build succeeds unchanged. A normal build shows no new warnings and no measurable launch-time cost.

## 10. Additional verified finding — localization coverage

Found while auditing area coverage after pass 2. Filed here rather than re-running a whole pass, because it is a single, directly verifiable fact.

---

### 22. Localization — Kazakh and Kyrgyz are entirely absent on iOS

**Gaps:** 2 total — P0: 0 · P1: 1 · P2: 1

#### iOS today

`MehrgoDriver/Localization/` contains exactly three bundles: `en.lproj`, `ru.lproj`, `uz.lproj`. Of those, **`en.lproj` holds only `InfoPlist.strings`** — it has no `Localizable.strings` at all. So the app ships **two** real UI languages, `uz` (436 keys) and `ru` (436 keys). There is no `kk.lproj` and no `ky.lproj`; they are not stubbed, not seeded, not empty — they do not exist.

#### Android today

`app/src/main/res/` ships **four** complete locales: `values/` (uz, default, 487 strings), `values-kk/` (485), `values-ky/` (485), `values-ru-rRU/` (487). `bundle.language.enableSplit = false` in `app/build.gradle` deliberately keeps every translation in every APK split so a language switch never needs a store download. The project convention (`CLAUDE.md`) is that **any new user-facing string is added to all four locales in the same change**.

<a id="gap-22-1"></a>

#### 22.1 `[P1]` Add Kazakh (`kk`) and Kyrgyz (`ky`) localizations

**What to build**

Create `MehrgoDriver/Localization/kk.lproj/Localizable.strings` and `ky.lproj/Localizable.strings`, register both in `project.yml` under the target's `resources` (the folder is already listed as `MehrgoDriver/Localization`, so confirm new `.lproj` folders are picked up by `xcodegen generate` — they are copied as a folder reference, but verify the build actually bundles them), and add both language codes to `CFBundleLocalizations` in `Info.plist`.

Source the translations from the Android bundles, which are already complete and reviewed: `app/src/main/res/values-kk/strings.xml` (485 strings) and `values-ky/strings.xml` (485). Note the key spaces differ — Android uses `snake_case` XML `name` attributes, iOS uses whatever key style `uz.lproj/Localizable.strings` already uses; map by English/Uzbek meaning, not by key name.

Also decide what `en.lproj` is for. Today it carries only `InfoPlist.strings`, so an English-locale device falls back to the development language (`uz`). Either give it a real `Localizable.strings` or drop the bundle so the fallback is explicit rather than accidental.

**Android reference** — `app/src/main/res/values-kk/strings.xml`, `values-ky/strings.xml`; split behavior at `app/build.gradle` (`bundle.language.enableSplit = false`); the all-four-locales rule in `CLAUDE.md`

**iOS target** — new `MehrgoDriver/Localization/kk.lproj/Localizable.strings`, `ky.lproj/Localizable.strings`; `project.yml`; `MehrgoDriver/Info.plist` (`CFBundleLocalizations`)

> **Gotchas.** This is a **commercial** gap, not a cosmetic one. The same source is white-labeled into brands operating in **Kazakhstan and Kyrgyzstan** (see `CLAUDE.md` — Karavan, Alo, Elga and others), and `Constants.PHONE_NUMBER_SIZE` already differs per country (UZ/KG 13, KZ 12). A KZ or KG driver on iOS today silently gets Uzbek. Treat `kk`/`ky` as release-blocking for those brands even though they are P1 for the Mehrgo-branded build. Also: iOS has 436 keys against Android's 487 — do not assume a 1:1 mapping exists; the delta needs a reconciliation pass, and some of it will be Android-only strings that need no iOS counterpart.

**Acceptance** — Set the device language to Kazakh, then Kyrgyz. Every screen renders in that language with no Uzbek fallback strings. Switching language in-app (Settings → Language) applies without a relaunch, matching the existing uz/ru behavior.

<a id="gap-22-2"></a>

#### 22.2 `[P2]` Correct the README's localization claim

**What to build**

`README.md` states: *"kk / ky localizations — currently seeded from `uz.lproj/Localizable.strings`. Hand off to translators; the key list is stable."* That is **false** — neither bundle exists. Update the line to state the real position (two shipping languages, `kk`/`ky` not started) so nobody plans a "translation pass" against files that were never created.

This is the **second** stale claim found in that README; the first is the Firebase one (§21). Both point at the same risk: the README is being trusted as a status document while drifting from the code. Worth a full read-through against reality in the same change.

**Android reference** — n/a (documentation accuracy)

**iOS target** — `README.md` ("Known Stubs" section)

**Acceptance** — README's Known Stubs section matches what a fresh `ls MehrgoDriver/Localization/` actually shows.
