## 11. Detailed gap reports — pass 3 (surfaces that EXIST on iOS but diverge)

Passes 1 and 2 hunted for things iOS was **missing**. Every file in this pass already exists on iOS, so the finding to look for is not absence but **divergence** — different thresholds, different endpoints, stale data, double-counting.

**This pass was the most productive of the three.** It produced five P0s, three of which are quantified money divergences: the same trip settles to a different number on the two platforms. That is the strongest argument in this document for treating the fare path as one system rather than two implementations.

---

### 23. Trip Services sheet (Xizmatlar) — rider add-ons applied during a trip, and how they enter the fare

**Gaps:** 4 total — P0: 2 · P1: 1 · P2: 1

#### iOS today

VERIFIED: iOS has the full read surface and it matches the claim. `TripServicesSheet.swift` renders the applied add-ons (summary header at :108, "activated_services" section at :71, orange non-tappable `ServiceRow` from :180, `activeServices = vm.services.filter { ($0.total ?? 0) > 0 }` at :162), presented from `TripView.swift:165-166` via `.sheet(isPresented: $showServices)` (opened at TripView.swift:860 and :1063), with the in-panel activated card reading the same filter at TripView.swift:1148. `TripMVVM.loadServices()` (:799-814) hits `GET service/order?id=` — the same endpoint Android's `getOrderServices` uses — and `reloadServicesIfDue()` (:825-829) debounces re-pulls at 3s, driven from both the `order_price_updated` socket sink (:986-1005) and the GPS-batch fare ack sink (:1009-1024). The read-only directive is real and documented at TripMVVM.swift:832-837; `POST order/item` returns ZERO hits across the whole repo (greps for `order/item`, `toggleService`, `OrderServiceAction`, `serviceEnabled` find only the dead enum at DomainEnums.swift:169-170 and the two unused `SoundPlayer` cases at :45-46). Money-wise the sheet is fully decoupled from settlement: `FareCalculator.servicesTotal` (:150) sums `order.services` off the CACHED `Order` struct, which is only ever reassigned at TripMVVM.swift:150 (init), :408/:467/:525 (the arrive/go/start POST responses) and :977 (the `.accepted` socket echo) — there is no `order.services =` merge from `vm.services` anywhere, and `finish()` (:609-665) does no refresh before `makeBreakdown`. `finish()` passes `serverGross: nil` (:659), so the `.server` branch at FareCalculator.swift:188-218 is dead in production and only pinned by FareCalculatorTests.swift:625-646.

#### Android today

Android's settlement lives in `MapFragment.setViewDialogTrackingFinish` (MapFragment.kt:2636-2876, invoked from its wrapper `showDialogTrackingFinish` at :2607-2634). CONFIRMED: `agreedTotal = Helper.roundPrice(order.price.toLong() + waitLine)` (MapFragment.kt:2723-2724) — services are NOT added to the agreed arm, while the taximeter arm does add them (`sp + additionalPrice!! + totalServicePrice`, :2711-2722). This is internally consistent with Android's frozen fixed-route hero, which shows raw `order.price` with nothing added (`calculateTotalPrice` :3331-3335, `applyServerFareToTrackingViews` :3369-3373). Android re-fetches the order at finish: `prepareOrderFinish` (:2529) calls `getUserMe()`, takes `orders.firstOrNull()`, reassigns `order`, and recomputes `totalServicePrice` from `order.services` (:2580-2585) before showing the receipt — so the receipt bills the fresh list. Android also parses the protocol-v4 `live` block (`FareLive.kt:18-37`: `distance_km`, `to_client_km`, `waiting_sec`, `on_way_sec`, `waiting_cost`, `services_price`, `podacha`, `extra_price`, `fare`, `price`, `agreed_price`, `surcharge`, `kept_projection`, `waiting_billing`, all money as `Double?` after a Gson `NumberFormatException` crash on `waiting_cost: 2613.33`), wired via `applyServerFare` (:238-253) into the `serverPriceIncludesExtras` flag (:236, :242) that prevents double-adding services onto `live.price` (:2711-2717) and drives the receipt services row from `live.services_price + podacha + extra_price` (:2679-2691). Top-level `price` is still decoded alongside `live` (FareResponse.kt:23, :28), so both shapes coexist on the wire. Note what Android does NOT have: there is no driver-side service EDITING anywhere in the shipping UI — the trip screen only reads the applied list (MapFragment.kt:2580-2585). An `AddRemoveServiceUC` → `ApiService.addRemoveService` (ApiService.kt:279) endpoint exists in the data layer, but nothing reachable calls it.

<a id="gap-23-1"></a>

#### 23.1 `[P0]` Quoted (agreed-price) settlement double-bills services — iOS adds servicesFloor on top of order.price, Android does not

**What to build**

Make the quoted arm match Android: `let quotedTotal: Double = roundTo100((order.price ?? 0).rounded(.towardZero) + waitChargeFloor)`. Keep `servicesFloor` in `calculatedGross` (FareCalculator.swift:169-172) — Android's taximeter arm adds it too (MapFragment.kt:2699, :2701-2705). Rewrite the comment block at FareCalculator.swift:174-178, which asserts the opposite of what ships. Note this ALSO fixes an iOS-internal inconsistency the report did not mention: `displayedFare` (TripMVVM.swift:352-354) returns raw `order.price` for a fixed-route trip and never adds services, so today's receipt exceeds the frozen hero the driver stared at all trip by exactly the service amount — Android's hero (MapFragment.kt:3313-3318, :3352-3356) and its `agreedTotal` agree with each other. Two tests pin the wrong semantics and MUST be updated in the same commit: `testComplete_quoted_addsServicesOnTop` (FareCalculatorTests.swift:650-669, expects 55 000 = 50 000 + 5 000) and, if you also strip the mirror `+ servicesFloor` at FareCalculator.swift:217, `testComplete_serverGross_addsServicesOnTop` (:625-646). Leaving :217 alone is defensible short-term — `finish()` passes `serverGross: nil` (TripMVVM.swift:659) so that branch is dead in production — but the comment at :196-207 should stop asserting a receipt observation that Android's shipped code contradicts.

**The blocking empirical question is RESOLVED — proceed.** Prod capture (order 77041, 2026-07-20) shows `order.price` DOES already contain booking-time add-ons: tariff `starting_price` 5 000 + one 10 000 add-on + 0 km = `order.price` **15 000**, with `breakdown.estimate` = `{price: 15000, services_price: 10000, podacha: 0, extra_price: 0}` confirming the decomposition. Android's assumption holds; the iOS comment at FareCalculator.swift:196-207 is factually wrong and must be deleted, not merely softened. The same capture also resolves the mid-trip case this section's gotcha flags: a second add-on applied after booking left `estimate.services_price` at 10 000 while `live.services_price` rose to 12 000, so the server itself distinguishes "already inside `order.price`" from "added since". Bill the delta (`live.services_price − estimate.services_price`), never the whole `servicesFloor`.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/maps/yandex_map/MapFragment.kt:2723-2724 (`agreedTotal = Helper.roundPrice(order.price.toLong() + waitLine)` — no services); taximeter arm contrast at :2711-2722; frozen agreed hero at :3331-3335 (`calculateTotalPrice`) and :3369-3373 (`applyServerFareToTrackingViews`)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/FareCalculator.swift:179 (`quotedTotal`), comment at :174-178; mirror at :217 with comment :196-207; consumed at :233. Tests: MehrgoDriverTests/FareCalculatorTests.swift:650-669 and :625-646

> **Gotchas.** Do NOT also drop `servicesFloor` from `calculatedGross` — the taximeter arm legitimately bills services and Android does the same; dropping both would under-bill every open/diverged trip. The quoted arm is selected only at FareCalculator.swift:232-234 (near C OR drove the planned distance), so a naive manual test that finishes far from C will hit the taximeter arm and show no change. `order.price` is also the value `isFixedRoute` (TripMVVM.swift:336-345) freezes the hero to, so any change here must keep hero and receipt equal. This compounds with gap 2: because the services come from a stale cached order, a rider add-on applied mid-trip that the server folded into a recomputed `order.price` gets counted twice today.

**Acceptance** — Given a booked order quoted at 50 000 with one 5 000 add-on and no wait, finishing within 300 m of C settles `total_price` = 50 000, not 55 000 — identical to what Android's `agreedTotal` submits for the same inputs. The receipt total equals the price frozen in the trip hero for the whole ride. `FareCalculatorTests` compiles and passes with the two service-on-top expectations rewritten.

<a id="gap-23-2"></a>

#### 23.2 `[P0]` Settlement services are read from a STALE cached order — iOS never re-fetches before finishing, and the sheet and the receipt read different arrays

**What to build**

Collapse the two sources into one. Preferred (Android parity): at the top of `finish()`, before `makeBreakdown`, `GET user/me`, take `orders.first`, and use that fresh `Order` for the compute — exactly what `MapFragment.prepareOrderFinish` does (MapFragment.kt:2534-2570), including its two hard-won guards: an empty `orders` array must NOT crash or settle (Android toasts `no_active_orders` and bails, :2550-2555) and a partial payload with `services == nil` must keep the previous total rather than zeroing it (:2563-2568). Minimal alternative: add a `servicesTotal: Double?` override parameter to `FareCalculator.compute` and have `finish()` pass `vm.services.filter { ($0.total ?? 0) > 0 }.reduce(0) { $0 + ($1.total ?? 0) }` (the socket-refreshed array), then point `TripFinishView.activeServices` (:127) at `vm.services` instead of `order.services` so the receipt's itemised list and its `services_total` row (:550) describe the same set. Either way the trip-panel card (TripView.swift:1148), the sheet (TripServicesSheet.swift:162), the receipt list and the submitted `total_price` must all resolve from one array.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/maps/yandex_map/MapFragment.kt:2512-2588 (`prepareOrderFinish`: parallel `getFare` + blocking `getUserMe` → reassign `order` → recompute `totalServicePrice` from `order.services` → `showDialogTrackingFinish`); empty-orders guard :2550-2555; nil-services guard :2560-2568

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MVVM/TripMVVM.swift:609-665 (`finish()` — builds `makeBreakdown` off the cached `order` with no refresh) feeding MehrgoDriver/Shared/FareCalculator.swift:150 (`(order.services ?? []).reduce`); receipt list at MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Home/TripFinishView.swift:127 (`order.services`) and row at :550 (`breakdown.servicesTotal`). The cached `order` is only reassigned at TripMVVM.swift:150, :408, :467, :525, :977 — none of them fires after boarding. Fresh source used by the UI: TripServicesSheet.swift:162 and TripView.swift:1148 (`vm.services`).

> **Gotchas.** After gap 1 lands, stale services still change money through the taximeter arm (`calculatedGross`, FareCalculator.swift:169-172), which is reached on open trips, no-GPS finishes, and >1 km route divergence — so this is not made moot by fixing gap 1. `vm.services` items carry `total` mapped from `enabled → price` by the dual-shape decoder (SharedModels.swift:613-710), so `total > 0` is the right applied-filter, and the sheet already uses it; do not switch to a different predicate for settlement. If you take the `GET user/me` route, keep it on the blocking path (Android gates its Finish slider on it) but preserve the existing `isTransitioning`/`didFinish` double-finish guards at TripMVVM.swift:619 and make sure a failed refresh does not leave the button permanently dead — Android needed a `finally` block for exactly this (:2580-2586).

**Acceptance** — A rider enables a 5 000 add-on AFTER the driver taps Kettik; the driver finishes on an open/diverged trip. The sheet, the trip-panel activated card, the receipt's itemised service list, the receipt's `services_total` row and the submitted `total_price` all reflect the 5 000 — today the first two do and the last three do not. A `user/me` response with an empty `orders` array aborts the finish with a message instead of crashing or settling at zero services.

<a id="gap-23-3"></a>

#### 23.3 `[P1]` iOS does not parse the `live` fare block, so it has no services_price/podacha/extra_price and its in-trip fare card under-reports

**What to build**

Add a `FareLive` struct decoded from the `live` key on `FareSummary`, 1:1 with Android's `FareLive.kt:18-37`: `distance_km`, `to_client_km`, `waiting_sec`, `on_way_sec`, `waiting_cost`, `services_price`, `podacha`, `extra_price`, `fare`, `price`, `agreed_price`, `surcharge`, `kept_projection`, `waiting_billing`. Then prefer it exactly as `applyServerFare` does (MapFragment.kt:240-245): `price = live.price ?? topLevel.price`, `distanceKm = live.distance_km ?? topLevel.distance_km`, and carry Android's `serverPriceIncludesExtras = live.price != nil` flag. The concrete payoff is the in-trip card, not settlement: `displayedFare` (TripMVVM.swift:374) reads the LEGACY top-level `price`, which per Android's own note (MapFragment.kt:233-235) omits services (backend bug 76967) and has no access to `podacha`/`extra_price` at all — so on the same free-mode order Android's hero shows the complete `live.price` and iOS shows less. Also use `live.services_price + live.podacha + live.extra_price` for the receipt's services row when the live block drove the total, mirroring MapFragment.kt:2662-2670, so the rows reconcile to the subtotal instead of drifting.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/domain/model/fare/FareLive.kt:18-37 (model + the Double-vs-Long crash note); app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/domain/model/fare/FareResponse.kt:27-30 (`live` + `events`); MapFragment.kt:231-236 (`latestLive` + `serverPriceIncludesExtras` fields), :238-253 (`applyServerFare` ingest), :2679-2691 (receipt services row), :2711-2717 (no-double-add guard)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/Models/GpsBatch.swift:62-119 — `FareSummary` decodes only `order_id`, `distance_km`, `in_city_km`, `out_city_km`, `waiting_sec`, `traffic_sec`, `price`, `fare`. Case-insensitive greps for `FareLive`, `services_price`, `podacha`, `extra_price`, `kept_projection`, `waiting_billing`, `to_client_km` across MehrgoDriver/ return nothing (the one `podacha` hit, HomeMVVM.swift:152, is a DEBUG mock order body, not a fare). Consumers: TripMVVM.swift:374 (`displayedFare`), :990 and :1014 (`self.serverFare = event.summary`), FareCalculator.swift `serverGross` path at :188-218.

> **Gotchas.** ALL money fields must be `Double?` — the backend sends fractional money (`waiting_cost: 2613.33`) and Android crashed with a Gson NumberFormatException when this was typed as Long; `FareSummary` already routes everything through `FlexDecode` (GpsBatch.swift:89-99), so extend that pattern rather than plain `Decodable` synthesis. The claimed double-count risk is currently theoretical on iOS, not live: `finish()` passes `serverGross: nil` (TripMVVM.swift:659), so the `serverGross + servicesFloor` line at FareCalculator.swift:217 never executes in production — priority is driven by the under-reporting live card, not by settlement. Top-level `price` is still on the wire alongside `live` (FareResponse.kt:23 and :28 coexist), so keep the fallback; do not hard-require the live block. Android's protocol-v4 work also consumed `events` (waiting_charge_started/step sounds) and the `surcharge`/`kept_projection` hint — those are separate surfaces, scope them out of this item unless the product owner asks.

**Acceptance** — On a free-mode trip with an add-on applied, the iOS in-trip fare card shows the same figure as the Android hero for the same order (both = `live.price`). The receipt's services row equals `live.services_price + live.podacha + live.extra_price` when the live block supplied the total, and base + distance + wait + services rows foot to the subtotal. A `live` payload with `waiting_cost: 2613.33` decodes without throwing.

<a id="gap-23-4"></a>

#### 23.4 `[P2]` Neither platform ships a driver-side add/remove path — Android has the `POST order/item` endpoint but no reachable caller

**What to build**

Nothing to code — and the premise that the two platforms contradict each other does not survive checking. iOS deleted the toggle by explicit directive (TripMVVM.swift:832-837). Android has the write path in its DATA layer (`AddRemoveServiceUC` → `MainRepository.addRemoveService` → `ApiService.addRemoveService`, ApiService.kt:279) but **nothing in the shipping UI reaches it** — the trip screen renders the applied services read-only (MapFragment.kt:2580-2585). So both platforms are read-only in production today and there is no live divergence to close.

What remains is a product question, not a port: should a driver be able to add or remove services mid-trip at all? If **no**, the Android write path is dead weight and can be deleted (`AddRemoveServiceUC`, `MainRepository.addRemoveService` MainRepository.kt:63, `MainRepositoryImpl` :125-131, `ApiService.addRemoveService` ApiService.kt:279). If **yes**, it is new work on BOTH platforms: iOS restores `toggleService` → `POST order/item` with `order_id`/`service_id`/`action` (0=remove, 1=add) plus the driver's lat/long/accuracy, wiring the already-declared-and-unused `OrderServiceAction` (DomainEnums.swift:169-170) and `SoundPlayer.serviceEnabled`/`.serviceDisabled` (:45-46), and re-fetches the order after a successful toggle so the fare follows — and Android needs a UI built for it.

**Android reference** — write path with no reachable caller: domain/use_case/main/AddRemoveServiceUC.kt; domain/repository/MainRepository.kt:63; data/repository/MainRepositoryImpl.kt:125-131; data/remote/ApiService.kt:279. Live READ path: app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/maps/yandex_map/MapFragment.kt:2580-2585

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MVVM/TripMVVM.swift:832-837 — the removal note ("a driver-side removal deleted the rider's service on both sides"); dead symbols at MehrgoDriver/Shared/DomainEnums.swift:169-170 (`OrderServiceAction`) and MehrgoDriver/Shared/SoundPlayer.swift:45-46; the read-only contract is stated at MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Home/TripServicesSheet.swift:12-20 and enforced by the non-tappable row at :180

> **Gotchas.** Do not build the toggle without fixing gap 2 first — a driver-side toggle mutating a stale `order.services` compounds the settlement error rather than fixing it. Also note the payload is dual-shape: the trip screen reads `service.total` (MapFragment.kt:2580-2585) but the API can also return `service.value`/`service.enabled`, which is why iOS's decoder normalises both (SharedModels.swift:613-710). Any cleanup of the unused Android write path must leave that read path intact.

**Acceptance** — A written product decision recorded in the plan, plus whichever branch it selects executed end to end: (a) the Android symbols above are gone and the services dialog renders non-tappable rows on both Android UI modes, with the existing read/display of applied services unchanged; or (b) an iOS driver toggle round-trips through `POST order/item`, the order is re-fetched, and the trip card, sheet and receipt all move together within one refresh.

---

### 24. Departure-confirm sheet + wait-meter stop/resume semantics (paid waiting lifecycle: arm at pickup, auto-start ride on departure, on-route auto-stop, kill-recovery, and how the wait reaches the server)

**Gaps:** 7 total — P0: 2 · P1: 3 · P2: 2

#### iOS today

Thresholds are confirmed 1:1 with Android: `Constants.swift:55/60/63` (`minimalSpeedKmh = 7`, `onRouteWaitMaxStartSpeedKmh = 10`, `onRouteWaitResumeSpeedKmh = 20`) match `Constants.kt:296/300/314` (`MINIMAL_SPEED=7`, `ON_ROUTE_WAIT_MAX_START_SPEED_KMH=10`, `WAIT_AUTO_STOP_SPEED_KMH=20`), and `HomeMVVM.swift:97/101`'s `departureConfirmSpeedKmh = 20` matches `AUTO_START_RIDE_SPEED_KMH=20` (the 3 km/h re-arm floor is an iOS-only refinement). `TripMVVM.arrive()` (TripMVVM.swift:435-495) geofences at 500 m then calls `markArrivedAtPickup()` + `startWaiting()` + `WaitActivityManager.start`, so the pickup wait auto-arms exactly like Android; `TripView.swift:928-940` blocks a manual wait start above 10 km/h. Wait accrual is pure wall clock, timestamp-anchored via `waitSegmentStartedAt` (LocationTracker.swift:505-557), persisted every 5 s, and `restoreLifecyclePhase` (TripMVVM.swift:242-284) correctly re-arms the PICKUP wait after a kill. `armOnRouteWaitStopIfNeeded` (HomeMVVM.swift:537-547) does call `stopWaiting()` headlessly from the Combine sink, so the on-route auto-stop needs no work. Two corrections to the claimed state: (a) `DepartureConfirmSheet.swift` is NOT dead — its `.departureConfirm` modifier is still used at TripView.swift:148 for the FINISH prompt, only the departure binding was removed; (b) a failed auto-start IS retried — `TripMVVM.swift:573` posts `.tripStartFailed` and `HomeMVVM.swift:589-593` clears `hasShownDepartureConfirm` so the next speed tick re-fires (the uncovered hole is the silent `guard !isTransitioning` return in `startTrip()`, which posts nothing). iOS remains ahead on the Dynamic Island wait Live Activity (`WaitActivityManager`).

#### Android today

Android runs the whole wait lifecycle headlessly in `MyTrackingService`. The speed observer at MyTrackingService.kt:427-450 does two things above 20 km/h: at the pickup (`isArrivedAtPickup`) it latches `rideAutoStartInFlight` and calls `autoStartRide()` (MyTrackingService.kt:1380-1432), which POSTs the Go transition from the service — the comment states explicitly "Runs in the service, so it works even while the app is backgrounded" — and only inside `Resource.Success` runs `onGoConfirmed()` (freeze `waitedTimeUntilGone`, re-arm uploader, `addEmptyPolyline`) and posts the `ride_started_auto` notice; `Resource.Error` just clears the latch so the next speed tick retries. Mid-ride it flips `isWaiting` false and shows `wait_auto_stopped_*`. `startWaitingTimer()` (MyTrackingService.kt:975-1017) sets a `was_waiting` pref, writes a `wait_last_tick` heartbeat every 5 s and calls `gpsBatchUploader.nudge()` on the same beat; `beginTracking` (MyTrackingService.kt:785-805) credits the dead gap after a process kill to BOTH `timeWait` AND `timeTrack` ("Keep waits ≤ execution_time (the backend proportionally shrinks otherwise)") and resumes the ticker regardless of stage. The wait reaches the server twice: live via `FareGpsBatchRequest` (`waiting_time` / `waiting_time_ontheway`, GpsBatchUploader.kt:331-367 including a POINTS-LESS flush when only the timers changed, with per-field change detection in `pendingTimers()` at :392-397), and at settlement via `RequestOrderFinish` (waiting_time = `waitedTimeUntilGone.coerceAtMost(total)` gated on state ≥ CHANGED_GONE, `waiting_time_ontheway` = the remainder — MapFragment.kt:2849-2871, split at :2853-2858). Local wait pricing uses whole-minute integer steps per slice (MapFragment.kt:3168-3218).

<a id="gap-24-1"></a>

#### 24.1 `[P0]` order/complete sends the COMBINED wait as `waiting_time` — the on-route slice is billed at the pickup tariff

**What to build**

Split the wait before POSTing `order/complete`, exactly as Android does. `waiting_time` = pickup slice only: `min(ticker.waitedTimeUntilGoneMs, ticker.waitedTimeMs)` but ONLY once `currentState.rawValue >= OrderState.changedGone.rawValue` — before Kettik `waitedTimeUntilGoneMs` is still 0 and the whole wait IS the pickup wait, so fall back to `ticker.waitedTimeMs` there. Add `waiting_time_ontheway` = `max(0, waitedTimeMs - pickupMs)`. Both in milliseconds, sent as Strings. The backend treats a missing `waiting_time_ontheway` as 0, so this is purely additive and safe to ship before any backend change.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/maps/yandex_map/MapFragment.kt:2849-2871 (state-gated split at :2853-2858, sent in `RequestOrderFinish` at :2859-2871) and domain/model/requests/RequestOrderFinish.kt:8-19 (both fields, documented contract)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MVVM/TripMVVM.swift:673 (`let totalWaitingMs: Int64 = ticker.waitedTimeMs`) and the `submit(_:)` body at :689-697, which sends only `waiting_time` + `execution_time`

> **Gotchas.** Do NOT add the two slices into a bigger `waiting_time` — `waitedTimeUntilGoneMs` is a SUBSET of `waitedTimeMs` (LocationTracker.swift:322-341 snapshots the running total at boarding), so summing them double-bills. The clamp matters: `waitedTimeUntilGoneMs` is restored from CalculationStore and could momentarily exceed the live total after a kill-recovery. Note the local `FareCalculator` already splits correctly for the DISPLAYED receipt — only the wire payload is wrong, so the driver's receipt and the server's settlement can silently disagree today.

**Acceptance** — Arrive → wait 2 min → Kettik → manually wait 3 min mid-ride → finish. The `order/complete` body carries `waiting_time` ≈ 120000 and `waiting_time_ontheway` ≈ 180000; the backend prices the second slice with `price_of_waiting_on_way`. Finishing from `.changedArrived` (no Kettik) still sends the full wait in `waiting_time` and `"0"` on-the-way.

<a id="gap-24-2"></a>

#### 24.2 `[P0]` Departure auto-start is view-driven on iOS — no headless path, and it can be silently swallowed

**What to build**

Move the state transition out of the view layer. `armDepartureConfirmIfNeeded` should perform the `order-change/state → changedGone` POST itself (or via a HomeMVVM-owned async method) with its own in-flight latch, mirroring Android's `autoStartRide()`; on success run the side effects `startTrip()` runs today — `LocationTracker.markPassengerOnboard()`, the `passengerOnboardAt` stamp, `WaitActivityManager.end()`, `SoundPlayer.play(.tripStarted)`, `syncActiveOrder` — and on failure clear the latch so the next speed tick retries. Keep the TripView/MainView `onChange` handlers as UI reactions to the already-committed state (expand the panel, refresh the sheet), never as the trigger for the network call.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/services/MyTrackingService.kt:427-436 (speed observer arms it in the service) and :1380-1432 `autoStartRide()` + :1327-1372 `onGoConfirmed()` (shared success side effects, in-flight latch cleared on both Success and Error)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MVVM/HomeMVVM.swift:491-526 (`armDepartureConfirmIfNeeded` sets `shouldConfirmDeparture` and stops) → MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Home/TripView.swift:207-211 and :222-225, MehrgoDriver/View/Main/MainView.swift:136-139. The ONLY `changedGone` POST in the repo is TripMVVM.swift:509-524, reachable exclusively from `startTrip()`

> **Gotchas.** Two concrete holes, one certain and one unverified. CERTAIN: `startTrip()` opens with `guard !isTransitioning else { return }` (TripMVVM.swift:504) and that early return posts no `.tripStartFailed`, so if the auto-start lands while any other transition is in flight the ride never starts and `hasShownDepartureConfirm` stays latched until the car drops below 3 km/h — Android instead clears `rideAutoStartInFlight` and retries on the very next speed tick. UNVERIFIED (measure it on device before sizing the fix): whether SwiftUI delivers `.onChange` at all while the app is backgrounded — the panel itself stays mounted (MainView.swift:59-63 keeps `TripPanel` alive at any detent, and there is no TabView), so mounting is not the risk, delivery is. Do NOT freeze/stop the pickup wait at arm time — Android deliberately leaves it running until Go actually confirms, so a failed transition doesn't silently lose billable wait.

**Acceptance** — With the app backgrounded and the trip at `.changedArrived`, drive past 20 km/h: the `changedGone` POST fires from HomeMVVM (verify in the server log / os_log), the pickup wait freezes, and the trip is in `.changedGone` when the app is brought forward. Repeat with a transition artificially in flight — the auto-start retries on a later speed tick instead of being dropped.

<a id="gap-24-3"></a>

#### 24.3 `[P1]` iOS never uploads the wait timers in `order-gps/batch` — the server's live fare is blind to waiting

**What to build**

Add `waiting_time` and `waiting_time_ontheway` (ms) to the `order-gps/batch` body, tracked with last-acked change detection so a field is attached only when it grew. Critically, allow a POINTS-LESS flush that carries only the timers: a stationary driver on a paid wait emits no GPS fixes, so today nothing is sent at all. Nudge the uploader from the wait ticker (Android fires `nudge()` every 5 s from inside the wait loop) so the live price reacts within seconds instead of at the next fix.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/fare/GpsBatchUploader.kt:331-367 (points-less timer-only batch + timers attached to normal batches) and :392-397 (`pendingTimers()` change detection); nudge source at common/services/MyTrackingService.kt:996-1003

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/GpsBatchUploader.swift:312 (`if points.isEmpty { return }` aborts the flush unconditionally) and :371-383 (`sendREST` body is `["points": ...]` and nothing else); request model at MehrgoDriver/Shared/Models/GpsBatch.swift:33-35

> **Gotchas.** Downgraded from the claimed P0: this does NOT change what the driver is paid. `order/complete` carries the authoritative wait (gap #1) and the backend keeps a per-field MAX, so the impact is confined to the live in-trip fare card and the `waiting_sec` echoed back in `FareSummary` (GpsBatch.swift:67) reading 0 all trip. The points-less path must not reset `lastFlushAt`/drain bookkeeping in a way that starves the point path, and it must honour the same 403/404 give-up streak — otherwise a terminal order keeps hammering every flush interval just because the wait timer ticks.

**Acceptance** — Park the car on a pickup wait with the app open. Within ~5 s of each 5 s tick a `order-gps/batch` POST goes out with zero points and a growing `waiting_time`; the returned `FareSummary.waitingSec` tracks it and the live fare card grows. Once the timers stop changing, no more empty batches are sent.

<a id="gap-24-4"></a>

#### 24.4 `[P1]` On-route wait is not re-armed after an app kill (iOS's own KNOWN GAP); Android resumes it

**What to build**

Persist an explicit "meter armed" flag alongside the wait state and, in `restoreLifecyclePhase`'s `.changedGone` branch, re-arm `LocationTracker.startWaiting()` when it was set instead of unconditionally disarming. Simplest route: a per-order UserDefaults key written in `startWaiting()`/`stopWaiting()`/`markPassengerOnboard()`, following the existing `tripStartedAt` / `passengerOnboardAt` pattern in TripMVVM.swift:196-214 — no CoreData model edit needed. If you prefer the store, `CalculationStore.setWaitState` (CalculationStore.swift:138-142) already holds base + segment start and the stack has lightweight migration enabled (CoreDataStack.swift:28-31).

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/services/MyTrackingService.kt:979-981 (sets `was_waiting`), :1013-1015 (clears it only on a CLEAN exit — a process kill never reaches that line), :785-805 (resume path calls `startWaitingTimer()` regardless of stage)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MVVM/TripMVVM.swift:271-283 — the `.changedGone` case whose own comment labels this a KNOWN GAP; `markPassengerOnboard(snapshotUntilGone: false)` (LocationTracker.swift:322-341) banks the open segment and sets `isWaiting = false`

> **Gotchas.** The flag must be cleared on every CLEAN stop (manual stop, the 20 km/h auto-stop, boarding, trip end) so only a kill leaves it set — that asymmetry is exactly what makes Android's `was_waiting` safe. Do not try to infer "armed" from a non-zero `waitSegmentStartedAt`: on iOS the segment stays open while armed but is closed by `stopTripTracking`, and after gap #6's fix the two are read together, so a stale segment start would double-count. Narrow blast radius (manual on-route wait + a kill in the same window), but it is silent under-billing.

**Acceptance** — Start a manual on-route wait mid-ride, kill the app from the switcher, relaunch: the wait meter is running again, the pre-kill total is intact, and the dead gap is credited once (not twice).

<a id="gap-24-5"></a>

#### 24.5 `[P1]` Kill-recovery credits the dead gap to the wait clock but not to `execution_time`

**What to build**

In `startTripTracking`, when `isReentry == false` and a recovered open wait segment contributes a gap, add the SAME gap to `trackedTimeMs` before resuming the ticker — i.e. compute `recoveredGapMs = openWaitSegmentMs(now:)` once and apply it to both counters, so the `waiting_time <= execution_time` invariant survives a kill.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/services/MyTrackingService.kt:788-803 — credits `gap` to `timeWait` and then to `timeTrack` with the comment "Keep waits ≤ execution_time (the backend proportionally shrinks otherwise): the trip clock was equally dead for the same gap"

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/LocationTracker.swift:254-271 — `trackedTimeMs = dto.trackedTimeMs` is restored from the last 10 s flush with no gap credit, while `waitedTimeMs = waitAccumulatedMs &+ openWaitSegmentMs(now:)` recovers the full wall-clock gap because it is timestamp-anchored. The mismatch flows into `execution_time` at TripMVVM.swift:694

> **Gotchas.** Android's comment is direct evidence the backend proportionally shrinks a wait that exceeds execution_time, so this is real money, not a cosmetic mismatch. Failure case is concrete: driver arrives, wait arms, app is killed for 30 min, relaunch → `waiting_time` ≈ 30 min but `execution_time` ≈ the 2 min drive to the pickup. Apply the credit ONLY on the `!isReentry` path — a minimise→resume re-entry does not restore the wait from the DTO (LocationTracker.swift:264) and would double-count. Land this together with gap #5 so a re-armed on-route wait gets the same treatment.

**Acceptance** — Arm the pickup wait, kill the app, wait 10 min, relaunch and finish. `execution_time` grew by the same ~10 min as `waiting_time`, and the submitted `waiting_time` never exceeds `execution_time`.

<a id="gap-24-6"></a>

#### 24.6 `[P2]` iOS posts the "ride started" notification BEFORE the ride has started

**What to build**

Move `postTripNoticeIfBackgrounded(titleKey: "ride_started_auto", ...)` into the success branch of the relocated auto-start from gap #3, so it fires only after the `changedGone` transition actually commits. Android posts its equivalent notice inside `Resource.Success`, additionally gated on `isTracking == true` so a Success that lands after the order was finished/cancelled can't pop a spurious notice — mirror that guard.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/services/MyTrackingService.kt:1387-1405 (`showNotice(ride_started_auto)` inside the Success branch, guarded by `isTracking.value == true`)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MVVM/HomeMVVM.swift:524 — called immediately after `shouldConfirmDeparture = true`, before any network call exists

> **Gotchas.** Downgraded from the claimed P1: `postTripNoticeIfBackgrounded` no-ops while the app is active (HomeMVVM.swift:801-802), so the false notice only reaches a driver who is backgrounded AND whose start then fails. It is a pure side effect of gap #3's design and should be fixed in the same change, not tracked as separate work.

**Acceptance** — Force the `changedGone` POST to fail while backgrounded: no "ride started" notification appears, and the auto-start re-arms. On success the notification appears exactly once.

<a id="gap-24-7"></a>

#### 24.7 `[P2]` Local wait charge is pro-rata on iOS vs whole-minute steps on Android

**What to build**

Floor each wait slice to completed whole minutes in `FareCalculator`, matching Android's integer division on SECONDS: `floor(max(0, waitedSec - freeWaitSec) / 60) * rate`, computed separately for the pickup slice and the on-way slice, then summed. Today iOS converts to fractional minutes first, so the charge creeps every second.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/maps/yandex_map/MapFragment.kt:3168-3218 (core math :3179-3216) — `((seconds - minWait) / 60 * priceWait)` with Long integer division, per slice, summed

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/FareCalculator.swift:138-147 — `waitedUntilGoneMinutes` / `waitedMinutes` are `ms / 60_000.0` and `freeWait` is fractional minutes, so `max(0, waitedUntilGoneMinutes - freeWait) * priceWait` is pro-rata

> **Gotchas.** Each Android slice is already an integer multiple of its rate, so the existing combined `waitChargeFloor` at FareCalculator.swift:159 becomes a no-op once the slices are floored — leave it in place rather than restructuring the truncation order. Bounded over-charge of up to one minute's rate per slice, and only in the taximeter/quoted branches (the `.server` branch at :188-218 deliberately does not add local wait), which is why this stays P2 — but it does make the driver's on-screen receipt drift from a whole-minute backend. `FareCalculatorTests` covers the wait math; update the expectations there in the same change.

**Acceptance** — With a 500/min pickup rate, 0 free minutes and a 90 s pickup wait, the local wait charge is 500 (not 750), and it steps only at whole-minute boundaries; the same holds independently for a 90 s on-route wait at the on-way rate.

### 25. Order cancellation by the driver — reason list, gating, penalties/escalation, confirmation, and post-cancel state cleanup

**Gaps:** 1 total — P0: 0 · P1: 0 · P2: 1

#### iOS today

Every layer of the cancel flow exists and is correct, but no UI presents it. `MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Home/CancelReasonSheet.swift` is a genuinely complete self-contained sheet (verified lines 12-58): `List(vm.cancelReasons)` with single-select checkmark, `.navigationTitle("select_reason")`, a `confirm_cancel` toolbar item `.disabled(selected == nil)`, and `.onAppear { vm.loadCancelReasons() }`; `select_reason` / `confirm_cancel` / `order_cancelled_toast` are translated in both uz and ru Localizable.strings. `TripMVVM.loadCancelReasons()` (TripMVVM.swift:840-854) hits GET `order-cancel-issue?type=1` decoding `BaseResponse<Array<OrderCancelReason>>`, byte-identical to Android's `ApiService.kt:207`; `TripMVVM.cancel(reasonId:)` (TripMVVM.swift:857-894) POSTs form-urlencoded `order/cancel` with `order_id` + `issue_id` merged with `LocationTracker.shared.orderActionLocationParams` (lat/long/optional accuracy, LocationTracker.swift:175-180), and its source comment records the same "socket route 401s the driver bearer" lesson as `MainRepositoryImpl.kt:82-84`. Its teardown is a superset of Android's (`stopTripTracking`, `GpsBatchStore.purge`, `CalculationStore.delete`, `saveTripStartedAt(nil)`, `savePassengerOnboardAt(nil)`, `WaitActivityManager.end()`, `freezeDurationCounters()`, `didCancel = true`), and the remote-cancel socket path is id-guarded at TripMVVM.swift:931 and HomeMVVM.swift:708/:723 — the HomeMVVM minimised-trip teardown has no Android analogue, so iOS is ahead of the `socket_cancel_id_guard` fix, not behind it. What is absent is only the call site: a whole-repo grep for `cancel(reasonId`, `CancelReasonSheet(`, `showCancelSheet`, `cancelSheet`, `order/cancel` returns nothing but the two definitions and a removal note, and the deletion is documented three times as a deliberate product directive (TripView.swift:338-341 "PER PRODUCT: the driver cannot self-cancel an order", :821-823 in `circleActionsRow`, :1197-1200 keeping both symbols "for an easy restore"). The only driver exit is the toolbar headphones button (TripView.swift:338-344 → `callDispatcher()` at :440-451), which dials `UserManager.shared.user?.branch?.dispatcherNumber` immediately with no confirm dialog. iOS also has no originator concept at all: `Order.from` is decoded (SharedModels.swift:375, :420) but read nowhere in the codebase, and there is no constant for 25/1/3/4/10; `OrderState` (DomainEnums.swift:26-29) does carry accepted=2/started=7/changedArrived=8/changedGone=9.

#### Android today

Android exposes driver self-cancel from two surfaces, both behind the same two-way gate. `MapFragment.kt:1204` (trip bottom sheet, `llOrderCancel`) and `ActiveMyOrdersFragment.kt:400` (`btnOrderCancel`) both branch on `order.state < ORDER_STATE_CHANGED_ARRIVED && order.from == ORDER_CREATED_CLIENT`: true → `getOrderCanselReasons()` → `showDialogOrderCancel()` (`dialog_order_cancel.xml`, `OrderCancelReasonAdapter`, submit gated on `orderCanselReasonId != null` with a "select_reason" toast otherwise); false → an inflated `DialogCallDispatcherBinding` escalation dialog showing `order.branch.dispatcherNumber` in `tvInfo` with Call/Back buttons, `setCancelable(false)` + `setCanceledOnTouchOutside(false)` and width pinned to 90% of the screen (MapFragment.kt:1207-1240, ActiveMyOrdersFragment.kt:404-424). The POST goes through `MainRepositoryImpl` to `order/cancel` (`ApiService.kt:211`), and on success `ActiveMyOrdersFragment.orderCansel()` re-fetches via `getActiveMyOrders()`. Double-submit protection is the generic 700 ms `setDebouncedClickListener` (common/ClickDebounce.kt, `DEFAULT_DEBOUNCE_MS = 700`) on both the cancel entry point and the dialog's `tvSubmit`, plus `if (dialogOrderCancel?.isShowing == true) return` and dismissing the dialog before the network call. Separately from the cancel gate, Android also dials the dispatcher immediately from the call rows (`llCallDispatcher`, MapFragment.kt:1252 / ActiveMyOrdersFragment.kt:450) and from the map's `cvDispatcher` button (MapFragment.kt:1146, which uses the driver's own `UserManager` branch rather than the order's).

<a id="gap-25-1"></a>

#### 25.1 `[P2]` Driver self-cancel is built but never presented on iOS — restore the call site plus Android's state/originator gate

**What to build**

BLOCKER FIRST: this is not an engineering oversight — the removal is a recorded product directive in three places (TripView.swift:338-341, :821-823, :1197-1200: "the driver cannot self-cancel an order; cancellation goes through the operator"). Get product to confirm the directive is being reversed before writing any code; if it stands, iOS is intentionally divergent and this item closes as won't-do. If reversed, the work is small and entirely presentation-layer — no data layer is needed, since `loadCancelReasons()`, `cancel(reasonId:)`, `CancelReasonSheet`, the localized strings, and the full teardown all already exist and already match Android's endpoint and payload exactly. (1) Add `@State private var showCancel = false` to TripView and a Cancel affordance in `circleActionsRow` (:825-864, alongside the existing Client/Map/Services circles), plus a `.sheet(isPresented: $showCancel) { CancelReasonSheet(vm: vm) { id in Task { await vm.cancel(reasonId: id) } } }` next to the existing `.sheet(isPresented: $showServices)` at :165. `didCancel` is already observed at :292 and already tears down correctly — do not add a second teardown. (2) Port the gate, not just the sheet: show the reason sheet only when `vm.currentState.rawValue < OrderState.changedArrived.rawValue && vm.order.from == 25`; otherwise show the dispatcher escalation. (3) Build the escalation dialog iOS lacks: a confirm alert/sheet displaying the number with Call / Back, mirroring `DialogCallDispatcherBinding` (non-dismissible by outside tap), rather than the current immediate `UIApplication.shared.open`. (4) Introduce the originator constant iOS has never had — add an `OrderOrigin` enum (client=25, driver/taximeter=1, manager=3, dispatcher=4, admin=10) to DomainEnums.swift rather than an inline `25` literal; `Order.from` is decoded today but read nowhere, so this is the first consumer. (5) Add a re-entry guard on the POST: `guard !isCancelling else { return }` / `isCancelling = true` with `defer` at the top of `cancel(reasonId:)`, and disable the `confirm_cancel` toolbar button once tapped instead of relying on `dismiss()` to race a second tap — TripMVVM already uses exactly this pattern via `isTransitioning` at :387/:436/:504/:619, so reuse the house style. Only reachable from the expanded trip panel: when the trip is minimised HomeMVVM owns the order (HomeMVVM.swift:723), so do not add a second cancel entry there.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/maps/yandex_map/MapFragment.kt:1204-1245 (llOrderCancel gate + dispatcher dialog), :5089-5195 (getOrderCanselReasons / showDialogOrderCancel / onOrderCancelReasonClick); app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/main/ui/my_orders/fragments/ActiveMyOrdersFragment.kt:398-425 (same gate) and :639-730 (reason dialog + orderCansel); app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/common/ClickDebounce.kt (700 ms submit debounce); app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/data/remote/ApiService.kt:207,211; app/src/main/res/layout/dialog_order_cancel.xml

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Home/TripView.swift — add the Cancel circle in `circleActionsRow` (:825-864), the sheet next to `.sheet(isPresented: $showServices)` (:165-167), the gate at the new call site, and a confirm wrapper around `callDispatcher()` (:440-451); presents the existing MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Home/CancelReasonSheet.swift (confirm toolbar item at :44-52) and calls MehrgoDriver/MVVM/TripMVVM.swift:857 `cancel(reasonId:)` (add `isCancelling` beside `isTransitioning` at :50); new `OrderOrigin` enum in MehrgoDriver/Shared/DomainEnums.swift beside `OrderState` (:26-29); `Order.from` already decoded at MehrgoDriver/Shared/Models/SharedModels.swift:375,:420. The removal rationale to reverse is at TripView.swift:1197-1200.

> **Gotchas.** The claim that iOS drivers "cannot cancel at all" overstates the delta: Android permits self-cancel ONLY for client-created orders before arrival (`state < 8 && from == 25`); for arrived, in-trip, taximeter, dispatcher, manager or admin orders Android's behaviour IS a dispatcher call, which iOS already provides. The true gap is one quadrant of the matrix, and a dispatcher workaround exists in the meantime — hence P2, not P1. Second: iOS's `callDispatcher()` reads `UserManager.shared.user?.branch?.dispatcherNumber` (the DRIVER's branch) while Android's cancel escalation reads `order.branch.dispatcherNumber` (the ORDER's branch) — these diverge on cross-branch orders and the escalation must use `vm.order.branch`, which is available on iOS (already used for `branchPolygon` at TripMVVM.swift:921). Third: do NOT add teardown at the new call site — `cancel(reasonId:)` already purges the GPS outbox, deletes the CalculationStore row and ends the Live Activity, and TripView.swift:292 already calls `homeVM.clearActiveOrder()`; duplicating any of it risks wiping a queued order's state. Fourth: `GpsBatchStore.purge` is irreversible, so the re-entry guard matters more than a normal double-tap guard — a second POST after teardown would fire against an already-purged order. Fifth: the `type=1` query on `order-cancel-issue` is the driver-side reason set; do not drop it.

**Acceptance** — Product has signed off on reversing the operator-mediated directive (or the item is closed won't-do). With a client-created order (`from == 25`) in `.accepted` or `.started`, tapping Cancel opens the reason sheet, the confirm button stays disabled until a reason is picked, and confirming POSTs `order/cancel` once with `order_id`, `issue_id`, `lat`, `long`, `accuracy` — verified against Android's request in a proxy capture. After a `.changedArrived` or `.changedGone` state, or on any order with `from` in {1,3,4,10}, the same button opens the dispatcher confirm dialog showing `order.branch.dispatcherNumber` with Call / Back and never the reason list. Rapid double-tap on confirm produces exactly one network request. After a successful cancel the trip panel dismisses, `activeOrder` is cleared, the Live Activity ends, the GPS outbox and CalculationStore row for that order are gone, and a queued order (if any) promotes correctly. Remote cancel of a different order id still does not tear down the active trip (TripMVVM.swift:931 guard unaffected).

### 26. Driver registration form (CompleteDriverInfo) — multi-field onboarding with photo upload

**Gaps:** 7 total — P0: 1 · P1: 2 · P2: 4

#### iOS today

`MehrgoDriver/View/Auth/CompleteDriverInfoView.swift` (484 lines) renders a card-sectioned form — photoBlock, personalCard, passportCard, licenseCard, vehicleCard, documentsCard, submitBlock — backed by `MVVM/CompleteDriverInfoMVVM.swift` (254 lines). Reference data is genuinely at parity and I confirmed it: `loadReferenceData()` (MVVM:151-171) fires `branch/index`, `cars/index`, `colors/index` in parallel and derives `brands`/`models` by `parentId` (MVVM:78-86), and every multipart field name in `submit()` (MVVM:210-226) matches Android's `uploadDriverInfo` call site in `CompleteDriverInfoFragment.convert()` one-for-one, including `pics[]` and `speciality_id = "1"`. The README's "/references/* await clarification" question is settled: there is no divergence — Android hardcodes both catalogs client-side in `CompleteDriverInfoVM.getGenderTypes` (Gender("6"/"8")) and `getDriverLicenseTypes` (A,B,C,D,E,BC,BCD), sends `DriverLicense.name` (the letter) at Fragment:323, and iOS copies those exact values. iOS is genuinely better in four places, all verified against source: the regex plate validator that blocks submit (`isValidUzPlate` MVVM:94-99 + `carNumberValid` gating `validationMessage`) where Android's `setupCarNumberFormat` (Fragment:243-269) only filters keystrokes and its submit gate is a bare `carNumber.isEmpty()`; `brandChanged()` clearing a stale model; a back-tap confirmation dialog (View:105-116); and correct model selection — Android's `atvModel` listener indexes the UNFILTERED `listCarModels` (Fragment:307-310) while the adapter shows the brand-filtered list, so Android saves the wrong `model_id`; iOS binds the object directly and is immune. Photo capture is `PhotosPicker`-only (View:140, :295) with raw `jpegData` and no resize — I grepped the whole repo for `UIGraphicsImageRenderer`, `preparingThumbnail`, `resize`, `scaledTo`, `downscale` and `compressionQuality`: the only image-encode sites are MVVM:187/193 here and `VerificationMVVM.swift:82`, none of which resize.

#### Android today

`presentation/auth/ui/complete_driver_info/CompleteDriverInfoFragment.kt` (1150 lines) + `CompleteDriverInfoVM.kt` (115 lines). Every photo goes through `Compressor.compress(requireContext(), file)` with no config block (Fragment:791 for the avatar, :800 for each document) — `id.zelory:compressor:3.0.1` (app/build.gradle:117), whose default constraint chain is resolution 612x816 + JPEG quality 80. All three photo slots open a Camera/Gallery bottom sheet (`createBottomSheetDialog`, Fragment:543-595) wired to `TakePicture()` and `GetContent()` contracts. `validate()` (Fragment:597-750) requires an explicit choice for every field iOS defaults: `genderTypeId == null`, `birthday.isEmpty()`, `passportGiveDate.isEmpty()`, `driverLicenseTypeId == null`, `carMade.isEmpty()` each abort with a toast. `setupPassportFormat` (Fragment:170-192) masks the passport to 2 letters + 7 digits, hard cap 9. The made-year dialog floors at `npSelectYear.minValue = 2010` (Fragment:347). Documents are two labelled tiles (licence + car) each with a preview and an X badge (`ivCloseLicence`/`ivCloseCar`, Fragment:373-388, 1054-1090), rebuilt into `listPhotoDocuments` in licence-then-car order at submit (:607-613). On success it calls `UserManager.saveUser(response.data)` (Fragment:883); since `UserManager.getBearerToken()` reads `authKey` off that stored User object (common/shared_pref/UserManager.kt:46-49) and `HeaderInterceptor` uses it for every request, Android implicitly adopts any rotated key. Known Android bug not to port: the `atvModel` index mismatch at Fragment:307-310.

<a id="gap-26-1"></a>

#### 26.1 `[P0]` Registration photos are never downscaled — multipart body can blow past the 60 s resource timeout

**What to build**

Downscale every image before it enters the multipart body, mirroring the Compressor 3.0.1 defaults Android relies on: fit inside 612x816 preserving aspect ratio, then encode JPEG at ~0.8, so each file lands in the 50-150 KB range instead of 1.5-3.5 MB. Add a UIImage resize helper (draw into a UIGraphicsImageRenderer sized to the aspect-fit target) in a shared file — VerificationMVVM.swift:82 has the identical raw-jpegData problem and should route through the same helper — then apply it to the avatar and every pics[] entry in CompleteDriverInfoMVVM.submit(). Also lower maxSelectionCount from 10 to 2, since Android only ever sends licence + car and the server contract expects that pair.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/auth/ui/complete_driver_info/CompleteDriverInfoFragment.kt:791 and :800 (`Compressor.compress(requireContext(), file)`, no config block); app/build.gradle:117 (`id.zelory:compressor:3.0.1`, defaults 612x816 / quality 80)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MVVM/CompleteDriverInfoMVVM.swift:187 (`avatar.jpegData(compressionQuality: 0.7)`) and :193 (`img.jpegData(compressionQuality: 0.6)`) — quality only, never a resize; MehrgoDriver/View/Auth/CompleteDriverInfoView.swift:295 (`maxSelectionCount: 10`) and :58-75 (both onChange handlers store the full-resolution UIImage from `loadTransferable(type: Data.self)`); MehrgoDriver/Shared/DynamicClient.swift:91 (`timeoutResource = 60`, applied at :98) and :248 (`MultipartBuilder.build` materializes the whole body as one in-memory Data before `session.upload`)

> **Gotchas.** VERIFIED: no resize helper exists anywhere in the iOS repo — grepped resize/scaledTo/UIGraphicsImageRenderer/preparingThumbnail/downscale/compressionQuality across all Swift files; the only UIGraphicsImageRenderer uses are marker rendering in YandexMap.swift. timeoutIntervalForResource is a hard cap on total task time, so on a slow uplink the upload aborts rather than degrading, and the catch at DynamicClient.swift:258 collapses it into a generic `ClientError.noConnection` — the driver sees 'no connection' after filling a 15-field form and has no way to diagnose it. Resize the decoded UIImage, do not just lower the quality constant further: quality alone still encodes 12 MP of pixels. Preserve `.imageOrientation` when redrawing or camera-captured portraits arrive rotated.

**Acceptance** — On a real device, pick a 12 MP camera photo for the avatar and both documents; the `sending (N bytes)` log line in DynamicClient.multipart reports a total body well under 500 KB, each embedded JPEG decodes to <=612x816, and the upload completes over a throttled (Network Link Conditioner '3G') connection inside the 60 s resource window.

<a id="gap-26-2"></a>

#### 26.2 `[P1]` No camera capture during registration — gallery only, so a fresh phone cannot complete sign-up

**What to build**

Give all three photo slots (avatar, licence, car) an Android-style Camera / Gallery choice instead of going straight to the photo library. The UIKit bridge already exists but is file-private to VerificationView.swift, so this is not zero-work: lift `PickerSource` and `ImagePicker` out into a shared file (e.g. MehrgoDriver/Utilities/ImagePicker.swift), drop the `private` modifiers, and drive them from CompleteDriverInfoView with the same `confirmationDialog` + `fullScreenCover(item:)` pattern. Route whatever comes back through the resize helper from gap 1.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/auth/ui/complete_driver_info/CompleteDriverInfoFragment.kt:543-595 (`createBottomSheetDialog` offering tvCamera/tvGallery for fromMyPhoto / fromPhotoLicence / fromPhotoCar), backed by `TakePicture()` contracts at :928, :976, :1024 and `GetContent()` at :938, :988, :1036

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/View/Auth/CompleteDriverInfoView.swift:140 (avatar `PhotosPicker`) and :295 (documents `PhotosPicker`). Reusable pieces to extract: MehrgoDriver/View/Main/Verification/VerificationView.swift:69-84 (confirmationDialog with `verification_photo_camera` / `verification_photo_gallery` buttons plus the `fullScreenCover(item: $pickerSource)`), :376-381 (`private enum PickerSource` with `uiKitType`), :387-419 (`private struct ImagePicker` UIViewControllerRepresentable + Coordinator).

Keep the submit gate at 'at least one of the two' to match Android.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/auth/ui/complete_driver_info/CompleteDriverInfoFragment.kt:368-388 (clPhotoLicence / ivCloseLicence / clPhotoCar / ivCloseCar), :1054-1090 (show/clear preview for each tile), :607-613 (`listPhotoDocuments` rebuilt licence-then-car at submit), :1122-1125 (`refreshSubmitEnabled` = avatar AND at least one document)

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/View/Auth/CompleteDriverInfoView.swift:290-326 `documentsCard` — one `PhotosPicker(maxSelectionCount: 10)` plus a horizontal thumbnail strip with no delete affordance; the replace-everything handler at :66-75 (`.onChange(of: documentItems)` rebuilds `vm.documents` wholesale); backing store `documents: Array<UIImage>` at MehrgoDriver/MVVM/CompleteDriverInfoMVVM.swift:22, validated only as `documents.isEmpty` at :129

> **Gotchas.** This is a prerequisite for gap 2, not independent of it: ImagePicker hands back one UIImage at a time, so per-slot storage must land before camera capture can be wired in. The wholesale rebuild at View:66-75 currently means re-picking to remove one photo discards the others — that is the user-visible symptom. Server-side ordering of pics[] is assumed from Android's licence-then-car sequence; confirm with backend before relying on index meaning, since neither client sends a per-file label.

**Acceptance** — The documents card shows two named slots; filling one and tapping its X clears only that slot and leaves the other intact; submitting with both filled sends exactly two `pics[]` parts in licence-then-car order; submitting with only one filled is allowed and sends one part.

<a id="gap-26-7"></a>

#### 26.7 `[P2]` auth_key returned by fill-data is saved to the user record but never into the Keychain

**What to build**

In submit()'s success branch, adopt the token off the returned user before saving it — `if let token = user.authKey, !token.isEmpty { UserManager.shared.setAuthToken(token) }` — exactly as the other three auth screens already do with the same `User` payload. Two lines, no behaviour change if the server returns the same key.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/auth/ui/complete_driver_info/CompleteDriverInfoFragment.kt:883 (`UserManager.saveUser(it.data?.data!!)`) combined with common/shared_pref/UserManager.kt:46-49 (`getBearerToken()` reads `authKey` off the stored User) and common/HeaderInterceptor.kt:57 — Android's bearer token IS the saved user's authKey, so saving the response implicitly adopts any rotation

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MVVM/CompleteDriverInfoMVVM.swift:236-242 — `UserManager.shared.save(user)` with no `setAuthToken` call; `UserManager.save` (MehrgoDriver/Persistence/UserManager.swift:35-41) only writes UserDefaults and never touches the Keychain, while the bearer header is read exclusively from `KeychainStore.shared.authToken` (DynamicClient.swift:244 for multipart, :294 in applyHeaders). Compare SignUpVerifyMVVM.swift:70, PasswordVerifyMVVM.swift:66 and LoginMVVM.swift:91-93, which all call `setAuthToken(user.authKey)` on the identical payload.

> **Gotchas.** The storage divergence is confirmed; the ROTATION is not — I found no evidence that `user/fill-data` issues a new key, and since the client sends its existing `auth_key` as a field the server may well echo it back, in which case this fix is a no-op. Treat it as defense-in-depth consistency across the four auth screens, not a known live bug. If rotation does happen, the failure is severe and silent: the very next authorized request 401s, DynamicClient fires `unauthorizedSubject` (:270) and RootMVVM signs the driver out immediately after a 15-field registration — which is why it is worth the two lines regardless.

**Acceptance** — After a successful fill-data submit, the Keychain token equals the `auth_key` in the response payload; the immediately following authorized request (profile/me on landing in Main) returns 200 and the driver is not bounced to login.
