### 27. Account/app gates — blocked driver, blocked competitor apps, force update, version check

**Gaps:** 8 total — P0: 0 · P1: 5 · P2: 3

#### iOS today

The gate lives in three files. `SplashMVVM.start()` (MehrgoDriver/MVVM/SplashMVVM.swift:27-39) runs `checkVersion()` (GET `mobile/version-driver`) then `loadUser()` (GET `user/me`, persisted via `UserManager.shared.save`), sleeps 1.2 s, and sets `state = .Positive`; `SplashView.onChange` (View/Root/SplashView.swift:44-50) forwards `(update, user)` on `.Positive` and `(update, nil)` on `.Negative` into `RootMVVM.continueAfterSplash` (MVVM/RootMVVM.swift:200-224), the actual ladder: forceUpdate if `versionCode > Constants.appVersion` → blockedApps if `blockedApps` non-empty → completeDriverInfo on status 7 → blockedDriver via `isBlockedDriver` → accessPermissions → main. `isBlockedDriver` (RootMVVM.swift:227-238) covers statuses 9/11/1 and `DriverStatus` (Shared/DomainEnums.swift:13-20) matches Android's `Constants.kt` numbering exactly, so status semantics are at parity; `BlockedDriverView`/`BlockedDriverMVVM` re-poll `user/me`, expose the dispatcher `tel:` call and a sign-out, and correctly treat a 401 on a pending-activation driver as "not approved yet" rather than a session loss (BlockedDriverMVVM.swift:47-57, mirrored in SplashMVVM.swift:58-66 and :89-97). `Constants.appVersion` (Shared/Constants.swift:42) auto-reads `CFBundleVersion`, strictly better than Android's hand-maintained `Constants.APP_VERSION`. Two structural differences drive the remaining gaps: iOS evaluates a freshly-fetched user exactly once at splash and never re-evaluates (Android re-runs its ladder off the cached user on every entry to Home, including a null-user → Login branch), and iOS's `UpdateApp` (Shared/Models/SharedModels.swift:1327-1340) decodes `mobile_version_code_ios`/`mobile_version_required_ios`/`blocked_apps`, none of which match the keys Android reads from the same endpoint — while iOS's own `Branch` model (SharedModels.swift:200) decodes the Android spelling `blocked_aps`, making the `_ios` key set internally inconsistent with the rest of the file.

#### Android today

Android splits the gate across two screens. `FirstActivity.getData()` (presentation/activity/splash/FirstActivity.kt:151-211) calls `firstVM.updateApp(UserManager.getToken(), localFcm)` — auth key as `token`, FCM as `device_token` (data/remote/ApiService.kt:326-331) — then branches: `data.versionCode <= APP_VERSION` continues, otherwise it swaps the splash for the update panel and hides `cvSkip` when `data.required` (FirstActivity.kt:190-197), with `cvUpdate` opening the real Play listing (:93-100). On the continue path it only shows the competitor-block dialog when the server list intersects `Constants.blockAppsInManifest` AND `PackageManager.getApplicationInfo` proves the package is actually installed (:171-189, :237-258); otherwise a 3 s timer starts `MainActivity`. A version-check `Resource.Error` never falls through — it retries every 3 s (:201-207, :226-235). The driver-status ladder is entirely separate and lives in `HomeFragment.onCreate` (presentation/main/ui/home/HomeFragment.kt:117-135), running off the CACHED user: `getUser() == null` → LoginFragment, status 7 → CompleteDriverInfo, status 9/11/1 → `notActiveUserFragment`. Because Home is recreated on each navigation to it, that ladder re-evaluates repeatedly, and `UserManager.saveUser` is called from the profile, my-payment, my-direction, not-active and order-finish flows (e.g. MapFragment.kt:2881), so a mid-session status flip is picked up on the next return to Home. `UpdateApp.kt:5-18` decodes `version`, `version_code`, `required`, `blocked_aps`, `device_token`, and the echoed `device_token` feeds `syncFcmTokenIfNeeded` (FirstActivity.kt:160-167).

<a id="gap-27-1"></a>

#### 27.1 `[P1]` Splash sign-out routes into Main instead of Login

**What to build**

Add an authentication check as the FIRST branch of `RootMVVM.continueAfterSplash`: `guard UserManager.shared.isAuthenticated else { route = .auth; return }`. This is the load-bearing fix — it mirrors Android's `UserManager.getUser() == null → LoginFragment` branch, which iOS's ladder has no equivalent of. Separately (hygiene, not the cause), gate the trailing `if errorMessage == nil { state = .Positive }` in `SplashMVVM.start()` on `state != .Negative`, since both sign-out paths set `.Negative` without ever setting `errorMessage`.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/main/ui/home/HomeFragment.kt:117-135 — the ladder's first real branch is `else if (UserManager.getUser() == null) → actionNavigationHomeToLoginFragment()`, evaluated on every entry to Home.

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MVVM/RootMVVM.swift:200-224 (`continueAfterSplash`); MehrgoDriver/MVVM/SplashMVVM.swift:31-38 (the `.Positive` overwrite), :58-66 and :89-101 (both `signOut()` + `state = .Negative` sites); MehrgoDriver/View/Root/SplashView.swift:44-50.

> **Gotchas.** VERIFIED but the claimed impact was overstated — this is NOT a permanent lockout, hence the downgrade from P0. `RootMVVM.recompute()` (RootMVVM.swift:187-195) runs from `init()` and routes a token-less user to `.auth`, so a force-quit + relaunch recovers; `MainView(onSignOut:)` (RootView.swift:94) is a second escape hatch. Damage is confined to the current session: the driver sits on a map where every request 401s, and `handleSessionRevoked` cannot rescue them because its own `guard UserManager.shared.isAuthenticated` (RootMVVM.swift:132) is already false after the splash sign-out. Also note the second `.Positive` emission never actually reaches `onReady` — the `.Negative` emission fires first, `continueAfterSplash` changes `route`, and `.id(vm.route)` (RootView.swift:41) tears `SplashView` down before the 1.2 s sleep completes. Do not expect the `state != .Negative` guard alone to fix anything.

**Acceptance** — With a revoked/cleared keychain token (the `-MapDiagFakeToken` harness at RootMVVM.swift:178 reproduces it), a cold launch lands on `LoginView`, not `MainView`, on the FIRST run — no relaunch required.

<a id="gap-27-2"></a>

#### 27.2 `[P1]` A nil user at splash silently bypasses the blocked-driver and complete-info gates

**What to build**

In `continueAfterSplash`, resolve the user as `let resolved = user ?? UserManager.shared.user` and run both status branches against `resolved`, so the gate evaluates the last-known cached status exactly like Android does. With the auth check from gap 1 in place, the only remaining nil case is an authenticated driver whose splash fetch failed, where a cached user will essentially always exist.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/main/ui/home/HomeFragment.kt:125-132 — Android reads `UserManager.getStatusValue()` from SharedPreferences (the CACHED user), so the status gate can never be skipped for want of a fresh network response.

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MVVM/RootMVVM.swift:211 and :215 (both `if let user = user` branches), fed by MehrgoDriver/View/Root/SplashView.swift:47-49 which passes `nil` on `.Negative`.

> **Gotchas.** VERIFIED, and there are two distinct nil paths, not one: (a) `loadUser`'s generic `catch` (SplashMVVM.swift:102-106) sets `.Negative` on a network error while leaving the session intact — a blocked driver on a flaky connection walks straight into `.main`; (b) a 200 whose `res.data` is nil leaves `vm.user` nil while `state` still becomes `.Positive` (SplashMVVM.swift:84-88), so the bypass happens even on the success path. Related divergence to decide on deliberately rather than silently: on a version-check failure Android never leaves the splash — `Resource.Error` schedules a 3 s retry loop (FirstActivity.kt:201-207, :226-235) — whereas iOS proceeds into the app. The cached-user fallback is the minimal fix; matching Android's blocking retry is a separate product call.

**Acceptance** — With status forced to 11 in the cached user and the device in airplane mode, a cold launch lands on `BlockedDriverView`, not `MainView`. Same result when `user/me` returns 200 with a null `data`.

<a id="gap-27-3"></a>

#### 27.3 `[P1]` Force-update "Later" and blocked-app "Continue" jump straight to .main, skipping every downstream gate

**What to build**

Extract the tail of `continueAfterSplash` (status 7 → completeDriverInfo, `isBlockedDriver` → blockedDriver, permissions → accessPermissions, else main) into `func routeAfterGates(user: User?)`, have `continueAfterSplash` call it, and point RootView's `onSkip` / `onContinue` at `vm.routeAfterGates(user: UserManager.shared.user)`. This is the same refactor gap 2 needs — land them together and let `routeAfterGates` own the `user ?? UserManager.shared.user` fallback.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/activity/splash/FirstActivity.kt:87-91 — the Skip button starts `MainActivity`, whose `HomeFragment.onCreate` (HomeFragment.kt:117-135) then re-runs the full ladder. On Android the escape hatch cannot bypass the status gates because the gates live downstream of it.

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/View/Root/RootView.swift:96 (`ForceUpdateView(required:onSkip: { vm.route = .main })`) and :98 (`BlockedAppView(onContinue: { vm.route = .main })`).

> **Gotchas.** VERIFIED as written, but exposure is currently gated behind gap 5: if the backend does not emit `mobile_version_code_ios`, `versionCode` decodes nil and `.forceUpdate` is unreachable — so this bug goes live the moment gap 5 is fixed. Sequence accordingly. Also, the "later" button is unconditionally visible today because `Constants.isAppStoreURLPlaceholder` is true (ForceUpdateView.swift:40), so `required == true` does not currently hide it and every driver can take this path. If gap 6 is actioned, the `onContinue` half disappears with `BlockedAppView`.

**Acceptance** — With a cached status of 11 and a stubbed `versionCode` above `Constants.appVersion`, tapping "later" on the update screen lands on `BlockedDriverView`, not the map.

<a id="gap-27-4"></a>

#### 27.4 `[P1]` Required updates are not enforceable — the App Store URL is still a placeholder

**What to build**

Replace `Constants.appStoreURL` with the real listing ID once Apple issues one, then delete `isAppStoreURLPlaceholder` and both of its consumers so `required == true` genuinely renders an Update-only screen with no "later" button.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/activity/splash/FirstActivity.kt:93-100 (`cvUpdate` opens `https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=${Constants.APPLICATION_ID}`) and :194-196 (`if (data.required) cvSkip.visibility = View.GONE`) — a required update on Android is genuinely unskippable and the store link works.

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/Constants.swift:122-130 (`appStoreURL`, `isAppStoreURLPlaceholder`), consumed at MehrgoDriver/View/Root/ForceUpdateView.swift:33-43.

> **Gotchas.** VERIFIED. The placeholder escape is deliberate and correct pre-launch (it avoids a dead apps.apple.com link), so this is a launch-checklist item rather than a code defect — but it must not be forgotten: until it lands there is NO way to force an iOS driver off a bad build, while Android can. `interactiveDismissDisabled(required)` (ForceUpdateView.swift:47) is not sufficient on its own; the visible "later" button is the hole.

**Acceptance** — With a real App ID configured and `required = true` stubbed, the update screen shows an Update button that opens the correct listing and offers no way past it.

<a id="gap-27-5"></a>

#### 27.5 `[P1]` version-driver response keys diverge from Android — the iOS version gate may never fire

**What to build**

Capture the real `mobile/version-driver` response for an iOS client (a proxy trace or one line from the backend team settles it). If the server only emits Android's keys, give `UpdateApp` a custom `init(from:)` that decodes both key sets — try `mobile_version_code_ios`, fall back to `version_code`; try `mobile_version_required_ios`, fall back to `required`; try `blocked_apps`, fall back to `blocked_aps` — the same dual-key pattern `Branch` and `UserStatus` already use in this file. Add a decode-level log so future key drift is visible instead of silent.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/domain/model/UpdateApp.kt:5-18 — Android reads `version`, `@SerializedName("version_code")`, `required`, `@SerializedName("blocked_aps")`, `@SerializedName("device_token")` from the SAME endpoint (data/remote/ApiService.kt:326-331).

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/Models/SharedModels.swift:1327-1340 (`UpdateApp` + its `CodingKeys`), consumed at MehrgoDriver/MVVM/RootMVVM.swift:201-210.

> **Gotchas.** UNREFUTED and now supported by concrete in-repo evidence: iOS's own `Branch` model decodes Android's misspelled `blocked_aps` (SharedModels.swift:182, :200, :215) while `UpdateApp` decodes `blocked_apps` (:1338). One of the two is wrong, and the rest of the file shows a careful pattern of matching Android's literal keys (`dispetcher_number`, `validity_period`, `descriptions`) — the `_ios`-suffixed set is the odd one out. The failure mode is silent in both directions: every field is Optional, so a total key miss decodes to all-nil and `continueAfterSplash` falls through to `.main` with no error, which is exactly what a healthy launch looks like. Do not conclude "it works" from the app behaving normally — only a captured response body proves it.

**Acceptance** — A captured `mobile/version-driver` response body is pasted into the ticket, and a unit test decodes that exact JSON into an `UpdateApp` with a non-nil `versionCode`.

<a id="gap-27-6"></a>

#### 27.6 `[P2]` Remove the dead .blockedApps route rather than porting Android's competitor blocking

**What to build**

Delete `RootRoute.blockedApps` (RootMVVM.swift:25), the branch at RootMVVM.swift:207-210, the RootView case, and `View/Root/BlockedAppView.swift`. Do NOT port the Android feature. iOS has no supported API to enumerate installed third-party apps — `LSApplicationWorkspace` is private and `canOpenURL` needs a per-scheme `LSApplicationQueriesSchemes` allowlist Apple will not approve for competitor detection — and App Review rejects apps that gate on a competitor's presence. Record this as a deliberate platform-constraint decision so nobody re-adds it during the next parity pass.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/activity/splash/FirstActivity.kt:171-189 and :237-258 (`canInstallCheckingFromManifest`), which cross-references the server list against `Constants.blockAppsInManifest` and `PackageManager.getApplicationInfo`.

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/View/Root/BlockedAppView.swift; MehrgoDriver/MVVM/RootMVVM.swift:25 and :207-210; MehrgoDriver/View/Root/RootView.swift:97-98.

> **Gotchas.** The route is inert today, not dangerous: `blockedApps` decodes from `blocked_apps` while the server sends `blocked_aps` (gap 5), so it is always nil. But if gap 5 is fixed by adding a `blocked_aps` fallback, this branch WAKES UP and misfires for every driver — iOS routes to the blocked screen on any non-empty string, whereas Android only blocks when one of those packages is actually INSTALLED. That makes deletion a prerequisite of gap 5's fallback work, not optional cleanup: land it before or with gap 5. Priority stays P2 because nothing is broken in the shipped build.

**Acceptance** — `grep -rn "blockedApps" MehrgoDriver/` returns only the `Branch` model's field; the project builds and no route can present a blocked-app screen.

<a id="gap-27-7"></a>

#### 27.7 `[P2]` No mid-session re-gate: a driver deactivated while the app is open keeps working until relaunch

**What to build**

Add a status observer in `RootMVVM.init()` — subscribe to `UserManager.shared.$user`, and when `route == .main` and the new status is 9/11/1, route to `.blockedDriver` (and to `.completeDriverInfo` on 7). Hang it off `UserManager` rather than off `validateSessionIfDue` so it covers every refresh path at once. Optionally have `validateSessionIfDue` decode and `save` its response too, so the probe contributes to the same signal instead of being pure liveness.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/main/ui/home/HomeFragment.kt:131-133 — Android re-runs `getStatusValue()` against the freshly cached user on every creation of the Home destination and navigates to `notActiveUserFragment`, so a status flip is caught on the next return to Home rather than at next launch.

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MVVM/RootMVVM.swift:53-58 (`init`, where the subscription belongs) and :227-238 (`isBlockedDriver`); the refresh sites that already persist a fresh user are MehrgoDriver/MehrgoDriverApp.swift:152-163, MehrgoDriver/MVVM/MapMVVM.swift:110-127, MehrgoDriver/MVVM/HomeMVVM.swift:607-621, MehrgoDriver/MVVM/ProfileMVVM.swift:37-45.

> **Gotchas.** The original claim's premise is REFUTED — the problem is not that the probe discards its response. iOS already fetches AND persists `user/me` on app foreground, on map appear, after a settlement, and on the profile screen, so `UserManager.shared.user.status` is genuinely fresh mid-session. The single missing piece is that nothing re-evaluates the ROUTE against it. Patching `validateSessionIfDue` alone would be a 30-second-gap, `.main`-only fix to a problem four other call sites also feed. Two hazards for the observer: it must not fire mid-trip in a way that yanks the driver out of an active order (check active-order state before routing — TripMVVM.swift:754 saves a user right at finish time), and it must not fight `didUnblockDriver()` when `BlockedDriverMVVM.refresh()` saves a now-active user.

**Acceptance** — With the app open on the map, flipping the driver to status 11 server-side and backgrounding/foregrounding the app lands on `BlockedDriverView` without a relaunch, and does not trigger during a normal order finish.

<a id="gap-27-8"></a>

#### 27.8 `[P2]` version-driver `token` query carries the FCM token instead of the auth key

**What to build**

Send the auth key as `token` and keep the FCM token as `device_token`, matching Android exactly — or get written confirmation from the backend that `token` on this endpoint means the push token. While in here, consider consuming the server's echoed `device_token` the way Android does: `UpdateApp` has no `deviceToken` field on iOS, so the defensive resync half of the contract is missing.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/presentation/activity/splash/FirstActivity.kt:152-155 calls `firstVM.updateApp(UserManager.getToken(), localFcm)`; `UserManager.getToken()` (common/shared_pref/UserManager.kt:40-44) returns `user.authKey`. Signature is `updateApp(@Query("token") token, @Query("device_token") deviceToken)` (data/remote/ApiService.kt:326-331), and the echoed value feeds `syncFcmTokenIfNeeded(data.deviceToken)` at FirstActivity.kt:160-167.

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/MVVM/SplashMVVM.swift:44-47 — both `token` and `device_token` are set to `FcmTokenManager.shared.token`.

> **Gotchas.** VERIFIED as a literal divergence, but impact is likely low, hence P2: `DynamicClient` sends the bearer header on this call anyway, and iOS registers its push token separately via `POST device-token/register` (Shared/PushNotificationCenter.swift:102), so the driver→token association does not depend solely on this query. The realistic failure is server-side: if the backend keys off `token` to attach the device token, iOS registrations arriving through this endpoint are attributed to nothing. Confirm before changing — if the backend has since redefined `token` as the push token for both platforms, changing iOS would introduce the bug rather than fix it.

**Acceptance** — A captured iOS `mobile/version-driver` request shows `token=<authKey>` and `device_token=<fcm>`, and a push sent to that driver arrives on the device.

### 28. Route service (second backend: OSRM route server) — endpoints, request/response shape, caching, reroute trigger policy, country base URLs

**Gaps:** 1 total — P0: 0 · P1: 1 · P2: 0

#### iOS today

iOS talks to the OSRM route server through exactly one type, `RouteService` (MehrgoDriver/Shared/RouteService.swift), a `@MainActor` singleton with `route(from:to:steps:)` (path `route/v1/driving/{lon},{lat};{lon},{lat}`, appending `steps=true` only when asked), `distance(from:to:)` → `routes?.first?.distance`, and `routeInfo(from:to:)` → `(distance, duration)`. Requests go through `DynamicClient.request(base: .route, …)`; `ApiBase.route` resolves to `Constants.routeBaseURL = "https://route.teamwork.uz/"` (Constants.swift:27). Verified only TWO live call sites, both `distance(...)` with `steps: false`: `OrderOfferMVVM.resolvePickupDistanceIfNeeded()` (OrderOfferMVVM.swift:139) and `PickupDistanceStore.ensure(_:driver:)` (PickupDistanceStore.swift:185) — `routeInfo` and the `steps: true` branch are genuinely unreferenced. `PickupDistanceStore` is confirmed ahead of Android: per-order `cache`, Haversine seed so a row never reads 0.00 km, `movementEpsilonMeters = 50` on the `$lastLocation` publisher, `staleThresholdMeters = 300` re-fetch anchor held separately from the display snapshot, per-order `inflight` coalescing, and eviction of both `cache` and `lastFetchAnchor` for orders that left the pool (`prefetch`, :117-131); it is driven only from `RecommendedOrdersView` (:81-83), i.e. while the driver is online browsing the broadcast pool. Confirmed iOS does NOT use the route server for map geometry at all — the booked route, the deadhead route and every reroute come from Yandex `YMKDrivingRouter` (YandexMap.swift:546 in `PickupRouteLayer`, :1573 reroute, :1939 main route) as a single multi-waypoint `requestRoutes`. iOS is single-brand by construction (project.yml: one target, bundle id `uz.teamwork.mehrgodriver`, `schemes: {}`, hardcoded keychain-access-group), so `routeBaseURL` is UZ-only exactly like `BASE_URL`, `phoneNumberSize: 13`, `mapkitKey` and the merchant ids — there is no country/brand enum to drift from.

#### Android today

Android reaches the same OSRM host three separate ways. The Hilt-provided route Retrofit (`NetworkModule.kt:74-80`, `@Named("retrofit_route")`, base `Constants.BASE_URL_ROUTE`) shares the SAME `okHttpClient` as the main API, so it does attach `Authorization` via `HeaderInterceptor` — it feeds `RouteRepositoryImpl` → `RouteUC`/`GetDistanceUC`, consumed by `MapViewModel.kt:38`, `AutoOfferService.kt:59` and `RouteNavControllerService.kt:55`. The two surfaces analogous to the iOS call sites instead use bare, token-free Retrofit instances built inline: `RouteDistanceFetcher.kt:22-27` (offer sheet, `OrderOfferBottomSheet.kt:200`) and `OrderAdapter.kt:273-281` (order list rows), neither of which installs any interceptor. Crucially, Android has no centralized 401 handler: forced sign-out is an `it.message == "401"` string sentinel emitted by only three main-backend use cases (`UserUC.kt:33-34`, `StartWorkUC.kt:34-35`, `OrderAddressUC.kt:35-36`) and consumed at `MapFragment.kt:1605`/`:1819` and `HomeFragment.kt:315` — both `RouteUC` and `GetDistanceUC` swallow `HttpException` into `Helper.getUnexpectedError()` and can never emit it, so a route-server 401 cannot log an Android driver out. Country presets live as a commented block at `Constants.kt:4-6` (`route.teamwork.uz` UZ active, `kgroute.` / `kzroute.` commented), switched by hand alongside `BASE_URL` and `PHONE_NUMBER_SIZE`. Android has no equivalent of `PickupDistanceStore` — no per-order cache, no Haversine seed, no movement epsilon, no in-flight coalescing.

<a id="gap-28-1"></a>

#### 28.1 `[P1]` Route-server 401 force-signs-out the driver and kills fare tracking; Android is structurally immune

**What to build**

Scope the 401 session-revocation signal to the MAIN backend. The load-bearing change is one line in `DynamicClient.request(...)`: guard the revocation send on the base, i.e. `if http.statusCode == 401 { if base == .main { Self.unauthorizedSubject.send(path) }; throw ClientError.unauthorized }` — a non-2xx from `.route` must throw and nothing more. Do NOT rely on filtering `route/v1/...` inside `RootMVVM.authFlow401Paths`: that set is an exact-string `Set<String>` and route paths embed coordinates, so no static entry can match; the filter belongs at the base level. SECONDARY, and explicitly NOT parity items (verified Android does the same or worse, so treat as optional hardening rather than gap closure): (a) passing `base` into `applyHeaders(to:formUrlEncoded:)` so `Authorization` is omitted for `.route` — Android's Hilt route Retrofit shares the main OkHttpClient and sends the token too (NetworkModule.kt:74-80); (b) skipping `queueError` for `.route` — the route URL carries the driver's lon/lat in the PATH and `redactingLocationParams` only redacts QUERY items named lat/long/accuracy, but Android's `HeaderInterceptor` persists the full URL plus the raw Authorization header for every non-2xx with no redaction at all, so iOS is already ahead here.

**Android reference** — app/src/main/java/uz/teamwork/mehrgodriver/domain/use_case/route/RouteUC.kt:24-28 and GetDistanceUC.kt:24-28 (both catch HttpException → Helper.getUnexpectedError(), never emit the "401" sentinel); the sentinel is emitted only by UserUC.kt:33-34, StartWorkUC.kt:34-35, OrderAddressUC.kt:35-36 and consumed at MapFragment.kt:1605, MapFragment.kt:1819, HomeFragment.kt:315. Token-free route clients: RouteDistanceFetcher.kt:22-27, OrderAdapter.kt:273-281.

**iOS target** — MehrgoDriver/Shared/DynamicClient.swift — the non-2xx block at :188-204, specifically the 401 branch at :198-201 (`Self.unauthorizedSubject.send(path)`); optional secondaries at `applyHeaders` :287-297 and `queueError` :356-370 / `redactingLocationParams` :344-354. Note the multipart path at :269-272 has the identical send but is hardcoded `base: .main` (:236), so it needs no change. Behaviour consumer: MehrgoDriver/MVVM/RootMVVM.swift — `handleSessionRevoked` :125-144 (whitelist `authFlow401Paths` :119-122).

> **Gotchas.** The failure is completely silent at the call site and that is what makes it dangerous: `unauthorizedSubject.send(path)` fires synchronously at DynamicClient.swift:199 BEFORE the throw, so `RouteService.distance`'s `catch { return nil }` (RouteService.swift:45-48) swallows the error while the sign-out has already been dispatched — the driver just gets logged out with no failing UI action to explain it. `handleSessionRevoked` unconditionally calls `LocationTracker.shared.stopTripTracking()` then `didSignOut()`, so if an order is active the GPS batch uploader is halted and metering stops — that is the money impact. Correct the original report's framing on two points: (1) the trigger is not strictly 'mid-trip' — both live call sites fire while the driver is online with the broadcast pool or an offer sheet on screen (`RecommendedOrdersView.swift:81-83`), and `PickupDistanceStore` re-fires every 300 m of movement, so exposure is continuous across a shift rather than tied to a trip; (2) the 'stop sending the auth header' and 'don't queue the GPS-bearing URL' sub-points are NOT gaps versus Android and should not be sold as parity work. Honest caveat for planning: this is a latent landmine, not a reproducible bug — a stock OSRM server has no auth layer and will not emit 401 today. Its P1 standing rests on blast radius (any nginx/WAF/gateway policy change in front of route.teamwork.uz, or a misproxy onto the main teamwork.uz API which DOES 401, would sign out the entire iOS fleet and silently stop their meters) against a one-line fix — not on current frequency. Do not let a reviewer 'verify' this by watching production; verify it by injection.

**Acceptance** — Point `Constants.routeBaseURL` at a stub returning HTTP 401 (or add a debug launch-argument that forces the route base to a 401 host). With a valid session and the broadcast pool on screen: `PickupDistanceStore` rows fall back to the Haversine value, no session-revoked alert appears, `UserManager.shared.isAuthenticated` stays true, and `LocationTracker` trip tracking keeps running with an active order. Confirm via os_log that `RootMVVM: session revoked` is NOT emitted for any `route/v1/driving/...` path. Regression check that real revocation still works: the existing `-MapDiagAutoMain -MapDiagFakeToken` harness (RootMVVM.swift:173-181) must still force sign-out on a main-backend 401, and a 401 on `user/login` must still be swallowed by LoginMVVM rather than triggering revocation.
